**AD-1001a shipped (2026-06-14) — Ship's Locker global capabilities catalog API (Agent Customizations epic #944, #946).** The global counterpart to the per-agent Service hub: `GET /api/tools/catalog` (added to the AD-894 ship-wide catalog router) aggregates all four capability axes — tools (with built_in/mcp/extension origin), skills, mesh intents (reuses AD-1000a `_mesh_intents`), MCP servers — plus `held_by` (which crew hold each tool/skill by explicit grant; one O(agents) grant-store walk; role/dept defaults not enumerated). Mesh intents ship-served → no held_by. Read-only; honest-degrade per axis. Tests: ad1001 (5, BF-287 real stores). UI panel (AD-1001b) binds next; #946 stays open. PUSHED.

**AD-1000c shipped (2026-06-14) — per-agent Service Configuration tab (Agent Customizations epic #944, #945).** The "button on the profile card": a dedicated crew-only **Service** tab in `AgentProfilePanel` (new `ProfileServiceTab.tsx`) hosting the three-axis CapabilityPanel (Tools/Skills/Capabilities) under a SERVICE CONFIGURATION header. Removed both duplicated CAPABILITIES blocks from `ProfileInfoTab` (fixes a copy-paste dup-render bug + declutters Profile). Crew-only via `visibleTabs`; `deps` passthrough for hermetic tests; mirrors the personnel ServiceRecord (one surface, two homes). Frontend-only, additive. Tests: ProfileServiceTab (3); profile+panel suites 943 passed; build clean. PUSHED. #945 closed.

**AD-1004 shipped (2026-06-13) — lifecycle-hook bus (Agent Customizations epic #944; prereq for Capability Packs #948).** Settled the per-agent write-intent gating design first: per-agent **authorization** at *origination* (default-OFF grant) layered with the **consensus** per-call safety gate — two orthogonal layers (VS Code's enablement + approval), enforced via a `PreDispatch` hook (never the serving pool — `IntentMessage` has no requester field; authz belongs where agent identity is known). Built the bus that enforcement plugs into. `hooks/bus.py` `HookBus`: cross-tool `HookEvent` set (SessionStart/PreToolUse/PostToolUse/PreDispatch/… for Capability-Pack portability), `register`/`unregister`/`fire`; gate events (PreToolUse/PreDispatch) aggregate verdicts most-restrictive-wins (deny>ask>allow, VS Code precedence); observational events run for side effects. Honest-degrade: a raising handler contributes no verdict (consensus is the real gate). `HooksConfig(enabled=False)` wired into `SystemConfig`. **Mechanism only** — NOT wired into the live dispatch path (firing with no handlers = no-op; inert until the follow-up registers grant+consensus handlers and fires from the IntentBus/agentic loop). Tests: ad1004 (15); config regression 580 passed; smoke clean. PUSHED.

**AD-1000a + AD-1000b shipped (2026-06-13) — capability provenance + mesh-intent visibility (Agent Customizations epic #944, #945).** Captain greenlit building the VS Code / Copilot / Claude harness-parity epic; this is the safe additive first slice both the per-agent Service Config hub (AD-1000) and the global Ship's Locker (AD-1001) need. **AD-1000a** (`routers/agents.py`): `_tool_origin` classifies tools by source — `built_in`/`mcp`/`extension` (VS Code taxonomy, orthogonal to the functional `ToolType` AD-422) — added as `origin` on each tool in `GET /api/agent/{id}/capabilities`; `_mesh_intents` surfaces the live mesh-intent set (`run_python`, `http_fetch`…) as a third `mesh_intents` array (description/usage_hint/requires_consensus/tier/origin/reachable). Pool-served → ship-wide reachable → **read-only visibility, not per-agent gated** (per-agent write-intent gating = separate epic design). **AD-1000b** (`CapabilityPanel.tsx`): read-only CAPABILITIES section (no toggle) + consensus badge on write intents + origin labels on tools — the panel now shows all three axes (Tools · Skills · Capabilities). Purely additive + read-only; gating model untouched. Tests: ad1000a (8, BF-287 real descriptors+registry) + CapabilityPanel (+5); 193 profile UI + build clean; backend regression 279 passed/1 skip. PUSHED.

**BF-628 (2026-06-13) — workspace root rebases onto the platform data dir; code execution ENABLED.** Enabling execution surfaced that `WorkspaceManager` resolved the relative default `data/execution/workspaces` against the process **cwd**, not `%LOCALAPPDATA%/ProbOS/data` (the split-brain `_platform_data_dir` prevents). Fix: `_resolve_workspace_root` rebases relative roots onto the platform data dir (leading `data/` stripped, mirrors AD-720 attachments); platform-dir logic **inlined** in `execution/workspace.py` (importing `probos.runtime` fails without optional `keyring`). Resolved root: `C:\Users\<user>\AppData\Local\ProbOS\data\execution\workspaces\<agent>\`. Near-miss avoided: `data/workspaces` is already the TaskSession dir (AD-815a) — execution stays under `data/execution/`. Also set `execution.enabled: true` + `allow_package_install: true` in system.yaml (Captain go-ahead; restart required). +2 tests (21 total); gate 672 passed/1 skipped. PUSHED.

**AD-997 + AD-998 + AD-999 shipped (2026-06-13) — per-agent working folders for code execution (#936 epic).** Captain's follow-ups to AD-992-996: per-agent vs shared folders, configurable root, HXI config, profile-card visibility. Tier 1 shipped *ephemeral-reaped*; viewing implies *persistence* + per-owner keying. Constraint: `IntentMessage` has no requester field + `CodeRunnerAgent` is one core agent → owner carried in `params`, defaulting to the executor's key (forward-compatible). **AD-997** `execution/workspace.py` `WorkspaceManager` (per-owner folder: sanitize/traversal-safe, `key_for_agent` callsign→type→id, `list_files` treats `.venv` as one opaque entry, honest-degrade) + `ExecutionConfig.persistent_workspaces=True` + `workspace_root`; `CodeRunnerAgent` writes to the owner's persistent folder (passed as workdir, not reaped), **reuses** `<ws>/.venv` across runs (idempotent pip). **AD-998** `GET /api/agent/{id}/workspace` → `{enabled,persistent,root,path,owner,exists,files[],total_bytes}`, keyed by the same `key_for_agent`. **AD-999** Core "Code Execution" Settings section (configurable `workspace_root` + the gates) + `WorkspaceFolder.tsx` in the profile Work tab (path + file list; disabled/ephemeral/empty honest states). Default-OFF preserved (now flippable from HXI). Tests: ad997 (19) + ad998 (6) + section_registry (5) + WorkspaceFolder (9); 188 profile UI tests + build clean; backend regression green. PUSHED.

**BF-627 (2026-06-13) — code execution must not be advertised to the planner when disabled.** Found while answering "how do agents know they can run Python?". Two defects in today's AD-994 (1fbedde9): (1) `runtime.py` registered the `code_runner` template unconditionally, so `_collect_intent_descriptors()` fed `run_python`/`install_package` into the decomposer prompt even with `execution.enabled=False` — advertising a capability with no pool to serve it. (2) `run_python` had a `usage_hint`, which placed a consensus WRITE in the read-only AD-983a `[MESH …]` affordance block. Fix: gate `register_template("code_runner", …)` on `execution.enabled` (mirrors the pool gate); drop the `usage_hint` (planner learns it from `description`, routes via consensus). Regression blast **825 passed / 7 skipped**. PUSHED.

**AD-992 + AD-993 + AD-994 shipped (+ AD-995/996 stubs) (2026-06-13) — governed code execution: tiered isolation for safe Python (Copilot/Claude Code parity) (#936 epic ← #937/#938/#939/#940).** The Captain asked for the Copilot/Claude Code capability — *create + run Python scripts, install libraries as needed* — and named the isolation references (Copilot working folder, Cowork containers, Microsoft MXC). ProbOS had **no general-purpose Python path**: capability evolution is a *design* problem (self_mod designs persistent agents), not a *runtime-execution* one; `CodeValidator` bans `subprocess`/`eval`/`exec` for designed code; `ShellCommandAgent` runs shell not Python. So ephemeral one-off computation had no home. **Stance — governed ephemeral execution, not an ungoverned scratchpad:** every run is **consensus-gated + episodically logged + default-OFF + tier-isolated** (sandbox strength is a config dial behind one `IsolationBackend` protocol). **Tiers:** 1 = subprocess/working-folder (process isolation + confinement-by-convention, NOT kernel containment); 2 = OS-native kernel-enforced (bubblewrap/Seatbelt/AppContainer/MXC); 3 = container/microVM (+ reproducibility + multi-tenant). Escalation can skip Tier 2 → Tier 3 (independent backends). **Built (Tier 1):** **AD-993** `execution/isolation.py` — `IsolationBackend(Protocol)` + `SubprocessSandbox` (ephemeral scratch reaped in `finally`, `Popen` in executor like ShellCommandAgent, **arg-list never shell** → no injection, `[python,-I,-B]`, scrubbed env + blackhole proxy when network-off, POSIX RLIMIT + process-group kill / Windows new-group, output-capped, honest-degrade; docstring states Tier-1 ≠ kernel containment). **AD-994** `agents/code_runner.py` `CodeRunnerAgent` — consensus-gated `run_python(code, packages?, timeout?)` (throwaway per-task venv when packages requested) + `install_package(packages)`; `ExecutionConfig` (**default OFF**: `enabled=False`, `default_tier=1`, `allow_package_install=False`) wired into `SystemConfig`; gated pool (spawned only when enabled) + core membership; NOT in `_MESH_READ_INTENT_POOLS` (consensus/WRITE); defense in depth (`_clean_packages` drops `-`-tokens → no pip-flag injection, timeout clamp ≤300s). **Not enabled in system.yaml.** **Stubbed:** **AD-995** Tier-2 — *waiting for MXC* (MIT, 0.7.0-alpha, README: "no MXC profiles should be treated as security boundaries currently"; absorb the pattern, never link the TS SDK). **AD-996** Tier-3 — deferred; **"do we need Tier 3 once we have Tier 2?"** → Tier 2 suffices for single-operator/own-machine; Tier 3 only for hostile/multi-tenant or reproducible/persistent envs (hosted/commercial), buildable directly after Tier 1. Tests: `test_ad993_isolation.py` (16) + `test_ad994_code_runner.py` (14, BF-287 real `ExecutionConfig`, real offline venv+pip degrade) — **30 passed / 1 skipped**; regression blast **601 passed / 8 skipped**; default-OFF verified. Design doc `prompts/ad-992-996-tiered-execution.md`; commercial note `governed-code-execution-2026-06-13.md`. Tier 1 PUSHED; epic #936 + stubs #939/#940 open.

**AD-989 + AD-990 + AD-991 shipped (2026-06-13) — ripgrep-absorption wave: lexical content grep for the mesh (#933, #934, #935).** The Captain noted Claude Code uses lexical project search (ripgrep) not RAG for code, and asked what to absorb. Code is an exact-token domain — grep beats embeddings (exact, fresh, self-explaining: `path:line:text` *is* the evidence, serving AD-988 transparency). ProbOS leaned lexical already (AD-979c, BF-625) but had **no content grep in the mesh** (FileSearchAgent = filename-glob only, walking `.venv`; CodebaseIndex = AST word-scoring; `dm_targeted_lookup` detects "grep the codebase" but had nothing to route to). **License:** ripgrep is dual MIT/Unlicense → pattern-absorbed, not vendored (it's Rust); zero new hard deps (pure Python; `rg` an optional operator binary under gitignored `/tools/`, resolved like Piper/Rhubarb). **Built (leaf→headline, all read-only/no-consensus):** **AD-991** `substrate/safe_regex.py` — `safe_compile` rejects over-long + catastrophic-backtracking patterns (ReDoS guard; Python `re` backtracks, supplied patterns are a DoS vector). **AD-990** `substrate/file_walk.py` — `IgnoreSpec` (common-subset gitignore + `!` negation) + `iter_files` (prunes `.venv`/`node_modules`/`__pycache__`/hidden/binary, bounded); FileSearchAgent walks ignore-aware by default (`include_ignored=True` escape hatch — fixes the `.venv` bug). **AD-989** `agents/code_search.py` `CodeSearchAgent` — `search_content` → `{path,line,text}`; prefers `rg` (subprocess, no shell), falls back to bounded ReDoS-guarded Python scan; wired into the pool topology + `_MESH_READ_INTENT_POOLS` (so `[MESH search_content]` surfaces crew-wide). Tests: `test_ad991_safe_regex.py` (16), `test_ad990_file_walk.py` (13), `test_ad989_code_search.py` (13, Python engine forced via `which→None`); AD-869 allowlist updated (7th read intent); wiring blast **97 passed**, descriptor blast **474 passed / 6 skipped**. PUSHED. Commercial note: `lexical-first-retrieval-2026-06-13.md` (route retrieval by domain — grep for code, semantics for recall, hybrid RRF as the bridge). Forward: `re2`/`pathspec`, a `[MESH search_content]` Architect/Builder wiring.

**AD-986a + AD-987 shipped (2026-06-13) — group-memory enrichment: speaker attribution + reflection fidelity + visual↔conversational binding (#929, #931; the AD-986 epic).** The Counselor's live recall feedback surfaced two code-grounded gaps at the AD-933a group-episode write, both verified before building. **AD-986a (#929):** the round-0 Captain trigger was stored unlabeled and the agent's own reply capped at 240 chars — "who said what" was weak and a substantive reply's payload was never indexed (the `AnchorFrame.trigger_agent` slot existed but was unused). **AD-987 (#931):** the conversation episode and the frame the agent *saw* were two disconnected streams (the `Episode` has no visual field; the frame lives in a TTL-reaped vision ring). **Fix (both default-OFF → byte-identical until enabled):** AD-986a adds `MemoryConfig.group_episode_enrichment_enabled` + `group_reflection_max_chars` (600) and a `trigger_speaker` param to `_fan_one_round` (round 0 = "Captain", cascades self-label); when on it prefixes the trigger, sets `AnchorFrame.trigger_agent`, and indexes up to the larger cap. AD-987 adds `episode_visual_binding_enabled` + two `AnchorFrame` fields (`visual_attachment_ref`/`visual_description`, auto-serialized via `asdict`, backward-compatible); when on, the write binds the replying agent's current `get_or_create_working_memory(agent_id).latest()` frame (content-addressable, survives ring reap; Tier-2), and `_prepare_document` indexes `[saw: …]` so "what was I seeing when X" is recall-searchable (the integrated recall she asked for). Both edit the same write site + frozen `AnchorFrame`, so they shipped together; the eviction-retention half of #929 is deferred (substrate-sensitive). Tests: `test_ad986a_987_group_memory_enrichment.py` (11, BF-287 real store + bus + vision rings + recording stub) — defaults-off, disabled→byte-identical, Captain-label + trigger_agent, raised-cap payload, visual capture, empty-ring honest-degrade, binding-off ignores frame, `_prepare_document` `[saw:]`, AnchorFrame round-trip + back-compat; fan-out regression **65 passed**, episodic/anchor regression **108 passed**. Both flags enabled in `config/system.yaml`. PUSHED. Forward: AD-988 (#932, retrieval-reason transparency); deferred #929 eviction half.

**BF-626 shipped (2026-06-12) — avatar-telemetry WebSocket close crashed on a boot-window peer-disconnect race.** The Captain saw repeated `ERROR: Exception in ASGI application` with `AttributeError: 'WebSocketProtocol' object has no attribute 'transfer_data_task'` from `agents.py:1668` — `websocket.close(reason="no_crew_agents")` in `fleet_avatar_telemetry_stream`. The telemetry handlers `accept()` then `close()` on a gate; `no_crew_agents` fires during the **boot window** (the HXI opens the socket before crew agents register, then reconnects), and when the peer has already vanished the `websockets` legacy `close()` awaits a `transfer_data_task` that was never created → unhandled ASGI exception. Harmless but noisy. **Fix:** new `_safe_ws_close(websocket, *, code, reason)` helper wraps `close()` in `try/except` (debug log, Tier-2 honest-degrade — the socket is going away regardless); routed **all 10** close sites in both `agent_avatar_telemetry_stream` + `fleet_avatar_telemetry_stream` through it. Happy path unchanged (same code/reason); a close-time race is now logged-and-ignored instead of crashing the ASGI app. The pre-accept `auth.py` close (default-off `crew_scope_token`, HTTP-403 path) left as-is. Tests: `test_bf626_ws_safe_close.py` (4) — the exact `transfer_data_task` `AttributeError` reproduction swallowed, `RuntimeError` swallowed, happy-path forwards code/reason, source guard that neither handler calls `websocket.close(` directly; 7 existing AD-722b-4 tests unchanged (11 together). PUSHED.

**BF-625 shipped (2026-06-12) — transcript-grounded recall picked the wrong room (the AD-986 epic).** The Captain enabled AD-986b and live-tested it: asked in a 1:1 *"Do you remember the group chat with Yeo? What visual issue did he have?"*, the Counselor honestly reported no memory. Read-only forensics on a **copy** of the live `chat_threads.db` (running instance untouched) found the sovereign gate was fine (8 rooms returned) but `consult_transcript` selected the **wrong room** — Ezri's 114-message default 1:1 (*"Hello Ezri"*) scored **386** vs the real *"Ezri, Yeo"* group room's **74**, because the v1 scorer summed raw token overlap across every message so a long room won on common-word volume. A restart alone would have injected the wrong transcript. **Fix:** replace the raw-sum with IDF-weighted **BM25 body relevance + a strongly-weighted title field** (`k1=1.5`, `b=0.75`, `_TITLE_FIELD_WEIGHT=12.0`). IDF neutralises ubiquitous words (no stop-list), BM25 saturates TF + normalises length (volume can't win), and the title field is the room's identity — for a group room the title IS the participant callsign list (AD-942), so naming "Yeo" matches the right room. Excerpt now centres on the winning content tokens. Verified end-to-end on the live data via the production `consult_transcript`: the Yeo query returns an *"Ezri, Yeo"* room (Yeo: *"I don't have the visual feed… the frame is stale"*); "divergence event with Scout" returns the Divergence room. Tests: `test_bf625_transcript_ranking.py` (5, BF-287 real store) — headline regression (61-msg catch-all must not outrank 4-msg named group room), raw-overlap-would-have-failed guard, title tiebreak, IDF neutralisation, no-match→None; 14 AD-986b + 21 AD-986d unchanged; transcript/AD-986 blast **110 passed**. PUSHED.

**AD-986d shipped (2026-06-12) — transcript lifecycle: retention purge + tombstones + recording-purged indication (#930, the AD-986 dual-layer conversational-memory epic).** AD-986b let a crew agent consult the canonical chat transcript (the recording), but the recording was immortal — `ChatThreadStore` had only `archived` + a manual `delete_thread`, so transcripts would persist forever. The Captain flagged it: recordings shouldn't persist indefinitely, and an agent could still hold a *subjective* memory of a purged group chat and must be *told* the recording is gone rather than silently trusting its lossy recollection. **Fix (four parts, default-off):** (1) `MemoryConfig.transcript_retention_days` (default **0 = never purge**, opt-in) + `transcript_reaper_interval_seconds`; (2) new `chat_thread_tombstones` table + `ChatThreadTombstone` + store methods `purge_thread` (tombstone-FIRST then hard-delete — a crash never deletes without a trace), `purge_threads_older_than` (**pinned exempt**, archived eligible), `tombstones_for_participant` (sovereign-scoped) — manual `delete_thread` deliberately leaves NO tombstone; (3) `purged_room_notice` + `render_purge_indication` in `transcript_grounding.py` — the complement of `consult_transcript`: when a *purged* room the agent took part in matches the query (by title), it injects an honest `=== RECORDING PURGED (retention) ===` block ("the recording … was purged on <date> … you may still hold your own memory, but it can no longer be consulted"), wired as the `else` branch in `_recall_relevant_memories` + `_build_user_message`; (4) `TranscriptReaper` (`threads/transcript_reaper.py`, the AD-733-1/AD-706b reaper shape) started from `finalize.py` only when `retention_days > 0`, stopped in `shutdown.py`. **Default 0 → reaper never starts, nothing is purged, recall prompts byte-identical to AD-986b.** Sovereign scope preserved end-to-end — a purge notice is only ever surfaced to a participant of the purged room (headline leak guard). Tests: `test_ad986d_transcript_retention.py` (21, BF-287 real `ChatThreadStore` + injected clock — purge+tombstone, delete-no-tombstone, stale-not-fresh + pinned-exempt + archived-eligible, sovereign leak guard, reaper sweep/disabled/lifecycle/honest-degrade); transcript/thread/reaper/fan-out blast **430 passed**; cognitive_agent + AD-986b sibling **21 passed**. PUSHED. **Operational:** `config/system.yaml` `memory.transcript_retention_days: 90` enabled (commit d3cf0869) — purges room recordings 90 days after last activity; needs a restart. Next: AD-986a (subjective-layer hardening); AD-981b (FoK-gated consult — consult the recording only when subjective recall is weak, the research's ideal trigger).

**BF-624 shipped (2026-06-12) — shared-meeting vision refreshes a STALE ring, not just an empty one (#882).** The Captain ran a live group chat and the crew surfaced a vision asymmetry: the Counselor (vision-capable observer) described the LIVE frame (plaid shirt) while the Yeoman (NOT vision-capable) described a 22-hour-old frame (black shirt, teapot) for the whole conversation — the crew diagnosed it exactly ("the camera channel routes to Ezri's context, not mine; a routing miss"). Root cause: `_render_agent_scene_block` shared the consumer's latest frame into a participant's ring ONLY when that ring was **empty** (the BF-617/BF-620 fallback); the Yeoman's ring held a stale disk-hydrated frame (AD-742f) so the fallback never fired, while `force_describe_current_frame` only freshens vision-capable observers' rings. **Fix:** generalize empty→stale — share the consumer's latest observation whenever it is **fresher** than the ring's own latest (`_own is None or _shared.timestamp > _own.timestamp`). Byte-identical for an up-to-date observer (no append); a fresher own frame is kept. Tests: `test_bf624_stale_meeting_vision_refresh.py` (6, BF-287 real consumer + rings) — the 22h-stale→live refresh headline + empty-ring preserved + no-downgrade + sentinel + disabled-gate; BF-617/BF-620/AD-978 regression 30 passed; fan-out/perception/temporal blast 526 passed. **Also (operational config, not new code):** set `temporal.captain_timezone: America/Denver` (the AD-984d feature shipped but the config value was empty `""`, so the crew only saw UTC and honestly estimated — the Captain's "added the feature, why isn't it picked up") + enabled `memory.transcript_grounded_recall_enabled` for the upcoming AD-986b 1:1 recall test. Both need a restart. PUSHED.

**AD-986b shipped (2026-06-12) — transcript-grounded recall: consult the canonical record (the recording) (#928, the AD-986 dual-layer conversational-memory epic).** A live FoK-calibration session had the Counselor honestly fail to recall a *group chat* she participated in and contributed to. Offline store query ruled out the obvious causes: group chats **do** create memories (30 `group_chat_fanout` episodes, AD-933a; 28/30 with AD-977 reflections) and attribution is **correct** (sharded to the replier, owner-id matches her 1:1 id). Real cause: her substantive contribution survived only as the **trigger text of the next speaker's episode** (owned by that speaker) — her own shard held only greetings, the substance lost to reflection truncation (240-char cap) + recency-weighted eviction (393→372 during the session). The sovereign shard is *subjective + lossy by design*; the missing layer is the **objective record** — the transcript an agent can consult to ground a recollection (the Captain's framing: "if it was recorded they can review it"). Grounded in transactive memory (Wegner — external recordings), distributed cognition (Hutchins), and the contagion-firewall finding (de-risks the AD-979 frontier). **Fix:** new `ChatThreadStore.threads_for_participant` (sovereign-scoped) + pure `cognitive/transcript_grounding.py` (`consult_transcript` — deterministic AD-979c lexical match over the agent's OWN rooms, bounded excerpt or `None`; `render_transcript_grounding` — labels it `=== CANONICAL TRANSCRIPT (the recording) ===`, distinct from subjective memory) wired into `_recall_relevant_memories` + `_build_user_message`. New `MemoryConfig.transcript_grounded_recall_enabled` (default off → byte-identical). **Sovereign scope is load-bearing** — an agent never receives a transcript for a room it wasn't in (the headline test). Tests: `test_ad986b_transcript_grounding.py` (14, BF-287 real store, incl. the unowned-room leak guard); regression blast **1633 passed / 1 skipped**. Next: AD-986a (subjective-layer hardening). PUSHED.

**AD-985 + BF-623 shipped (2026-06-12) — hands-free voice wave: group-meeting open-mic + 1:1 conversation-mode gate fix (#925, #924).** The Captain's live test of "Conversation mode" hit two faults. **BF-623:** a 1:1 conversation armed only if the global Ship's-Computer `voiceEnabled` flag was on (default OFF), so it silently fell back to push-to-talk — the BF-614/AD-949 mis-gating class. **Fix:** conversation mode is **itself** the opt-in — the 1:1 arm effect depends ONLY on the mic-mode selection, not any voice-output flag (the open mic is an *input* affordance; whether a reply is *spoken* stays a per-reply `hxi_chat_tts_{agentId}` decision in `onAgentReply`). **AD-985:** a group meeting had no continuous-listen path (the AD-747 controller is a 1:1 duplex; meetings use composer PTT). **Fix:** generalize the singleton `ConversationController` with two optional hooks — `submitTranscript(text, history)` (route a completed utterance through the live `sendText` group fan-out instead of the 1:1 POST; `useMeetingVoice` speaks the replies so the controller goes straight to `silence_pending`, mic stays live) and `canListen()` (the AD-922 **meeting-wide echo gate** — drop a transcript while `speakingAgentId != null`, so the mic never transcribes the crew's own TTS; a dropped echo refreshes the 30s release timer). The ProfileChatTab arm effect is **relocated** below `sendText` (so it can read `meetingActive`/`callAudioEnabled`/`sendText`/`speakingAgentId`) and branches: not-conversation → disarm; meeting → arm the open-mic (gated on the AD-949 `callAudioEnabled`, default ON) with the echo gate + group-submit; else → 1:1 (BF-623 mode-only gate). Two refs keep the callbacks current without re-arming each render. Tests: `conversationController.group.test.ts` (6, mocked boundary — group submit not 1:1 fetch, silence_pending, echo-gate drop/admit, timer refresh, 30s release, clean disarm); `ProfileChatTab.voicewave.test.tsx` (11, `?raw` source assertions); two obsolete-contract tests updated (conversationWiring voiceEnabled-flip + bf290 TTS-disabled). Full UI **1480 passed / 1 skipped**, build clean. PUSHED.

**AD-981a shipped (2026-06-12) — recall-confidence on the live sovereign path: Feeling-of-Knowing logging for `recall_for_agent` (the earmarked AD-981 recall live-wiring).** AD-979a built the recall-confidence FoK signal + AD-979c the hybrid axis, but both landed on `recall()`/`recall_with_confidence` while the crew's actual sovereign recall goes through `recall_for_agent` (AD-397) — so they were **mechanism-only**, and the "invisible miss" stayed invisible on the path that matters. **Fix (mirrors the AD-979a shim, conservative observability — no behavior change):** new **`recall_for_agent_with_confidence(agent_id, query, k)`** is the single source of truth; **`recall_for_agent` is now a byte-identical shim** over it. It surfaces an **agent-scoped** band — best similarity + candidate count over the agent's OWN episodes, tallied before the BF-027 relaxed return threshold (Koriat accessibility) and classified against the general `relevance_threshold`/`weak_floor` so it's comparable to the global AD-979a band. New default-off **`MemoryConfig.recall_fok_logging_enabled`** (threaded through `__main__.py`) emits the band per sovereign recall as a structured INFO line (agent/band/best_sim/owned-candidates/returned/query), turning a live multi-agent session into a recall-calibration tool; logged even on an empty recall. Default off → byte-identical recalled episodes + zero new log noise; the band is logged, **not yet** used to drive the AD-979b expand loop on this path (deferred to AD-981b after the logged bands give calibration data). Tests: `test_ad981a_recall_fok_logging.py` (10, BF-287 real `EpisodicMemory` + real embeddings) incl. the agent-scoped-not-global discriminator + shim byte-identity; episodic/recall regression blast **737 passed / 1 skipped**. PUSHED.

**AD-983d shipped (2026-06-12) — deferred-tool model: manifest + lazy `find_intents` retrieval (#917, COMPLETES the AD-983 Copilot-parity epic).** The scale piece. `PromptBuilder` rendered **every** `IntentDescriptor`'s full param table into the decomposer prompt; at hundreds of intents that blows the context budget AND degrades selection (a long flat list makes the model worse at picking) — the failure the GitHub Copilot **deferred-tool** mechanism prevents. **Fix (mechanism + conservative wiring, default-off — the AD-979b/c precedent):** new **`CapabilityRetriever`** (`cognitive/capability_retriever.py`) = `tool_search` for ProbOS — `manifest(scope)` → cheap `[(name, one_line)]`, `find_intents(concept, scope, k, dense_ranking=None)` → full descriptors ranked by the AD-979c `reciprocal_rank_fusion` over two **lexical** axes (name-tokens + full-text incl description/usage_hint/params), reusing `fts_or_query`. **Deterministic** for `(scope, concept, catalog)`; **vocabulary-mismatch safe** (a capability whose name shares no token with the query is still found via its description); `dense_ranking` is the forward semantic-axis extension point (fused via the same RRF). New `CognitiveConfig.deferred_capability_threshold: int = 0` (**0 disables → byte-identical**; manifest kicks in only when the catalog exceeds it). `PromptBuilder.build_system_prompt(..., manifest_mode=False)` + `_build_intent_table(..., manifest_mode=False)`: manifest mode keeps **core/utility** full (tiering) and renders the **domain** tier as a compact name+one-line manifest. Decomposer `_use_manifest()` gate + `runtime.py` threads the threshold through. **Skill bodies** already lazy (AD-596). Tests: `test_ad983d_deferred_capability.py` (21, BF-287 real descriptors + real retriever/PromptBuilder/registry) — manifest 8-of-200, `find_intents("search the web")`→web_search w/ params+usage_hint, determinism, vocab-mismatch, scope, k-cap, dense fuse, byte-identical default, manifest tiering + boundedness, the gate; regression blast **455 passed / 6 skipped**. **The AD-983 epic (a→b→c→d) is complete.** PUSHED.

**AD-983c shipped (2026-06-12, LOCAL ONLY — the Captain decides the push) — Capability panel UI: the Captain surface for per-agent tool/skill enablement (#916, the AD-983 Copilot-parity epic).** AD-983b shipped the backend (per-agent skill grants + the unified `/capabilities` API) but the only Captain-facing control was the AD-982 single vision toggle (one boolean). The "each agent independently enables different tools/skills" half of the epic needed a reusable UI for the FULL tool + skill set. **Fix:** new reusable **`CapabilityPanel`** (`ui/src/components/profile/CapabilityPanel.tsx`) bound to the AD-983b API — `GET /capabilities` → `{tools, skills}`, `POST /capabilities/set` `{kind, id, enabled}`. Renders `TOOLS (n)` + `SKILLS (n)` sections, each row a toggle (`aria-pressed`, On/amber `#f0b060`·Off/dim `#666680`) + name + human `source` label (granted/restricted/role default/dept default); **optimistic with revert-on-failure** (the AD-982 pattern); a `deps` prop injects fetchers for tests; HXI-compliant (stroke styling, no emoji). Mounted on the personnel **`ServiceRecord`** (`sr-section-capabilities`, after Skills) and the **`ProfileInfoTab`** Profile tab (crew-only `CAPABILITIES` block). Tests: `CapabilityPanel.test.tsx` (10, `deps`-injected — loading/error/empty states, On/Off + aria-pressed, the four source labels, optimistic POST `{kind,id,enabled}`, revert-on-fail, refetch-on-agentId, no-emoji guard); `ProfileInfoTab.wakePhrase.test.tsx` updated to find the voice-profile PUT by URL (the mount shifted fetch ordering). Full UI **1463 passed / 1 skipped** (239 files), `npm run build` clean. Next: AD-983d (#917) — manifest + lazy intent retrieval (the deferred-tool model), the final sub-AD of the epic. COMMITTED LOCAL ONLY — NOT pushed.

**AD-983b shipped (2026-06-12, LOCAL ONLY — the Captain decides the push) — per-agent capability enablement: per-agent skill grants + unified /capabilities API (#915, the AD-983 Copilot-parity epic).** The "each crew agent independently enables different tools and skills" piece. Tools already had per-agent grants (`ToolPermissionStore`); cognitive skills were gated only by department + rank, so a skill couldn't be enabled on one agent and withheld from its peers. **Fix:** new **`SkillGrantStore`** (`cognitive/skill_grants.py`, mirrors `ToolPermissionStore` exactly minus the permission level — skills are binary) + a catalog overlay `CognitiveSkillCatalog.effective_entries_for_agent(agent_id, *, department, min_rank)` = dept/rank defaults ∪ explicit grants − restrictions (back-compat: byte-identical to `list_entries` with no grant store). The onboarding intent-subscription path resolves skills through the overlay so grants drive what an agent reaches. Unified **`GET /api/agent/{id}/capabilities`** → `{tools, skills}` each with `granted`+`source`, and **`POST /api/agent/{id}/capabilities/set`** `{kind, id, enabled, reason}` (audit-logged via the new `CAPABILITY_ACCESS_RESOLVED` event) — the generalization of the AD-982 vision set endpoint. Store wired in `startup/communication.py` (`data/skill_grants.db`) → `runtime.skill_grant_store` → stopped in shutdown. **Independence proven:** a skill granted to agent A is in A's effective set and NOT in a same-dept peer B's. Tests: `test_ad983b_capability_enablement.py` (16, BF-287 real store + real catalog with on-disk SKILL.md). Next: AD-983c (#916) Capability panel UI. COMMITTED LOCAL ONLY — NOT pushed.

**AD-983a shipped (2026-06-11, LOCAL ONLY — the Captain decides the push) — capability affordance layer: the tool carries its own manual (#914; the AD-983 Copilot-parity north-star).** The headline of the AD-983 epic (#913): a capability's *affordance* ("how to invoke a tool you hold") was authored into ONE agent (Yeo's BF-599 block + AD-870 task-protocol), so every other crew agent held the same mesh capabilities and didn't know how to reach them (the web-search incident). **Fix:** the affordance now travels WITH the capability and auto-surfaces to whoever can reach it (the Copilot tool-schema model). New `IntentDescriptor.usage_hint` (declared on the 5 `[MESH]`-backed read intents: `list_directory`/`read_file`/`search_files`/`web_search`/`read_page`); new `CognitiveAgent.capability_affordances()` derives `{intent: usage_hint}` from the LIVE registry (substrate-gated — only serving agents contribute); the base `_conversational_capability_block` (was `""`) renders them into EVERY crew agent's conversational prompt. Yeo's capability override + `_available_mesh_read_intents` retired; its task protocol keeps only the role-specific `[CREATE_TASK]` delegation judgment. **Folds in AD-957 (#893)** — web search (and every reachable read) for all crew, not just Yeo. Tests: `test_ad983a_capability_affordances.py` (18, BF-287 real registry + real descriptors, incl. a non-Yeo Counselor getting the web affordance with no override); `test_ad870`/`test_bf599` rewritten to the generalized contract; regression blast **1110 passed / 6 skipped**. Next: AD-983b (#915). COMMITTED LOCAL ONLY — NOT pushed.

**AD-984 wave + BF-622 shipped (2026-06-11, LOCAL ONLY — the Captain decides the push) — meeting voice/chat experience (#882, #918-#923).** A live group-meeting design conversation (Captain + Ezri + Yeo) landed on one insight: a meeting is **one conversation** and voice is the Captain's chosen *interface*, so the chat should be able to recede. The voice-first loop already exists (AD-921/922/923/949 + BF-621); this wave ships the build-ready presentation slice + a robustness bug. **AD-984a:** a `meetingChatVisible` store flag (default ON) + a `chat-visibility-toggle` on `GroupChatHeader` (shown only while `meetingActive`) gates the transcript message-list in `ProfileChatTab` — hide the chat for an avatars+voice-only call; conversation/persistence/BF-621 reveal unaffected. **AD-984c:** new pure `chat/scrollAnchor.ts` `isPinnedToBottom` + a `pinnedToBottomRef` on the message-list so auto-scroll only fires when the Captain is at the bottom (never yanks them down while reading earlier turns). **AD-984d:** `TemporalConfig.captain_timezone` (IANA) injects the Captain's current local time into `_build_temporal_context` alongside UTC (fixes the crew confabulating "3am" from UTC when it was 9pm Mountain); `tzdata` added to deps (Windows has no system IANA db). **BF-622:** new `strip_visual_context_block` removes the AD-978 `Current Visual Context` scaffolding from a reply before persist (it leaked when a degraded LLM proxy echoed its input); applied at the 1:1 + group reply chokepoints, guarded so normal replies are untouched. Tests: `GroupChatHeader.meeting` +3, `scrollAnchor` (7), `test_ad984d_captain_timezone` (6), `test_bf622_strip_visual_context` (8); full UI **1453/1 skip**, build clean, backend blast **1266 passed**. AD-984b (condensed-sidebar presentation + WCAG 2.1 AA) deferred (design). COMMITTED LOCAL ONLY — NOT pushed.

**AD-982 shipped (2026-06-11, LOCAL ONLY — the Captain decides the push) — Captain-controlled persistent vision toggle (#882).** The Captain can now grant/revoke a crew agent's PERMANENT ambient vision from two UI surfaces: the `AgentProfilePanel` header (eye glyph toggle) and the personnel `ServiceRecord` (Granted/Off button). **AD-982a (backend):** a data-dir override sidecar (`data/vision_overrides.json`, keyed by agent_type) re-applied onto the registry at boot so a grant survives restart without mutating the tracked crew-profile YAML; new `POST /api/agent/{id}/vision-capability/set` direct grant/revoke (audit-logged), distinct from the agent-initiated propose/approve flow; `vision_capable` surfaced on `/profile`, `/roster`, `/record`. **AD-982b (frontend):** the two toggles, optimistic with revert-on-failure, HXI-compliant. Tests: `test_ad982a_vision_overrides.py` (15) + `ServiceRecord.vision` (6) + `AgentProfilePanel.visionToggle` (3); full UI 1438/1, build clean, backend blast green. COMMITTED LOCAL ONLY — NOT pushed.

**BF-621 shipped (2026-06-11, LOCAL ONLY — the Captain decides the push) — meeting text reveal follows speech, "hear then see" (#882).** The Captain's re-test of BF-618: text was more delayed but still said "{callsign} is typing" *while the agent spoke*, and drifted from the voice. Decision: label it "{callsign} is **speaking**…" and reveal each reply's text **after** that agent finishes speaking. Root cause: BF-618 ran two independent clocks (voice + a timer-paced text reveal) concurrently, so they diverged. **Fix (frontend only):** when a meeting is live AND call audio is ON, drive the reveal off the AD-921 voice sequencer's per-utterance completion (the single clock — can't drift/burst). `MeetingVoiceDeps` gains `onUtteranceStart`/`onUtteranceEnd`; `useMeetingVoice.speakReplies(replies, hooks?)` forwards them gen-guarded; `ProfileChatTab` sets the `'speaking'` label on start and clears-then-appends on end. Audio OFF/muted or text chat keeps the AD-960 timer reveal. Honest-degrade: TTS failure reveals text on the BF-615 safety timeout (never lost). Tests: `meetingVoice.test.ts` +3, `ProfileChatTab.ad976.test.tsx` rewritten, `TypingIndicator.test.tsx` +1; full UI **1443/1**, build clean. COMMITTED LOCAL ONLY — NOT pushed.

**BF-620 shipped (2026-06-11, LOCAL ONLY — the Captain decides the push) — shared meeting vision survives restart (follow-up to BF-617, #882).** The Captain's re-test after BF-617 STILL showed "Yeo can't see but Ezri can." Root cause: `VisionConsumer._last_observation` is in-RAM and starts `None` after a restart, but a vision-capable observer's ring **hydrates from disk** (AD-742f), so post-restart Ezri renders a hydrated scene while the yeoman's fallback found `None` → the "camera not active" sentinel. The Captain restarts before every test, so it reproduced every time (BF-617's `_FakeConsumer` tests never exercised the in-RAM-vs-hydrated split). **Fix:** `latest_shared_observation()` now, when `_last_observation` is unset, borrows the most-recent observation any registered observer currently holds (scanning their real rings) — additive, the warm in-RAM path is unchanged. Tests: `test_bf620_shared_meeting_vision_restart.py` (5, BF-287 real consumer + real rings); BF-617 (6) + AD-978 (13) + AD-733a (26) regression green. COMMITTED LOCAL ONLY — NOT pushed.

**BF-617 + BF-618 + BF-619 shipped (2026-06-11, LOCAL ONLY — the Captain decides the push) — live group-meeting test fixes (#882).** The Captain's first live test of the AD-976/978 wave surfaced three integration bugs the mocked tests missed. **BF-617** (Yeo couldn't see): perception frames reach only `vision_capable` observers, so a non-observer participant got the "camera not active" sentinel; `VisionConsumer.latest_shared_observation()` + a fallback in `_render_agent_scene_block` now share the one meeting feed into every present participant's ring (meeting-scoped, no ambient enrollment). **BF-618** (text dumped all at once): the AD-976 speech-event coupling burst when TTS didn't pace; meetings now use the same timer-paced progressive reveal as text chat (cannot burst), with voice kicked off concurrently. **BF-619** (hand-off ignored): `extract_directed_callsign` was leading-only; new `extract_handoff_callsign` also catches an end-of-turn vocative ("... Yeo, your read?"), so the cascade hard-includes the addressed peer and earns the AD-961 extension. Tests: `test_bf619_*`, `test_bf617_*`, rewritten `ProfileChatTab.ad976.test.tsx`; backend blast green, full UI 1429/1, build clean. COMMITTED LOCAL ONLY — NOT pushed.

**AD-976 shipped (2026-06-10, LOCAL ONLY — the Captain decides the push) — Meeting-mode text follows the voice (#899, the Natural Conversation epic).** In a live meeting the text dumped all replies at once while the voice paced them one at a time ("shouldn't see the text until you've said the words"). **Fix (frontend):** `ProfileChatTab` stages the round's replies in a ref and reveals each as its agent starts speaking, off the existing `useMeetingVoice` `speakingAgentId` seam (the voice hook is untouched); a muted meeting + text chat fall back to the AD-960 progressive reveal. Tests: `ProfileChatTab.ad976.test.tsx` (8, mirror convention); full UI **1434 passed / 1 skipped**, build clean. COMMITTED LOCAL ONLY — NOT pushed.

**AD-980c shipped (2026-06-10, LOCAL ONLY — the Captain decides the push) — Dream interpretation, the loop closes (#912, the Agent-self-interpretation epic).** The novel sleep->dream->wake->interpret loop. New `CognitiveAgent.interpret_own_dream()` gathers the agent's own AD-980b-attributed dream reflections (`MemorySource.REFLECTION` via `recent_for_agent`) and interprets them via the AD-980a engine (refactored into a shared `_run_interpretation`), storing an agent-owned `[interpretation]` episode that feeds its self-model. Honesty-bound (AD-592); opt-in `dream_interpretation_enabled` (default False). Tests: `test_ad980c_dream_interpretation.py` (7) + AD-980a (9) + AD-980b (5) = 21 green. **The full AD-980a+b+c dream-interpretation loop is built and tested.** COMMITTED LOCAL ONLY — NOT pushed.

**AD-980b shipped (2026-06-10, LOCAL ONLY — the Captain decides the push) — Per-agent dream attribution (#911, the Agent-self-interpretation epic).** The subjectivity gap: dream reflections (AD-599) were stored `agent_ids=[]` (ownerless), so no agent could recall "its" dream — a dream had no dreamer, making AD-980c interpretation impossible. Each candidate already computed its `involved_agents` (recorded in `dag_summary`) but discarded it at storage. **Fix (gated `per_agent_dream_attribution_enabled`, default False):** store the reflection with `agent_ids=<involved agents>` so `recall_for_agent` returns it for each dreamer; agentless snapshots (emergence/notebook) stay ownerless. Default off → byte-identical to AD-599. Prerequisite for AD-980c. Tests: `test_ad980b_dream_attribution.py` (5) + AD-599 suite (18) green. COMMITTED LOCAL ONLY — NOT pushed.

**AD-980a shipped (2026-06-10, LOCAL ONLY — the Captain decides the push) — Reflective recall interpretation (#910, the Agent-self-interpretation epic; from Ezri's "can crew interpret their dreams?" question).** The meaning-making rung above the AD-979 metamemory layer: AD-979 made recall honest; this makes it meaningful. New `CognitiveAgent.interpret_recall(episodes, *, focus, store)` runs an instructions-first LLM pass over the agent's OWN recalled episodes (reusing the AD-721d `propose_appearance` reflection shape) and stores the result as an agent-owned `[interpretation]` `MemorySource.REFLECTION` episode. Honesty-bound (AD-592): no episodes → `None` (never invents); gap-regex-safe prompt. Opt-in `recall_interpretation_enabled` (default False — a real extra LLM pass). Tests: `test_ad980a_recall_interpretation.py` (9). COMMITTED LOCAL ONLY — NOT pushed.

**AD-977 shipped (2026-06-10, LOCAL ONLY — the Captain decides the push) — Group-thread episodic continuity (#900, the Natural Conversation epic).** The crew flagged patchy recall of GROUP conversations. Root cause: AD-933a group episodes indexed only `user_input` (the Captain's trigger) + an empty `reflection`; the agent's own reply lived in `outcomes[]`, which `_prepare_document`/FTS5 do not embed — so an agent couldn't recall what IT said in a room. **Fix (mirrors the 1:1 `_store_action_episode`):** the group episode now sets `reflection="<callsign> said in group chat: <reply>"`, which IS indexed by both the embedding and the FTS5 sidecar. Additive — `user_input` and `outcomes[].response` unchanged. Tests: `test_ad977_group_recall.py` (3) + AD-933a suite (7) green. Composes with AD-979a/b/c. COMMITTED LOCAL ONLY — NOT pushed.

**AD-979c + AD-979b shipped (2026-06-10, LOCAL ONLY — the Captain decides the push) — Hybrid retrieval + metacognitive control loop (#905, #904, the Oracle-recall epic).** **AD-979c** fixes the vocabulary-mismatch gap: pure `fts_or_query` + `reciprocal_rank_fusion` (RRF) helpers fuse the cosine ranking with the existing AD-567b FTS5 keyword axis, so a memory encoded under different words than the query is still surfaced; gated `hybrid_recall_enabled` (default False → byte-identical; RRF single-ranking identity guarantees it). **AD-979b** adds the Nelson–Narens control loop: pure `decide_recall_control` (strong→accept / weak→expand / none→abstain) + `recall_expansion_variants` + `recall_with_control()` which, only on a weak band, issues bounded re-queries and returns `(episodes, confidence, actions)` for honest reporting (AD-592). The control loop is built+tested but not yet wired into the live recall path (conservative — a follow-up activation). Tests: `test_ad979c_*` (16) + `test_ad979b_*` (15); three AD-979 suites together 44 passed. COMMITTED LOCAL ONLY — NOT pushed.

**AD-979a shipped (2026-06-10, LOCAL ONLY — the Captain decides the push) — Recall-confidence Feeling-of-Knowing signal (#903, the Oracle-recall epic).** `EpisodicMemory.recall()` computed `similarity = 1.0 - distance` per candidate then discarded it, so callers couldn't tell fast-absence ("nothing on this") from the *invisible miss* ("best match 0.65, just under the 0.7 bar") — the crew flagged this as a confidence-calibration gap. **Fix:** a frozen `RecallConfidence` (band/best_similarity/candidate_count) + a pure `classify_recall_confidence` classifier (strong/weak/none; `candidate_count==0` = fast-absence vs `>0` below floor = slow-gap), surfaced by a new `recall_with_confidence()` that is now the single source of truth — `recall()` is a byte-identical shim over it. Accessibility-derived (Koriat), never self-reported. New `MemoryConfig.recall_confidence_weak_floor` (0.45). Tests: `test_ad979a_recall_confidence.py` (13); episodic regression blast 804 passed. COMMITTED LOCAL ONLY — NOT pushed.

**AD-978 shipped (2026-06-10, LOCAL ONLY — the Captain decides the push) — Camera perception in group chat (#882, the "Natural Conversation" epic).** The Captain reported the camera feed was invisible to the crew in a group chat even though the 1:1 preview shows live scene descriptions. Root cause: the AD-733a scene-context injection lived only in the 1:1 DM path; the group fan-out (`thread_fanout.py` `_send_one`) never pulled each agent's `VisionWorkingMemory`. **Fix:** a once-per-round `_maybe_force_describe_frame` (the shared frame is described once, then written to every observer's WM) + a per-agent `_render_agent_scene_block` whose output is prepended to each dispatched agent's `params["text"]`, mirroring the 1:1 path. Gated on `perception.enabled` (default False → byte-identical when off); BF-294 empty-ring sentinel preserved (the agent is told not to confabulate). `trigger_body` stays clean so the stored episode records the Captain's real message. Tests: `test_ad978_group_perception.py` (13); regression blast 410 passed. COMMITTED LOCAL ONLY — NOT pushed.

**AD-975 + AD-963a shipped (2026-06-10, LOCAL ONLY — the Captain decides the push) — Turn-taking self-knowledge + broadcast terminator (#882, the "Natural Conversation" epic).** From the Captain's live 3-person room review (which validated AD-967 roster grounding + AD-935/950/951/955 self-regulating turn-taking, and was captured as the first AD-956 calibration point on #892). **AD-975** (instructions-first): the crew reasoned accurately about reading the floor but assumed possible *simultaneity*; the AD-935/967 group-chat hook now teaches the real mechanism — turns are sequential (you see each peer's completed reply before yours, never at once), collisions are structurally impossible so build on prior turns with confidence, there is no live "typing" cue, and addressing a colleague by name invites their turn. **AD-963a** (the ratified AD-963 recommendation, part a): a conservative `classify_broadcast` cue detector drives a broadcast terminator in `group_chat_fanout` — a plural ask to the whole room ("what do you all think?") round-robins every crew participant exactly once (cumulative exclude, relevance-ordered, each seeing prior answers, bounded by participant count) instead of stopping at the discussion `max_agent_rounds` cap; the default Discussion classification is byte-identical to AD-935/961. New `GroupChatConfig.broadcast_terminator_enabled` (default off; yaml on). Tests: `test_ad975_*` (7) + `test_ad963a_*` (8); cascade regressions green. Deferred (further live observation): AD-956 threshold, AD-958 ledger-write, AD-963b weight tilt, AD-954a dedicated window. COMMITTED LOCAL ONLY — NOT pushed.

**AD-972 → AD-974 shipped (2026-06-10, LOCAL ONLY — the Captain decides the push) — Meeting/call UX polish (#882, the "Natural Conversation" epic).** Three Captain-reported issues from testing the improved call experience. **AD-972** cuts the seen-message → TTS-start delay: `useMeetingVoice` prewarms the TTS status probe + the room's voice profiles when the meeting opens (and the resolver now promise-caches), so the first reply speaks as soon as the Piper synth returns instead of waiting on a cold `/api/agent/{id}/profile` fetch + status probe first — and inter-speaker gaps vanish. **AD-973** removes the redundant second microphone: a call showed both the composer dictation mic (next to Send) and a centered push-to-talk (`MeetingMicButton`); both auto-submit via `sendText`, so the redundant centered one is dropped — exactly one mic, next to Send. **AD-974** makes the video gallery resizable: `AvatarSlot`/`CaptainSlot` take a `scale` prop and `MeetingView` adds a drag handle below the gallery (drag down = larger portraits, clamped [1,3], persisted to `localStorage`). Frontend only. Tests: `useMeetingVoice` +2, `MeetingView` +4; full UI **1426 passed / 1 skipped**, build clean, Playwright e2e 4/4. COMMITTED LOCAL ONLY — NOT pushed.

**AD-966 → AD-971 shipped (2026-06-10, LOCAL ONLY — the Captain decides the push) — Agent-initiated group-chat bug cluster (#882, the "Natural Conversation" epic): make agent-started rooms first-class conversations.** The Captain live-tested agent-initiated group chats and surfaced six bugs, all facets of one reality — agent-initiated rooms (AD-924/925) could be *created* but were never wired into the live conversation loop. **AD-966** ≥2-participant floor in `create_group_chat` (no more 1-participant rooms talking to an absent peer like "Vance"; suppress with `no_participant_resolved`). **AD-967** present-participant **room roster** injected into each agent's group prompt (extends the AD-935 hook; rides a new `room_roster` fan-out param) — fixes agents addressing peers who aren't in the room (e.g. "Sentinel"). **AD-968** the Captain **auto-joins** a group/agent-created room they post into (scoped away from the 1:1 default) — fixes "Join" showing on chats I'm already in. **AD-969** the add-control mints a new thread ONLY for the Captain's `is_default` 1:1 home DM; every other room adds in place — fixes "adding Vance made a new chat." **AD-970** agent-initiated **kickoff**: an agent's opening message fans out to the invited crew (`group_chat_fanout(opener_id=…)`, gated `agent_initiated_kickoff_enabled`, bounded by AD-935 backstops) — fixes "Atlas opened a room but Wesley never replied." **AD-971** added participants **survive close + reopen**: `handleOpen` re-fetches the thread from the backend (was clobbering persisted adds with a stale list object) + the CHATS row prefers the fresher version — fixes "Lyra/Sentinel vanished on reopen, the list didn't update." The history-context ask is already satisfied (`_build_session_history` feeds recent turns to every participant on their next turn). Tests: `test_ad966/967/968/970_*.py` (Python) + `GroupChatHeader`/`ChatsPanel.reopenPersist` (UI), all green in isolation; full gates + commit follow. COMMITTED LOCAL ONLY — NOT pushed.

**AD-958a shipped (2026-06-10, LOCAL ONLY — the Captain decides the push) — Exploration / anti-rich-get-richer term (#882/#894, the "Natural Conversation" epic): a domain-relevant newcomer is surfaced to earn trust.** AD-958's sub-part that needs no live calibration and writes NOTHING to the trust ledger — so the safe first build. Trust-weighted selection risks monopolisation (high-trust dominates, low-trust never earns a chance — exactly the Ezri+Yeo room concern + the prior-art finding). **Decision (backend only):** a deterministic exploration bonus in the pure `ChatFacilitator.rank()` — `weight_exploration × department_relevance × (1 − trust)` (UCB-style optimism, NO randomness) — boosts a domain-relevant but unproven agent so it earns trust; gated on relevance (off-topic never boosted), ~0 for a high-trust agent (already carried by the trust term), bounded BELOW the mention weight (0.15 live vs 0.40) so a direct address always wins. New `GroupChatConfig.weight_exploration` (Pydantic default 0.0 off; system.yaml 0.15). Touches ONLY the facilitator ranking — never `record_outcome` — so zero trust-ledger poisoning risk; the ledger-WRITE core is deferred to the live observation per the issue. Tests: `test_ad958a_exploration.py` 8 (pure, no fixtures); 48 passed across AD-958a/915/955, blast green. No UI change. COMMITTED LOCAL ONLY — NOT pushed.

**AD-954 shipped (2026-06-10, LOCAL ONLY — the Captain decides the push) — "Start call" reframing (#882/#890, the "Natural Conversation" epic): the meeting toggle is a call (Teams mental model).** #890 asks for a first-class group/call surface; AD-965 already delivered the dedicated-room identity (neutral title + Chat-only) and AD-920/921/947/949 the live call (gallery + voice). The remaining naming gap: the control read "Start/End meeting." **Decision (frontend only):** reframe the existing meeting toggle (already a video glyph wired to the AD-947 gallery + AD-949 call audio) to **"Start/End call"** (aria-label + title); the testid, the `meeting_active` flag, the AD-923 marker, all wiring byte-identical. The larger AD-954 piece — a separate window keyed by `thread.id`, decoupled from `activeProfileAgent` — is sequenced last (forward marker AD-954a); AD-965 already delivers the first-class *visual* surface. Tests: `GroupChatHeader.meeting.test.tsx` +1, e2e `group-chat-open` updated; full UI suite **1416 passed / 1 skipped**, build clean, **4 Playwright e2e passed**. No backend change. COMMITTED LOCAL ONLY — NOT pushed.

**AD-965 shipped (2026-06-10, LOCAL ONLY — the Captain decides the push) — Neutral, nameable group surface (#882/#898, the "Natural Conversation" epic): a group is a room, not the host agent's profile.** Starting a group opened the host agent's `AgentProfilePanel` (title "Ezri (Counselor)", her 6 agent-scoped tabs). AD-917 had already built the rename/title/participant/meeting controls in `GroupChatHeader` + the `ChatThread.title` field + `PATCH /api/threads/{id}`, so AD-965 is the surfacing layer: **`AgentProfilePanel`** detects a group (`activeProfileThreadId` → `isGroupChat`) and shows the group title (`chatDisplayName`, Teams-style or a Captain-locked custom name) with a neutral dot instead of the host agent identity, and collapses the tab bar to Chat-only (the agent-scoped tabs are meaningless for a room); the `GroupChatHeader` still owns the participant strip + rename inside the chat tab. A 1:1 is byte-identical. Tests: `AgentProfilePanel.groupSurface.test.tsx` 4 (group title, locked custom title, tab collapse, 1:1 unchanged); full UI suite **1415 passed / 1 skipped**, `npm run build` clean. Substantially also delivers AD-954's "first-class group surface" (dedicated room identity + Chat-only), minus a separate window. No backend change. COMMITTED LOCAL ONLY — NOT pushed.

**AD-962 shipped (2026-06-10, LOCAL ONLY — the Captain decides the push) — Typing indicator during backend generation (#882/#896, the "Natural Conversation" epic): fill the dead air before the first reply.** AD-960 paces the reveal, but the backend cascade takes ~10-30s before ANY reply lands and the POST is synchronous — a silent "is it broken?" window. **Decision (frontend only, extends AD-952):** `TypingIndicator` gains an optional `verb: 'typing' | 'thinking'` (default typing); the `typingAgent` slice gains an optional `verb`; `ProfileChatTab.sendText` sets `{agentId:'', callsign:'The crew', verb:'thinking'}` BEFORE the fetch so "The crew is thinking…" shows within a frame of send, cleared the moment the first reply arrives (the AD-960 reveal then re-sets per-agent "{callsign} is typing"; the meeting path leaves it cleared for AD-921/923) and on the error path. v1 is a generic whole-crew beat; naming the likely first responder via a pre-flight facilitator rank is AD-962a. Tests: `TypingIndicator.test.tsx` +2, `useStore.typingAgent.test.ts` +1; full UI suite **1411 passed / 1 skipped**, `npm run build` clean. No backend change. COMMITTED LOCAL ONLY — NOT pushed.

**AD-961 shipped (2026-06-10, LOCAL ONLY — the Captain decides the push) — Cascade-extend-on-address (#882/#895, the "Natural Conversation" epic): a directed peer hand-off is always answered.** AD-951 routes "Ezri, …" to that peer, but only within `max_agent_rounds` — an address in the LAST cascade round was dropped (the Captain had to re-prompt). **Decision (backend only):** the fixed `for _ in range(max_agent_rounds)` cascade becomes a `while` loop granting up to `GroupChatConfig.max_address_extensions` (new, default 1) EXTRA rounds past the cap, each consumed only by a fresh **answerable** directed address (resolved via `_resolve_callsigns` to a real participant who hasn't just spoken — so a false vocative opener like "Agreed," can't burn an extension), bounded so mutual hand-offs can't ping-pong (ceiling = `max_agent_rounds + max_address_extensions`). Forced to 0 when `agent_next_speaker_selection_enabled` is off, so a zero-config boot is unaffected; within the cap the loop is byte-identical to AD-935/951. Tests: `test_ad961_cascade_extend.py` 6 (BF-287; core fix + disabled-drops + bounded-no-pingpong + inert-without-flag + false-opener-guard + config default); 37 passed across AD-961/951/935, group-chat blast green. No UI change. COMMITTED LOCAL ONLY — NOT pushed.

**BF-615 + BF-616 + AD-964 shipped (2026-06-10, LOCAL ONLY — the Captain decides the push) — three group-chat test fixes from a live Captain test (#882).** **BF-615 (voice cutoff):** the AD-921 meeting voice sequencer had a FIXED 20s per-utterance safety timeout; a long crew reply (~180 words ≈ 60–70s) overran it, the timer fired mid-speech, and the next reply's `speechSynthesis.cancel()` truncated it (every reply cut off except the last) — fix: `computeUtteranceTimeout(text)` scales the cap with word count at a conservative TTS rate (460ms/word × 1.5 headroom) floored at 20s, so real speech still resolves on `'end'` and the cap only fires when `'end'` never arrives. **BF-616 ([NO_RESPONSE] leak):** the group fan-out decline check matched only an EXACTLY-`[NO_RESPONSE]` reply, so a model's "prose + [NO_RESPONSE]" leaked the marker into the room (Ezri) — fix: case-insensitive substring match (`_NO_RESPONSE_RE`), matching the proactive.py contract, suppresses the whole reply. **AD-964 (avatar centering):** AD-947 framed every avatar at a fixed 1.42m face height regardless of stature — fix: `CrewVRM.onHeadY` measures the bounding-box crown after load and `MeetingView.FaceFraming` aims per-avatar (crown − head-center drop), defaulting to 1.42 until measured. +4 Vitest (BF-615) +2 pytest (BF-616); UI suite **1408 passed / 1 skipped**, `npm run build` clean, group-chat pytest blast **576 passed**. Issue 4 (group window is the host agent's profile, not a neutral nameable surface) filed as design issue **AD-965 (#898)**, not built. COMMITTED LOCAL ONLY — NOT pushed.

**AD-960 shipped (2026-06-09, LOCAL ONLY — the Captain decides the push) — Human-paced group-chat reveal (#882, the "Natural Conversation" epic): crew replies arrive at human speed — a think-time beat, then a 60-wpm "typing" beat — and the composer is freed during the reveal.** AD-952 paced the reveal but at a fast char-based cadence tuned to "not all at once," and the composer was locked for the whole reveal. **Decision (frontend only, refines AD-952):** `staggerReplies.ts` reworked to a two-part word-based model with exported tunable constants — `computeTypingDelay` at `TYPING_MS_PER_WORD=1000` (60 wpm) clamped `[800,9000]ms`, plus a new pure `computeProcessingDelay(index)` think-time beat (`PROCESSING_FIRST_MS=5000` for the first reply reading the Captain's message, `PROCESSING_CASCADE_MS=2500` for later reactions). `revealRepliesProgressively` shows "{callsign} is typing" across the whole `processing+typing` window (no dead air), clears it the instant the message lands, and counts the revealed-index over non-empty replies. `ProfileChatTab.sendText` releases the composer (`setSending(false)`) the moment `per_agent_replies` arrives so the Captain can read/reply WHILE the reveal paces in (the reveal is still awaited for AD-921 voice ordering). Stays sequential, not parallel — temporally correct for the relay cascade and (per the Captain) often better than parallel because it forces each speaker to listen before responding. Tests: `staggerReplies.test.ts` reworked (19); full UI suite **1404 passed / 1 skipped** (+7 over 1397 baseline, zero regressions), `npm run build` clean. Forward markers: AD-961 cascade-extend-on-address (#895), AD-962 typing-during-generation (#896), AD-963 turn-order policy (#897). COMMITTED LOCAL ONLY — NOT pushed.

**AD-959 shipped (2026-06-09, LOCAL ONLY — the Captain decides the push) — Lean shutdown consolidation: defer the LLM-bound idle steps so shutdown stops tripping the AD-820 boot-refusal.** A packaged-instance shutdown stamped `consolidation_result=partial, note="phase1_elapsed=39.0s"` → AD-820 refuses the next normal boot (SystemExit 3, recoverable only via `--force-unclean`). Root cause: the Tier-3 shutdown handler ran the FULL `dream_cycle()` under a 30s budget, and at real episode volume its per-cluster LLM calls (Step 7 procedure extraction, 7b/7c evolution + negative extraction, 7c-2 failure distillation, 7e observational learning, Step 11 spaced-retrieval therapy) overran it — the cancelled mid-flight cycle is exactly what AD-820 flags, and historically tore the HNSW index (#750). Those steps are idle-time optimization that re-runs next cycle; episodes are already persisted (BF-207), so shutdown only needs the cheap in-memory weight updates. **Decision (backend only, additive):** new `DreamingEngine.consolidate_for_shutdown()` runs ONLY micro-dream Hebbian replay + weight prune + trust consolidation, makes **no episodic-collection writes** (so no torn index), honest-degrades per step, and returns a clean `DreamReport` so the handler stamps a `full`/clean marker; `startup/shutdown.py` swaps `engine.dream_cycle()` → `engine.consolidate_for_shutdown()` (BF-303 shield + 30s safety-net `wait_for` + AD-820 marker logic untouched). Tests: `test_ad959_shutdown_light_consolidation.py` 10 tests (BF-287 real router/trust/config + real episodic/LLM stubs — proves no-LLM + no-writes + real trust boost + source-level wiring guard); 2 obsolete-contract ordering tests renamed to the new method (ordering invariant preserved). No UI change. COMMITTED LOCAL ONLY — NOT pushed.

**AD-955 shipped (2026-06-09, LOCAL ONLY — the Captain decides the push) — Advisory room awareness (#882, the "Natural Conversation" epic; weighted-trust frame from the Captain + Counselor Ezri): the facilitator becomes a SENSE ORGAN the agents read, not a director.** The `ChatFacilitator` (AD-915) already computes a confidence-weighted per-speaker ranking every round (mention/recency/department/trust) — but it was thrown away after gating, so turn-taking was the `max_speakers_per_turn=1` cap *forcing* it (a backstop doing a director's job — works at 2 agents, "one speaks, rest mute" at 5). **The frame:** determinism in PERCEPTION is fine; determinism in AGENCY feels robotic. **Decision (instructions-first, advisory — NO dispatch change, the cap/convergence backstops untouched):** a pure `chat_facilitator.build_room_signal` yields a per-speaker dict (recent_share, this_is_your_area, room_would_value = the peer the room would most value hearing); `thread_fanout` rides it on `params["room_signal"]`; and `CognitiveAgent._conversational_room_awareness_protocol` renders it on the GROUP `direct_message` path, tying each fact to a move — been carrying the room → lighter touch / hand off; better-placed peer → **defer by name** (an AD-951 hand-off, so deference gives them the floor) — reframed "teamwork … never a shortfall" to dissolve the ego problem, led by "for your judgment, not a directive" so it self-regulates without going quiet. Advisory because a facilitator that PICKS speakers is a central scheduler for conversation (against the agent-native grain); one that PUBLISHES state and lets agents decide is a sense organ feeding self-organizing agents — and *you can't defer if you were never asked.* **AD-956** (scale-aware enforcement: advisory below a small-N threshold, gating above) is deliberately deferred — the threshold is empirical, CALIBRATED after watching self-selection run with the cap still on, not guessed. `CommunicationsConfig.room_awareness_enabled` default **ON** so the Captain can observe it. Tests: `test_ad955_room_awareness.py` 19 (BF-287 real config/store/bus; pure helper + hook gap-audited + end-to-end through `group_chat_fanout`); blast-radius **2063 passed** (the only 4 failures are the pre-existing `TestSkillPipeline` serial-isolation flakes, orthogonal to AD-955), zero real regressions. No UI change. COMMITTED LOCAL ONLY — NOT pushed.

**AD-953 shipped (2026-06-09, LOCAL ONLY — the Captain decides the push) — Conversational memory & callbacks (#882/#889, the "Natural Conversation" epic): agents draw on what they genuinely recall and make natural callbacks, with a hard anti-confabulation bound.** Agents replied each turn as if meeting the Captain for the first time — no "you mentioned that last time," no continuity — even though the recalled material (episodic memories + session history) is already injected into the reply context (AD-573/AD-723a-1). A continuing presence with shared history is a strong realism signal; an amnesiac one is an instant tell — but naively encouraging callbacks risks the opposite failure, a fabricated "as you said last week…" (the AD-592 hazard). **Decision (backend only, instructions-first — Design Principle #6):** a new overridable `CognitiveAgent._conversational_memory_protocol` hook (sibling to AD-950's), invoked right after the AD-950 proactivity hook, appends guidance to weave GENUINELY recalled material into the reply (pick up an earlier thread, call back to what the person said before, let shared history shape recipient design) — framed as continuity vs amnesia — under a **top-stated AD-592 honesty rule**: reference only what is actually recalled; if uncertain, treat it as not having happened and do not raise it; never manufacture a shared memory or callback; when nothing genuine surfaces, speak to the present. Honest by construction: the agent really does receive recalled episodes (`_build_user_message` renders the AD-723a-1 memory section), so the guidance points at real content and the bound fences invention. Returns `""` off the `direct_message` path or when the new `CommunicationsConfig.conversational_memory_enabled` flag (default **ON**) is off. Tests: `test_ad953_conversational_memory.py` 9 tests (BF-287 real `CommunicationsConfig` + real hook, audited against the real `_CAPABILITY_GAP_RE` incl. the `lack` token — clean); blast-radius **1531 passed** (the only 4 failures are the pre-existing `TestSkillPipeline` serial-isolation flakes — green in isolation), zero real regressions. No UI change. Epic remaining: AD-954 first-class group/call surface. COMMITTED LOCAL ONLY — NOT pushed.

**AD-952 shipped (2026-06-09, LOCAL ONLY — the Captain decides the push) — Human response dynamics (#882/#888, the "Natural Conversation" epic): group replies arrive one at a time behind a "{callsign} is typing" beat, not all in the same instant.** The AD-914 fan-out returns ALL of a round's `per_agent_replies` synchronously, and the UI dumped them in one tight loop — every crew reply appearing in the same millisecond, the single clearest group-chat bot tell. **Decision (frontend only, additive):** a NEW pure DI sequencer `chat/staggerReplies.ts` (`computeTypingDelay` length-proportional clamped `[360,2400]ms`; `revealRepliesProgressively` = per reply: show typing → sleep → clear → append, Tier-2, always clears in `finally`, abortable — mirrors the AD-921 voice sequencer shape, fake-clock testable) + a session-scoped `typingAgent` store slice (AD-949 `callAudioEnabled` pattern, no localStorage) + a `TypingIndicator` component (three amber `#f0b060` dots pulsing via a self-contained `@keyframes`, no emoji per HXI #3, motion = state per #4). `ProfileChatTab.sendText` extracts the per-reply append into a shared `appendReply` (DRY) and, in TEXT chat, reveals progressively; in a LIVE MEETING the AD-921 voice + AD-923 speaking indicator already pace the crew, so the text stays instant there (byte-identical). The transcript renders the beat threadId-guarded. Variable LENGTH was already taught by AD-950; AD-952 owns the TIMING + the typing affordance (no duplicate clause); true streaming stays the AD-935a forward marker. Tests: `staggerReplies.test.ts` (12), `TypingIndicator.test.tsx` (5), `useStore.typingAgent.test.ts` (4); full UI suite **1397 passed / 1 skipped** (+21 over the 1376 baseline, zero regressions), `npm run build` clean, the 4 AD-941 Playwright specs **4 passed**. No backend → no pytest. Epic remaining: AD-953 conversational memory & callbacks, AD-954 first-class group/call surface. COMMITTED LOCAL ONLY — NOT pushed.

**AD-951 shipped (2026-06-09, LOCAL ONLY — the Captain decides the push) — Agent next-speaker selection (#882/#887, the "Natural Conversation" epic): an agent that names a peer actually hands them the floor.** AD-950 taught agents to "hand the floor to a named peer by callsign," but the AD-935 cascade still chose the next speaker by the AD-915 relevance ranking — so "Ops, your read on the variance?" might be answered by Bones, not Ops. AD-951 implements Conversation-Analysis turn-allocation **rule 1a** ("current speaker selects next") for agent-to-agent hand-offs (the facilitator already did it for the Captain's @-mentions). **Decision (backend only, routing — no new prompt text):** a pure `crew_profile.extract_directed_callsign` helper detects a LEADING directed address — the `@yeo …` form AND the VOCATIVE `Yeo, …` / `Yeo: …` forms (essential for AD-921 VOICE meetings where you can't speak an `@`), with `is_directed_mention`/BF #467 discipline (a message *to* a peer is a hand-off; *about* a peer is not). `group_chat_fanout` scans each prior-round reply's CLEAN text (AD-948 already stripped the `<intent>` tag), and a detected peer callsign is threaded through `_fan_one_round` → `_assemble_speaker_signals` to hard-include that peer (`mentioned=True`) in the next round — the facilitator ALWAYS honors `mentioned` (overriding the per-turn cap + convergence), still bounded by `max_agent_rounds`. Honest-degrade: a non-participant callsign is ignored; no address → byte-identical AD-935. New `GroupChatConfig.agent_next_speaker_selection_enabled` (default **OFF**, convention #14; `system.yaml` flips it on). Tests: `test_ad951_next_speaker_selection.py` 16 tests (BF-287 real `ChatThreadStore`+`IntentBus`+registry; the pure helper, the hard-include wiring, AND an end-to-end ON-vs-OFF discriminator through `group_chat_fanout`); blast-radius `-k "fanout or facilitat or reactiv or crew_profile or callsign or group or thread or chat or config or ad951 or ad935 or ad915 or ad914"` **1381 passed**, zero regressions. No UI change. Epic remaining (now self-implemented, subagent runner offline): AD-952 human response dynamics, AD-953 conversational memory, AD-954 first-class group/call surface. COMMITTED LOCAL ONLY — NOT pushed.

**AD-950 shipped (2026-06-09, LOCAL ONLY — the Captain decides the push) — Conversational proactivity (#882/#886, the "Natural Conversation" epic): teach sovereign agents to ADVANCE a live conversation instead of answering and stopping.** Agents produced a competent answer on the live 1:1/group `direct_message` path and then ended their turn — no follow-up, no proposal — so a conversation died between Captain turns and a stranger could tell instantly (humans in an engaged exchange take it somewhere; a Q&A service waits to be asked). This is the CORE Turing-test lever of the epic. **Decision (backend only, instructions-first — Design Principle #6):** a new overridable `CognitiveAgent._conversational_proactivity_protocol` hook appends calibrated guidance — end an ENGAGED turn with ONE forward move (a genuine follow-up question / an inviting observation / a concrete proposal that gives the other party an easy opening), using recipient design (react to the SPECIFIC thing said, name it, build on it), explicitly NOT every turn (calibrated to engagement + the agent's personality so it reads as conversation, not interrogation), honest disagreement welcomed, and **never invent** a follow-up about something that did not occur (honesty preserved, AD-592). In a group chat (`params["is_group_chat"]`) an extra paragraph permits handing the floor to a named peer by callsign (sets up AD-951; AD-950 only PERMITS the address). The hook returns `""` off the conversational path or when the new `CommunicationsConfig.proactive_conversation_enabled` flag (default **ON**, on even when config is absent) is off; it's invoked AFTER the AD-935 group decline protocol so it composes with reply-only-when-substantive. A cognitive_agent hook (not a standing order) because the DM/group reply path composes the `instructions` with `hardcoded_instructions=""` — a standing order is DEAD on this surface. Tests: `test_ad950_conversational_proactivity.py` 9 tests (BF-287 real `CommunicationsConfig` + real hook, both renderings audited against the real `_CAPABILITY_GAP_RE` — clean); blast-radius `-k "cognitive_agent or dm or reply or pipeline or standing or group or facilitat or config"` **1519 passed / 1 skipped**, zero regressions. No UI change. Epic remaining (architect→builder): AD-951 agent next-speaker selection, AD-952 human response dynamics, AD-953 conversational memory, AD-954 first-class group/call surface. COMMITTED LOCAL ONLY — NOT pushed.

**AD-949 shipped (2026-06-09, LOCAL ONLY — the Captain decides the push) — Call-scoped group/meeting voice (#882/#885, the "Natural Conversation" epic): the live call gets its OWN audio gate, decoupled from the Ship's-Computer `voiceEnabled`.** Group/meeting voice was silent though 1:1 voice works — the meeting path (`useMeetingVoice.ts:50`) gated on the **global `voiceEnabled`** (the AD-945 Engineering "voice output" toggle, default `false`) while 1:1 has its own per-agent `hxi_chat_tts_{agentId}` override; so group voice piggybacked on the ship-computer flag and was off unless the Captain enabled the Engineering voice AND was in a meeting — exactly what BF-614 triaged as "the voice toggle was likely off, not a code bug." **AD-949 is the structural fix:** a new session-scoped store flag `callAudioEnabled` (default **TRUE**, mirrors `voiceEnabled` minus the `localStorage` line) + the `useMeetingVoice.ts:50` gate swap (`voiceEnabled` → `callAudioEnabled`) means a live call is audible by DEFAULT (the `meetingActive` self-gate at `:49` already silences everything outside a meeting) WITHOUT turning on the Ship's Computer; `GroupChatHeader` gains a `data-testid="call-audio-toggle"` in-call mute button (shown only while `meetingActive`, local stroke-SVG, amber `#f0b060` audible / dim `#666680` muted, no emoji, `aria-pressed` = `callAudioEnabled`). The AD-921/922/923 `speakRepliesSequentially` sequencer, the per-agent AD-718 voice profiles, the AD-923 speaking indicator, the AD-922 echo gate, and the Ship's-Computer / 1:1 / wake-word voice are byte-for-byte intact — AD-949 changes ONLY the gate flag + adds the in-call control. Tests: `useMeetingVoice.test.tsx` migrates the gate flag (verbatim rename, +0) and `GroupChatHeader.meeting.test.tsx` gains +3 cases; full UI suite **1376 passed / 1 skipped** (+3 over the 1373 baseline, zero regressions), `npm run build` clean, the 4 AD-941 Playwright specs **4 passed** (unaffected). Forward markers: AD-949a persist the mute across sessions, AD-949b also surface it on `MeetingView`, AD-949c honor per-agent 1:1 overrides in group. COMMITTED LOCAL ONLY — NOT pushed.

**AD-947 + AD-948 shipped (2026-06-09, LOCAL ONLY — the Captain decides the push) — first wave of the "Natural Conversation" epic (#882): make sovereign-agent dialogue feel human end-to-end.** **AD-947 — avatar camera framing (#883):** the meeting avatar gallery rendered crew showing only their FEET — the `MeetingView` gallery `<Canvas camera={{position}}>` had no lookAt, so react-three-fiber aimed at the origin `[0,0,0]` (floor); the `CrewAvatarPopout` avoids this via `OrbitControls target={[0,1.42,0]}`.
