Read-only v1: no edit/delete/upload, no Output pane (AD-927), no population path. **Tests:** `tests/test_ad926_inputs_folder.py` (+10, BF-287 — real `ChatThreadStore` + real `WorkItemStore` + real `FilesystemAttachmentStore`, direct-await the endpoint with a `SimpleNamespace` runtime stub) + `ui/src/components/inputs/__tests__/InputsList.test.tsx` (+3, incl. a no-emoji guard). Focused gate 52 (10 AD-926 + AD-925 + AD-916 + AD-791); blast-radius `-k "thread or chat or attachment or workforce or input"` 746 passed; vitest 3 passed; `npm run build` clean. Local-only commit (the Captain reviews the epic before it goes public).

**AD-925 shipped (2026-06-08) — auto-create the task-linked workspace room (FOUNDATION of the "Task Workspace Rooms" epic AD-925 → AD-929).** When `CrewTaskExecutor` (AD-859) fans a parent `WorkItem` out to **≥2 distinct crew** agents, it now opens **ONE** task-linked group chat so the collaborators share a room while they work — the room IS the workspace. A new `CrewTaskExecutor._maybe_open_task_room(parent_id, children)` fires at the top of `run`, immediately after the `if not children: return []` guard (so the room exists BEFORE the children execute), and reuses the **existing** AD-918 `AgentGroupChatService.create_group_chat` path — no parallel thread-creation, so the per-agent cooldown + sliding-window cap + `_is_crew` participant resolution all apply unchanged. **Five honest-degrade gates, every branch returns (never raises out of the fan-out):** (1) **config** — return unless `runtime.config.group_chat.auto_task_room_enabled` (a NEW `GroupChatConfig` field, **default-OFF**: wave-10 transitional-flag convention #14 + the AD-918 create-storm history); (2) **substrate** — return if `runtime.agent_group_chat` / `runtime.chat_thread_store` are not wired (the AD-859 tests pass `runtime=object()`, so the path must never assume a full runtime); (3) **≥2-crew** — collect the DISTINCT `child.assigned_to` ids that resolve to crew via the shared public `is_crew_agent(agent, None)` predicate, return if `< 2`; (4) **idempotency** — return if a thread already exists with this `task_id` (a NEW additive `task_id` filter on `ChatThreadStore.list_threads`, mirroring the existing `project_id` clause; the `task_id` column exists since AD-791a) so a retry / re-run never duplicates; (5) **create** — fetch the parent for its title (`Task: <parent.title>`) and call `create_group_chat(creator_id=<first stable-sorted crew assignee>, title=…, participants=<the rest>, task_id=parent_id)`. **The first crew assignee is the creator** (NO service change, NO synthetic system id): it passes the service's `_is_crew` gate, is auto-added as a participant anyway, and its `_rate_ok` budget is the natural anti-storm guard — so the final participants are exactly the crew child-assignees, and the room is tagged `metadata.created_by_agent` so AD-919 surfaces + Captain-joins it. **Doubly gated (dormancy caveat):** even with `auto_task_room_enabled` ON, the crew pipeline that drives the executor (`agentic_dispatch.orchestrator_enabled`) itself ships OFF, so a zero-config boot creates no task rooms. **NOT built (epic scope — deliberate):** no Input pane (AD-926), no Output/`[ARTIFACT]` tag (AD-927), no status protocol (AD-928), no UI (AD-919 already lists the room), no `system_created` service bypass, no new `EventType`, no change to `CrewOrchestrator` / `orchestrator_enabled`. **Tests** — `tests/test_ad925_auto_task_room.py` (+8, BF-287 discipline: a real `WorkItemStore` + a real `ChatThreadStore` + a real `AgentGroupChatService` over the SAME `ChatThreadStore` instance on both the service and the runtime stub; `_Fake*` duck stubs for registry/callsign/clock/agentic-executor, never `MagicMock`): a 2-crew fan-out opens exactly one task-linked room (right title + `task_id` + child-assignee participants + sorted-first creator); a single-crew parent opens no room; a second run does not duplicate (idempotent); flag OFF → no room; non-crew assignees → no room (crew gate holds); a rate-limited creator degrades to no room while the fan-out still returns its `SubtaskResult`s; a missing-substrate runtime honest-degrades with no crash; and a direct `list_threads(task_id=…)` filter unit test. Focused gate green (50 = 8 AD-925 + AD-918 + AD-791 + AD-859); blast-radius `-k "crew_executor or crew_orchestrator or thread or chat or group or workforce"` green (683 passed). Files: `src/probos/cognitive/crew_executor.py`, `src/probos/threads/__init__.py`, `src/probos/config.py`, NEW `tests/test_ad925_auto_task_room.py`.

**AD-924 shipped (2026-06-08) — the agent-facing group-chat trigger + crew awareness (follow-up to the closed Ad-hoc Crew Collaboration epic AD-913 → AD-923).** AD-918 gave the group-chat service a LISTENER (a `create_group_chat` bus subscriber) and no EMITTER — nothing parsed an agent's proactive output into a create, and the crew were never told the feature exists, so a crew agent literally could not open a group chat (only the Captain could, via the AD-917/919 HXI). AD-924 adds the missing three layers, exactly mirroring how the Ward Room DM capability was made usable. **(1) Proactive tag extractor** — a module-level `_GROUP_CHAT_PATTERN` parses `[GROUP_CHAT title="Short room name" @cs,@cs] optional first message [/GROUP_CHAT]` (title quoted so it tolerates spaces; participant blob split on `[,\s]+`, `@` stripped, passed raw — the service resolves each by agent_id OR callsign; requiring ≥1 named participant + the auto-added creator guarantees the 2+-crew floor), and a new `ProactiveCognitiveLoop._extract_and_execute_group_chats` dispatches them DIRECT + SYNCHRONOUS via `runtime.agent_group_chat.create_group_chat(...)` — the same direct-service-call shape proactive uses for every other action (DMs → `ward_room.create_thread`, notebooks → `_records_store`). The bus `handle_intent` path is untouched (it stays the forward marker for decomposer/NL discovery). **(2) Rank gate** — Commander+, INLINE in `_extract_and_execute_actions` right after the DM block (reusing the in-scope `_RANK_ORDER_DM`), reading a NEW `CommunicationsConfig.group_chat_min_rank="commander"` placed next to `dm_min_rank`/`recreation_min_rank` (the rank POLICY lives with the comms ranks; `GroupChatConfig` keeps only the rate limits). **(3) Crew awareness** — a new `### Group Chat (Ad-Hoc Collaboration)` standing order in `federation.md`'s `## Communications` section (after Direct Messages, before Notebook; ASCII-only per the Encoding Safety rule) with the anti-flood guardrail ("open a room ONLY when 2+ crew genuinely need to work a task together; NOT during idle proactive thinking; one room per collaboration"), plus a NEW dedicated `config/manuals/group-chat.md` (NOT folded into `ward-room.md` — the Captain ruling is the substrate is `ChatThreadStore`, NOT the Ward Room; `seed_manuals` globs `*.md` so it is drop-in) covering the `[GROUP_CHAT]` tag AND the AD-920→923 meeting experience (Start Meeting, avatar gallery, sequenced voice, push-to-talk). **Storm guard preserved:** `create_group_chat` runs `_rate_ok` itself, so the AD-918 per-agent cooldown + 5/hour sliding-window cap apply on the direct path unchanged — NO new cooldown dict in `proactive.py` (DRY, the service owns the limiter). **NOT built (deliberate):** no UI (AD-917/919 cover it), no change to the AD-918 service internals (AD-924 only *calls* `create_group_chat`), no agent-to-agent auto-reply loop, no consensus gate (creating a room is reversible/low-risk — Safety Budget axiom; the rate limits are the spam control), no bus-path change, and `task_id` is omitted in v1 (the proactive loop has no clean handle on the agent's current work-item id — a forward marker, consistent with AD-918's own deferral). **Tests** — `tests/test_ad924_group_chat_trigger.py` (+11, BF-287 discipline: a real `AgentGroupChatService` over a real `ChatThreadStore`, a real-but-fake registry, and the real `CallsignRegistry` for the callsign path; the runtime container is a `MagicMock(spec=ProbOSRuntime)` shell only — every substrate the code under test touches is real, every room assertion is against the real store): Commander creates a room with the right title + participants + creator auto-added; the tag is stripped from the posted text; Ensign and Lieutenant are gated out (no room); participants resolve by callsign; a rapid second create is suppressed `rate_limited`; a malformed tag degrades cleanly (no room, no crash); a non-crew creator is rejected `not_crew`; `federation.md` carries the `[GROUP_CHAT]` instruction (ASCII-only); the manual is seeded (ASCII-only, references the meeting experience); and `group_chat_min_rank` defaults to `"commander"`. Focused gate green (27 = 11 AD-924 + 16 AD-918); blast-radius `-k "proactive or group or thread or chat or standing or manual"` green (978 passed). Files: `src/probos/proactive.py`, `src/probos/config.py`, `config/standing_orders/federation.md`, NEW `config/manuals/group-chat.md`, NEW `tests/test_ad924_group_chat_trigger.py`.

**AD-923 shipped (2026-06-07) — CLOSES the Ad-hoc Crew Collaboration epic (AD-913 → AD-923).** Meeting presence + speaking indicator + transcript writeback — the FINAL Phase-2 AD. AD-922 closed the two-way voice loop; AD-923 finishes the "video call" feel and records it: the active speaker's gallery avatar lights (amber ring + pulse, HXI #4 motion = state), the others dim, a presence header shows who's in, and ending the meeting writes a transcript marker back to the thread. A purely-additive FRONTEND AD: NO backend file touched, NO new pytest (the `POST /messages` endpoint, the store method, and the `system` role enum all already exist). **Part 1 — who's-speaking highlight (WRAPPER treatment, `CrewVRM` untouched):** `MeetingView` gains ONE optional prop `speakingAgentId?: string | null` (kept optional so AD-920's `MeetingView.test.tsx` stays green); `ProfileChatTab:826` passes the value already destructured from `useMeetingVoice` (AD-921) and already live at the mount (it feeds the AD-922 mic echo gate at `:836`). Per-slot it threads `speaking={id === speakingAgentId}` + `someoneSpeaking={speakingAgentId != null}`; `AvatarSlot` applies an amber `boxShadow` ring + a co-located `meetingSpeakingPulse` `@keyframes` (mirrors `CrewCollaborationPanel.tsx:186`) + full opacity on the speaker, `opacity:0.5` dim on non-speakers, neutral idle — all on the INNER avatar container `<div>`, never `CrewVRM`. `data-speaking` (slot root) + `data-dim` (inner) give jsdom-safe assertions; the animation is browser-real, jsdom ignores it. **Part 2 — presence:** a `meeting-presence` header (`{crewIds.length} in meeting`) + a `captain-present` chip (`You (Captain)` — the Captain is the viewer, excluded from the gallery); join/leave needs no new code (the gallery already re-renders on `thread.participants` change). **Part 3 — transcript writeback (deterministic, client-side, no LLM, no backend):** a thin `threadApi.appendMessage(threadId, {author_id, role, body, metadata?})` wrapper (Tier-2 honest-degrade → `null`) POSTs the EXISTING `/api/threads/{id}/messages`; in `GroupChatHeader.handleToggleMeeting`, on END ONLY and BEFORE `setMeetingActive(false)`, it appends `"Meeting ended — N participant(s): <callsigns>."` with `role:'system'` — which is already a valid enum value (`threads.py:71`) and SKIPS the AD-914 `role=="captain"` fan-out gate (`threads.py:291`), so no agent dispatch. Honest-degrade: a `null`/failed append NEVER blocks ending the meeting; the marker persists server-side and appears on next thread load (immediate in-transcript echo = forward marker AD-923-3). The marker carries crew count + callsigns rather than a start time because `meeting_started_at` is not persisted (AD-920 stores only the flag; start-time = AD-923-2). +14 Vitest across 3 new files (`MeetingView.speaking.test.tsx` 6 — lit speaker via `data-speaking`, dimmed non-speakers via `data-dim`, neutral idle, presence count, Captain chip, no-emoji; `GroupChatHeader.transcript.test.tsx` 4 — End appends a `role:'system'` marker AND clears `meeting_active`, append fires before clear, Start does not append, no-emoji on the body; `threadApi.appendMessage.test.ts` 4 — POST endpoint/method/serialized `role:'system'` body, dict-on-ok, `null` on `!res.ok`, no-emoji) + a one-line completion of AD-920's `GroupChatHeader.meeting.test.tsx` `threadApi` mock (`appendMessage: vi.fn()`, since the End path now calls it). Full UI vitest green (**1208 passed / 1 skipped**, +14 vs AD-922's 1194); `npm run build` (tsc -b + vite) green. NO agent raise-hand (the lit avatar IS the turn signal), NO LLM summary (AD-923-1), NO live echo (AD-923-3), NO `meeting_started_at`/backend change, NO `CrewVRM` change, NO audio recording, NO full-duplex barge-in, NO federation, NO new config. **Epic complete:** the Captain can create/join a group chat, promote it to a live meeting, see/hear the crew speak in turn with the speaker lit, talk back by voice, and end with a transcript marker — Phase 1 (text, AD-913 → AD-919) + Phase 2 (meeting voice + avatars, AD-920 → AD-923) both done.

**AD-922 shipped (2026-06-07).** Captain voice input to the meeting — the third Phase-2 AD of the ad-hoc crew-collaboration epic. AD-921 made the crew SPEAK their replies; AD-922 lets the Captain TALK BACK: in an active meeting a push-to-talk control captures one VAD-bounded utterance via the existing offline STT, and the transcript is routed through the AD-914 GROUP fan-out (not the 1:1 path) — closing the two-way voice loop (Captain speaks → crew reply as text → AD-921 speaks the replies). A purely-additive FRONTEND AD: NO backend file touched, NO new pytest. The key reuse: `sendText` in `ProfileChatTab.tsx` ALREADY contains the AD-917 group-routing branch (≥2 crew → `POST /api/threads/{id}/messages` `role:"captain"` → renders `per_agent_replies` → `speakMeetingReplies`), so feeding it the transcript self-routes to the group — NO new dispatch branch, NO parallel POST path. Three pieces: (1) NEW pure-DI hook `ui/src/audio/useMeetingMic.ts` — click-to-arm `armTransformersStt` (the BF-301 STT; the deprecated `whisperStt` is avoided), a ONE-SHOT `onTransformersTranscript` listener torn down before submit on every exit path (BF-319: the per-agent mic and this mic share the global transcript set, so a straggler must not fan two submits), gate inputs read live via refs (BF-292) so `toggleCapture` stays reference-stable, opens NO new mic (the app-wide `startVoiceActivity` stream is only tapped), and honest-degrades to typing when STT/mic is unavailable or permission is denied (`getMicPermissionState`/`onMicPermissionState` reused). The ECHO GATE is `speaking` = `useMeetingVoice.speakingAgentId != null` (meeting-wide, NOT BF-300's host-filtered `ttsActiveRef` which misses non-host speakers) — the mic refuses to arm while ANY agent speaks, so the Captain's mic never transcribes the crew's TTS. (2) NEW thin presentational `ui/src/components/profile/MeetingMicButton.tsx` — a local inline mic SVG (HXI #3, no emoji, `Glyphs.tsx`/`Glyphs.test.tsx` untouched to avoid the AD-917/919 export-count gotcha): amber while capturing, dim idle, muted treatment while an agent speaks or the mic is unavailable. (3) EDIT `ProfileChatTab.tsx` — destructure `speakingAgentId` from the existing `useMeetingVoice`, mount `useMeetingMic({ meetingActive, speaking: speakingAgentId != null, submit: sendText })`, and render `MeetingMicButton` by the `MeetingView` mount, gated on `meetingActive && supported`. The conversation-mode (`conversationController` → `/api/agent/{id}/chat`) and IntentSurface wake-word paths are untouched (they stay 1:1 — no AD-705a regression). +17 Vitest (`useMeetingMic.test.tsx` 10, `MeetingMicButton.test.tsx` 6, `MeetingMic.routing.test.ts` 1 — STT/VAD/mic mocked, no real audio; the routing test mirrors the AD-917 `routeSend` branch and asserts the transcript hits `/api/threads/{id}/messages`, NOT `/api/agent/{id}/chat`). Full UI vitest green (1194 passed / 1 skipped, +17 vs AD-921's 1177); `npm run build` (tsc -b + vite) green. Implementation authored by the Builder subagent (source files + ProfileChatTab wiring) but completed by the orchestrator after the subagent crashed pre-test/pre-commit — the diff was reviewed, the three test files written, and both gates run before commit.

**AD-921 shipped (2026-06-07).** Sequenced meeting voice — the second Phase-2 AD of the ad-hoc crew-collaboration epic. AD-920 shipped a SILENT avatar gallery; AD-921 turns it into a voice "call": when a meeting is active, the AD-914 `per_agent_replies` (already in AD-915 facilitator order) are SPOKEN one at a time — each in its own per-agent voice (AD-718) so they sound distinct, with NO talk-over — and the speaking agent's gallery avatar lip-syncs (AD-721b). A purely-additive FRONTEND AD: NO backend file touched, NO new pytest. Three pieces, zero change to `voice.ts`/`useLipSyncCapture.ts`/`CrewVRM.tsx`/`MeetingView.tsx`. (1) NEW pure DI sequencer `ui/src/audio/meetingVoice.ts` — `speakRepliesSequentially(replies, deps)` loops the replies in ARRAY order (= facilitator order; `asyncio.gather` preserves input order at `thread_fanout.py:325`, so NO order metadata and NO backend change), awaiting each utterance before the next via `_speakAndWait`, which subscribes to `onSpeechEvent('end')` matched on `agent_id` (the BF-290 subscribe-before-speak one-shot) PLUS a ~20s safety timeout so a TTS-unavailable utterance drains the queue instead of deadlocking; `createVoiceProfileResolver` caches per-`agent_id` `VoiceProfile` over the same `GET /api/agent/{id}/profile` endpoint ProfileChatTab already uses for the host (Tier-2 fail → `undefined` = same-voice, never silence). (2) NEW thin hook `ui/src/audio/useMeetingVoice.ts` — injects the real `speakResponse`/`onSpeechEvent`/`stripMarkdownForSpeech`, self-gates on `meetingActive && voiceEnabled` (read live via `useStore.getState()`, BF-292), exposes `speakingAgentId` (the AD-923 indicator seam), and supersedes an in-flight batch via a generation token (no talk-over across Captain re-sends; a stale `onSpeakingChange` can't clobber the current speaker). (3) EDIT `ProfileChatTab.tsx` — mount the hook next to the AD-920 `meetingActive` selector; after the existing AD-917 text-render loop, call `speakMeetingReplies(replies)` (text render byte-identical; voice is additive + self-gating so non-meeting sends stay silent). **The speaking→lip-sync binding already existed:** passing the speaking `agent_id` to `speakResponse` fires `onSpeechEvent({agent_id})`, and only the `CrewVRM` whose `agentId` matches reacts (`CrewVRM.tsx:459`) — one speaker at a time means exactly one avatar animates; AD-921 builds NO lip-sync and does NOT change `speakResponse` (it stays fire-and-forget — sequencing comes from the `onSpeechEvent('end')` wrap, not a resolve-on-end return). Gate: focused vitest 18 (`meetingVoice.test.ts` 12 + `useMeetingVoice.test.tsx` 6, mock the audio layer per `ProfileChatTabVoice.test.tsx`, no-emoji guard each); full UI suite **1177 passed / 1 skipped** (+18 vs AD-920 1159/1); `npm run build` (tsc -b + vite) green. NO STT (AD-922), NO presence/transcript writeback (AD-923).

**AD-920 shipped (2026-06-07).** Meeting mode + avatar gallery — the FIRST Phase-2 AD of the ad-hoc crew-collaboration epic. "Start Meeting" promotes a Phase-1 group chat to a live *meeting* (a MODE of the thread): a toggle flips a persisted `metadata.meeting_active` flag, and an avatar **gallery** renders every crew participant's VRM avatar at once, bound to the AD-722b-4 fleet avatar-telemetry stream — the chat thread stays the transcript below. NO voice, NO STT, NO speaking/presence indicators (those are AD-921/922/923). **Backend — a scoped, additive meeting-state writer, NOT a generic metadata PATCH:** new `ChatThreadStore.set_meeting_active(thread_id, active)` does a `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` read-modify-write that MERGES the JSON `metadata` column (mirroring `set_title(lock=True)`) — `active=True` sets `meeting_active=True`, `active=False` deletes the key (clean "not in a meeting"), sibling keys (`created_by_agent` AD-918, `title_locked` AD-794) preserved — returning the updated `ChatThread` or `None`. A new `UpdateThreadRequest.meeting_active: bool | None` field plus an EARLY branch at the top of the `PATCH /api/threads/{id}` handler routes the flag to `set_meeting_active` and returns `to_dict()` (404 on a missing thread). Scoping the writer to one flag is Defense-in-Depth — a generic arbitrary-metadata PATCH would let a client clobber `created_by_agent`/`title_locked`. **Frontend:** `threadApi.setMeetingActive(threadId, active)` wraps `patchThread` with `{meeting_active}`; `GroupChatHeader` gains a Start/End Meeting toggle (local inline stroke-SVG video glyph — HXI #3, NOT a `Glyphs.tsx` export, so the `Glyphs.test.tsx` count is untouched; amber `#f0b060` when active, `aria-pressed` reflects the flag, shown when `crewParticipants.length >= 1`); a new `MeetingView.tsx` iterates `thread.participants` (crew only, excludes the `"captain"` sentinel), mounting `useFleetAvatarTelemetry` (the shared `setAvatarTelemetryFrame` sink, fan-out by `agent_id`) and rendering one `AvatarSlot` per participant — a `<Canvas><CrewVRM …/>` when the agent has a `vrm_url`, else an `AgentAvatarBadge` (pure `<span>`, no R3F) fallback triggered by absent appearance OR `CrewVRM.onLoadError`; per-avatar `AgentSignals` come from the existing `deriveAgentSignals` (v1 signal source — no telemetry-payload→signals converter, which is AD-921/923; the populated `avatarTelemetry` map is the forward binding). `ProfileChatTab` mounts `MeetingView` below `GroupChatHeader` via a reactive `meetingActive` selector. **VRM-absent is the CI/dev default:** `.vrm` binaries are operator-provided/gitignored, so the gallery renders all-badges in CI — the tests reflect that (R3F + `CrewVRM` + the fleet hook mocked per the `CrewAvatarPopout.test.tsx` precedent — no WebGL, no `.vrm`). **`tsc -b` caught type drift the editor missed:** `appearance` lives on `AgentProfileData`, NOT the store `Agent` (which also lacks `department`), so `MeetingView` reads appearance via a narrow runtime-field cast (the `GroupChatHeader` `department` precedent) and casts the `deriveAgentSignals` store arg exactly like `voice.ts:323` (`store as unknown as Parameters<typeof deriveAgentSignals>[1]`). +8 pytest (`tests/test_ad920_meeting_active.py`, real `ChatThreadStore` on `tmp_path` + real FastAPI router via `TestClient`, BF-287 — no MagicMock at the store boundary) + 17 Vitest across 3 files (`MeetingView.test.tsx` 8, `GroupChatHeader.meeting.test.tsx` 5 SEPARATE file, `threadApi.meeting.test.ts` 4; each with a no-emoji guard). Backend §1 gate `test_ad920 + test_ad791 + test_ad791a` green (43); focused Vitest green (17); full UI suite 1159 pass/1 skip (+17 vs the AD-919 baseline); `npm run build` (tsc -b + vite) green; thread/chat pytest blast radius green (468). NO voice/STT/presence/transcript-writeback, NO generic-metadata PATCH, NO `ParametricAvatar` in the gallery, NO new overlay/route, NO `Glyphs.tsx`/`CrewAvatarPopout`/`CognitiveCanvas`/`useFleetAvatarTelemetry`/`CrewVRM`/`avatarSignals` change, NO new config/agent/pool/consensus.

**AD-919 shipped (2026-06-07).** Group-chat visibility surface + Join — the final Phase-1 AD of the ad-hoc crew-collaboration epic. A focused, self-contained `GroupChatListPanel` (`ui/src/components/groupchat/GroupChatListPanel.tsx`), surfaced by a `TopNav` toggle (`group-chats-toggle`, the NotebooksPanel self-gate precedent — open flag + NavButton + unconditional mount), lists every *group* chat the Captain can see — agent-created (AD-918 `metadata.created_by_agent`) and Captain-built — badges the agent-created ones, renders participant avatars via `AgentAvatarBadge`, lets the Captain **Join** (AD-913 `add_participant`), and **opens** the existing AD-917 chat on click. **Decision A — focused panel, NOT the full LeftRail wire:** `LeftRail.tsx` (AD-719b) is built but not imported by `App.tsx`, has no group/`metadata`/Join concept (`LeftRailThread = {thread_id, title, is_dm?}`), and wiring it into the root flex is a layout reflow — so it stays forward marker **AD-719b-parent-wire** (default-flip **AD-719b-2**); AD-919 does not import or modify it. **Decision B — frontend-only:** `GET /api/threads` already returns `metadata`/`participants`/`title` via `to_dict()`, so no backend change and no pytest. **Decision C — group predicate (client-side):** a thread is a group chat when `metadata.created_by_agent` is truthy OR it has ≥2 crew participants (a `participants[]` id that is not the literal `"captain"` sentinel and resolves as `isCrew` in `useStore(s=>s.agents)`); 1:1 default threads are excluded. Join calls `addParticipant(threadId, "captain")` — the single `CAPTAIN_PARTICIPANT_ID` constant, the v1 sentinel consistent across AD-914 (crew gate excludes it) / AD-917 (header strips it) / era-4/5 (Captain `author_id="captain"`). **Decision D — open-on-click routes per-host:** `ProfileChatTab` resolves its thread from `threadIdByAgent.get(agentId)`, NOT the store's top-level `activeThreadId`, so opening = pick host = first crew participant, `setThreadForAgent(host, thread.id)` + `openAgentProfile(host)` (wiring the row to `setActiveThread` would be a silent no-op). **Decision E — glyphs:** reuse the existing `UserPlus` glyph + a local inline header glyph (LeftRail precedent), so `Glyphs.tsx`/`Glyphs.test.tsx` are untouched (no export-count bump). Un-joined agent-created chats sort to the top (HXI #9 alert-driven ordering); amber `#f0b060` accents, inline stroke-SVG only, no emoji (HXI #3). Additive `App.tsx`/store edits only (`groupChatListOpen` flag + `openGroupChatList`/`closeGroupChatList`, mirroring notebooks). **+10 Vitest** (`ui/src/components/groupchat/__tests__/GroupChatListPanel.test.tsx`, real store via `useStore.setState` + `vi.mock` of `threadApi` per BF-287, no-emoji guard): group-vs-1:1 filter, agent-created badge, avatar count, Join → `addParticipant(id,"captain")`, Join flips to "Joined", row-click → `threadIdByAgent`+`activeProfileAgent` change, host-open after Join, alert sort, self-gate-when-closed, no-emoji. Full UI suite 1142 pass / 1 skip; `npm run build` (tsc -b + vite) green. No meeting/voice/avatars (Phase 2, AD-920+); no `LeftRail.tsx`/backend/`Glyphs.tsx`/consensus change.

**AD-918 shipped (2026-06-07).** Agent-initiated group chats — a crew agent can now open + name a group chat on the AD-791 `ChatThreadStore` substrate, add crew collaborators, optionally link a work item, and optionally post the first message, via a governed `create_group_chat` intent (lifts the AD-719a-2 deferral on the ChatThreadStore substrate — NOT Ward Room). Mechanism (roadmap-prescribed): the intent is handled by a **bare-callable bus subscriber** (`IntentHandler` is a `Callable`; the `yeoman.py:242` non-agent-subscribe precedent) under a synthetic id `group_chat_coordinator` — NO new agent, NO pool, NO registry entry — backed by a new stateful `AgentGroupChatService` (`src/probos/threads/agent_group_chat.py`) where all create/validation/rate-limit logic lives (constructor-injected, real-fixture testable per BF-287; the AD-915 pure/impure split). This honors the uniform-transport invariant (Design Principle #10 — agent actions route through the bus, not a side-channel; the BF-265/267 lesson) over a bare `runtime.<svc>.create()` call. Crew-only (`is_crew_agent`, ontology read lazily via `ontology_provider` since `runtime.ontology` is set late); the creator is auto-added; other participants resolve by agent_id AND callsign (`CallsignRegistry.resolve`), non-crew refs dropped (Tier-2). Spam is bounded by a per-agent **cooldown** (`agent_create_cooldown_seconds=60.0`) + sliding-window **cap** (`agent_create_max_per_window=5` / `agent_create_window_seconds=3600.0`) on a long-lived `_create_times` dict (BF-163 + BF-257 shape) — three new `GroupChatConfig` fields; budget is consumed only AFTER the crew check, so non-crew rejections cost nothing. `requires_consensus=False` is deliberate and justified against the **Safety Budget** axiom: creating a chat is reversible (`delete_thread`) and low-risk, so the spam risk is handled by the rate limiter, NOT a quorum gate. Persistence: an additive optional `metadata` param on `ChatThreadStore.create_thread` (the INSERT now writes the existing AD-791a `metadata` column; NULL/`{}` read-identical, so existing callers are unaffected) tags `metadata.created_by_agent=<id>` so AD-919 can surface + Captain-join the room; `task_id` is passed through (optional caller param; live task-id auto-wiring deferred). **Loop-safe by construction:** the created thread has NO Captain post, so AD-914 `group_chat_fanout` (fires only on `role=="captain"`) never auto-runs on creation; the optional first message is a SINGLE persisted `role=="agent"` row that is NOT dispatched — no agent-to-agent reply loop exists in v1. The created thread is a normal `ChatThread`: AD-913 `add_participant`/`remove_participant` and AD-914 `crew_agent_participants` operate on it unchanged. No UI (AD-919), no Ward Room change, no new agent/pool, no fan-out change, no decomposer discovery (the `CREATE_GROUP_CHAT_DESCRIPTOR` is a forward marker only). +16 pytest (`tests/test_ad918_agent_initiated_group_chat.py`, BF-287 real `ChatThreadStore` + real `IntentBus(SignalManager(reap_interval=1.0))` + real-but-fake registry/callsign stubs + injectable fake clock + real `CallsignRegistry` for the callsign path); focused gate green (66 = 16 + AD-914 + AD-791 + AD-791a), `-k "thread or chat or fanout or group or intent"` blast-radius green (1005 passed, 4 skipped).

**AD-917 shipped (2026-06-07).** UI group-chat experience — the in-chat controls that make the AD-913/914/916 thread backend reachable from the HXI, with NO new backend (drives the shipped endpoints only). The 1:1 `ProfileChatTab` posted every Captain turn to the single-agent `POST /api/agent/{id}/chat`, which never fans out; AD-914 group fan-out fires ONLY on `POST /api/threads/{id}/messages` with `role=="captain"` AND ≥2 crew participants. Four focused, independently-testable pieces, no IntentSurface/Ward Room coupling: (1) a new HXI-compliant `UserPlus` glyph in `Glyphs.tsx` (inline stroke-SVG, `strokeWidth 1.5`, no fill, no emoji); (2) `addParticipant`/`removeParticipant` thread-API wrappers (`POST`/`DELETE /api/threads/{id}/participants`, Tier-2 honest-degrade to `null`); (3) `AddParticipantPopover.tsx` — a keyboard-navigable crew picker reusing the AD-719 picker's DATA SOURCE (`useStore((s) => s.agents)`, filtered `isCrew && callsign`, `department` via defensive cast) + `AgentAvatarBadge`, with arrow/Enter/Tab/Esc nav and a `scrollIntoView` guard for jsdom/embedded-webview parity; (4) `GroupChatHeader.tsx` — click-to-rename (`PATCH {title, title_locked:true}`), a crew-only participant avatar strip from `thread.participants`, hover-reveal remove-×, and the add-participant popover, all hydrating the `chatThreads` store on each mutation. The one non-trivial `ProfileChatTab` edit is the send-routing branch: when the active thread has ≥2 crew participants the Captain turn posts to `POST /api/threads/{id}/messages` (`author_id/role="captain"`, `body: text || '(attachment)'` — the `'(attachment)'` placeholder satisfies `AppendMessageRequest.body` `min_length=1` on attach-only sends, `attachment_ids`) and renders each `per_agent_replies` entry (callsign-prefixed); otherwise the 1:1 path stays byte-identical. The chat's OWN existing uploader (`POST /api/chat/attachments/multipart`, already sending `attachment_ids`) is reused — not a Ward Room component. +23 Vitest across 4 new files (`threadApi.participants` 5, `AddParticipantPopover` 6, `GroupChatHeader` 7, `ProfileChatTab.groupsend` 5; every component test carries a no-emoji guard); full UI suite 1132 passing / 1 skipped, `npm run build` (tsc -b + vite) green. No `DECISIONS.md` entry (UI wiring over shipped endpoints; no new architectural decision).

**AD-916 shipped (2026-06-07).** File sharing in ad-hoc group chats — a chat message now carries content-addressable attachment refs (AD-731 refs-not-blobs) persisted on `chat_thread_messages.metadata`, and a vision-capable agent participant receives image refs in its AD-914 fan-out prompt so it can actually see a shared image. Two seams, both additive and Tier-2 honest-degrade, with NO schema/protocol/config change. (1) `POST /api/threads/{id}/messages` gains an `attachment_ids: list[str]` field — SHA-256 refs of files already uploaded via the existing `POST /api/chat/attachments` endpoint (bytes are stored ONCE there; AD-916 only references them, mirroring `agents.py req.attachment_ids`, never re-validating). A new `resolve_attachment_refs(store, attachment_ids)` (in `thread_fanout.py`) resolves each SHA → `{content_hash, mime}` via `FilesystemAttachmentStore.mime_for` (an unknown SHA is skipped with a warning, never fabricated), and the append handler folds the resolved refs into `metadata.attachments` before `append_message` (attachments-disabled or any failure → plain message; the bytes were already persisted). (2) `group_chat_fanout` builds the AD-730/731 `vision_messages` array ONCE from the Captain message's `metadata.attachments` via a new `build_chat_vision_messages` that filters `image/*` mimes and reuses `build_multimodal_messages` (so the emitted blocks are the exact AD-731 `attachment_ref` shape the LLM client resolves from `runtime.attachment_store`); `_send_one` then sets `params["vision_messages"]` ONLY for `vision_capable=True` participants (gate via `callsign_registry.get_profile(agent_type).vision_capable`, mirroring the `agents.py` DM gate) — non-vision participants get byte-identical AD-914 text-only params. No image refs → `None` → AD-914 text-only fan-out (zero behavior change). Non-image attachments are stored + referenced + carried on the message (link-only; no PDF/doc text extraction in v1). Reuses `chat._get_attachment_store(runtime)` for byte-identical parity with the proven DM path; lazy imports avoid `chat.py`↔`threads.py`/`thread_fanout.py` cycles. +16 pytest (`tests/test_ad916_chat_file_sharing.py`, BF-287 real `FilesystemAttachmentStore` tmp dir + real `ChatThreadStore` + real `IntentBus`): store+ref roundtrip, ref persisted + survives `to_dict()`, vision participant receives the image ref in fan-out params, non-vision participant unaffected, non-image attachment stored+referenced, unknown-SHA skip. Focused gate green (70 = 16 + AD-914 15 + AD-915 21 + AD-791 18); `-k "thread or chat or fanout or attachment or vision"` blast-radius green (666).

**AD-915 shipped (2026-06-07).** Turn-taking facilitator for ad-hoc group chats — relevance-ranked speaking order + convergence-gated termination, turning AD-914's blind all-at-once fan-out into "the most relevant participants reply, in a sensible order, and the exchange stops when it converges." A new **pure, deterministic, LLM-free** value-class `src/probos/cognitive/chat_facilitator.py` (`SpeakerSignals`/`SpeakerScore`/`FacilitationResult`/`ChatFacilitator`, mirroring the AD-641c `ThreadPriorityScorer` pure pattern) ranks speakers by weighted factors (mention 0.40, recency 0.25, department 0.25, trust 0.10), hard-includes any @-addressed agent at the front (overriding both the truncation cap AND convergence), truncates NON-mentioned speakers to a cap, and suppresses non-mentioned speakers when the recent agent exchange has **converged** (mean pairwise Jaccard ≥ threshold across ≥`min_agents` distinct agents over ≥`min_messages` recent agent turns). Honest reuse-vs-new accounting: AD-641c scores **threads** not speakers (wrong shape) → NEW pure scorer that mirrors its pattern; the AD-583 convergence DETECTORS are substrate-coupled (`check_cross_agent_convergence` on `RecordsStore`, `ThreadEchoAnalyzer` on Ward Room posts) and detect pathological echo, not healthy agreement-termination → reuse only their atomic PURE primitive `probos.cognitive.similarity.jaccard_similarity` + `text_to_words` (threshold `0.6`, AD-614 precedent); `WardRoomRouter.check_and_increment_reply_cap` is a **phantom** (does not exist) and `max_thread_posts` is the wrong (Ward Room) substrate → the chat cap is a NEW `GroupChatConfig` field, **default 0 = off** so AD-914's all-at-once is preserved out of the box. The impure assembly helper `_assemble_speaker_signals` (in `thread_fanout.py`, Tier-2-guarded on every registry/callsign/ontology/trust lookup) builds the per-speaker snapshots from the runtime; the facilitator is wired **inside** `group_chat_fanout` between participant resolution and the `asyncio.gather`, so AD-914's `_send_one` dispatch+persist primitive is byte-identical — the facilitator only decides WHO/ORDER (the `gather` now iterates `speaking_order`; an empty order on a converged thread with no mention persists the Captain message but dispatches no agent). Single-read DRY: `prior` is fetched once and reused for history, recency, and the convergence window via an **additive optional** `prior=None` param on `_build_session_history` (the 4-arg AD-914 call stays byte-identical). All defaults make AD-914 inert (cap off; convergence cannot fire below `min_messages=4`/`min_agents=2`). +21 pytest (`tests/test_ad915_turn_taking_facilitator.py`, BF-287 real `ChatThreadStore` + real `IntentBus(SignalManager(reap_interval=1.0))` + real-but-fake registry/callsign/ontology — no MagicMock): ranking order, cap truncation, convergence suppression, @-mention always-included + override, assembly, and the AD-914 single-/converged-/mentioned-path integration invariants. Focused gate `test_ad915 + test_ad914 + test_ad913 + test_ad791` green (68); `-k "thread or chat or fanout or facilitat or convergence or priority"` blast-radius green (677).

**AD-914 shipped (2026-06-07).** Group-chat fan-out + cross-agent visibility on the AD-791 `ChatThreadStore` — the substrate form of the dormant `AD-719a-wire` marker. AD-913 made a thread's participant set mutable, but a multi-participant thread was still N blind 1:1s: no fan-out, and the only reply path injected only client-supplied history, so two agents in a thread never saw each other. AD-914 extends the existing `POST /api/threads/{id}/messages` handler so that when the author is the Captain (`role == "captain"`) **and** the thread has **≥2 crew-agent participants** (`is_crew_agent(registry.get(pid), ontology)` — the literal `"captain"` sentinel and any non-crew/unresolvable id are excluded), the turn fans out to every crew participant **in parallel** via the `direct_message` intent (`runtime.intent_bus.send` — targeted request/reply, **not** `broadcast`), and each reply is persisted as a `role="agent"` `chat_thread_messages` row (`metadata.fanout="ad914"`). Cross-agent visibility is the wire: a server-side `session_history` is built from the store — `list_messages(before=captain_msg.created_at, limit=1000)` then **tail-sliced `[-20:]`** (the store is `ORDER BY created_at ASC LIMIT ?`, so a bare limit returns the OLDEST N) — and injected into each agent's prompt as `{"role": <callsign|Captain|system>, "text": body}` entries (the `cognitive_agent.py` consumer reads the `text` key; agent rows labelled by **callsign** so agents are legible to each other). The Captain's current turn rides `params["text"]`, excluded from history; all agents in a turn receive the SAME snapshot (sequential turn-taking where B sees A's same-turn reply is AD-915). Boundary: fan the Captain seed out ONCE and STOP — no agent-to-agent auto-reply (AD-918), no turn-taking facilitator (AD-915). New focused module `src/probos/routers/thread_fanout.py` (`group_chat_fanout`/`crew_agent_participants`/`_build_session_history`); the chat.py AD-719 @-mention branch is untouched (a future AD-914a may unify them — forward marker only). Per-agent dispatch **and** the whole gate are Tier-2 log-and-degrade: one agent's failure (no subscriber → `(no response)`, raising handler → `(delivery failed)`) never blocks the others, and the gate runs `crew_agent_participants` inside the guard so even a minimal runtime without a `registry` degrades to a plain append. History window is a module constant (`_FANOUT_HISTORY_LIMIT=20`) — no new config field. +15 pytest (`tests/test_ad914_group_chat_fanout.py`, real `ChatThreadStore` + real `IntentBus(SignalManager(reap_interval=1.0))` + a real-but-fake subscribed handler that records the injected history, BF-287; no MagicMock at the store/bus boundary); focused gate `test_ad914 + test_ad913 + test_ad791` green (47), thread/chat/fanout/intent blast-radius green.

**AD-913 shipped (2026-06-07).** Chat-thread participant management — the substrate foundation for the ad-hoc group-chat epic ("start a 1:1, then add crew"; "the Captain joins an agent-created chat"). The AD-791 `ChatThreadStore` could create/get/update threads, set titles, and append messages, but had **no way to mutate the participant set of an existing thread**. Two synchronous, idempotent store methods — `add_participant(thread_id, agent_id)` and `remove_participant(thread_id, agent_id)` — perform a `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` read-modify-write of the JSON `participants` column (mirroring the `set_title(lock=True)` race-safe precedent), return the updated `ChatThread` (or `None` when the row is missing), and bump `last_active_at` **only on the mutating path** (re-adding a present member / removing an absent one is a true no-op — no write, no activity bump). Two REST routes ride the already-registered `threads` router: `POST /api/threads/{id}/participants` (body `{agent_id}` declared `agent_id: str = ""` so a missing OR empty value hits an explicit `400` rather than Pydantic's `422`) and `DELETE /api/threads/{id}/participants/{agent_id}` (path segment; absent-member removal is the 200 no-op case); both return `thread.to_dict()`, `404` on a missing thread, and `503` via `_get_store` when the store is absent. No `AgentRegistry` validation (pure list ops; a future AD may add it); `api.py` unchanged. +14 pytest (`tests/test_ad913_participant_management.py`, real `ChatThreadStore` on `tmp_path` per BF-287, deterministic monotonic clock for the two `last_active_at` assertions); focused gate `test_ad913 + test_ad791` green (32), thread/chat blast-radius green (416).

**BF-612 fixed (2026-06-07).** Empty-content LLM failures after long uptime — DMs to crew agents returned `(no reply — agent's LLM endpoint returned empty content …)` (the BF-289 surface) after ProbOS had been up ~11-18h under continuous proactive load; restarting the **proxy** fixed it. Live triage isolated the fault to the runtime, not the proxy: a fresh request to the Copilot proxy at `127.0.0.1:8080` returned real content even at full ~14k-char prompt size, while the same agent through the runtime's pooled `httpx.AsyncClient` got empty content. Mechanism: the LLM client holds one long-lived keep-alive `AsyncClient` per `base_url|format`; when the proxy rotates its upstream Copilot session in the background it keeps the TCP socket open, so the runtime reuses a connection bound to a dead upstream session and the proxy answers **HTTP 200 with empty content** — no transport error, so `raise_for_status()` passes and it surfaces as BF-289. Under continuous load the socket never idles past keep-alive expiry, so it is reused indefinitely; a proxy restart only "fixes" it by force-closing every socket. Fix reproduces that in-process: `OpenAICompatibleClient._build_client(tier)` (single construction source), `async _refresh_client(tier)` (close stale + rebuild, Tier-2 log-and-degrade), and an empty-content guard in the `call()` loop that recycles the socket and retries the same tier **once** (`_refreshed_tiers` set bounds it). OpenAI/proxy path only (Ollama excluded); tool-call replies (`content_blocks`) and healthy replies are untouched; a genuinely empty upstream still surfaces via BF-289 after the single retry. +12 pytest (`tests/test_bf612_empty_content_retry.py`, real `OpenAICompatibleClient` per BF-287); `test_llm_client` (35) + `test_ad835_tier_adaptation` (6) green. The restart-the-proxy ritual is no longer needed for the stale-socket failure mode.

**AD-912 shipped (2026-06-07).** Generalized 1:1 notebook persistence from Yeoman-only to **all crew agents**. AD-911 scoped both the teaching hook and the `DmReplyPipeline` persistence to `agent_type == "yeoman"`; the Captain noted that saving a note from a 1:1 conversation is a general crew capability (notebooks are already universal on the proactive / Ward-Room path — `extract_and_execute_notebooks` keys notes by callsign + department generically). Three changes removed the artificial scoping: (1) `CognitiveAgent._conversational_notebook_protocol` (base hook) now teaches the `[NOTEBOOK slug]…[/NOTEBOOK]` reply tag to **any** agent with a records store wired (honest-degrade to `""` without one, via `getattr(self, "_runtime", None)`; AD-592 confabulation gate intact); (2) `YeomanAgent` drops its now-redundant override and inherits the base hook (DRY); (3) `DmReplyPipeline.step_4i_notebook_parse` drops the `agent_type != "yeoman"` gate — it persists for any agent under its own callsign (AD-550 dedup), with a final **safety-net unwrap** so a raw block never leaks when persistence is unavailable. No new store, no consensus gate, `extract_and_execute_notebooks` unchanged. +11 pytest (`tests/test_ad912_crew_notebook_generalization.py`, real `RecordsStore`/`ProactiveCognitiveLoop`/`DmReplyContext` per BF-287); the AD-911 suite updated to match (the former non-Yeoman unwrap test now asserts persistence); `test_ad726_dm_reply_pipeline`, `test_ad845/869/870`, `test_bf599`, `test_ad550`, `test_yeoman_agent`, `test_proactive*` all green.

**AD-911 shipped (2026-06-06).** Yeoman notebook persistence from 1:1 Captain DMs. The Captain asked Yeo (the Yeoman, AD-766) in a 1:1 chat to "save this to your notebook"; Yeo emitted a `[NOTEBOOK spacex-ipo-trade-setup]…[/NOTEBOOK]` block and replied "Done — saved," but **nothing was persisted**. Root cause: a 1:1 Captain chat routes `agent_chat → direct_message → DmReplyPipeline`, whose post-LLM cleanup chain had **no notebook extractor** (only `[DM]`/`[MESH]`/`[CREATE_TASK]`). Notebook persistence (`records_store.write_notebook` + the AD-550 dedup gate) lived **only** on the proactive / Ward-Room path (`ProactiveLoop.extract_and_execute_actions`); the 1:1 system prompt even forbids structured tags, so Yeo's block was discarded as plain text — confabulation. Fix scoped to the Yeoman: (1) base hook `CognitiveAgent._conversational_notebook_protocol` (default `""`, Open/Closed) appended in the `is_conversation` branch; `YeomanAgent` overrides it to teach the `[NOTEBOOK slug]…[/NOTEBOOK]` reply tag, **honest-degrading to `""`** when `runtime._records_store` is absent (AD-592 lesson; tag text is `_CAPABILITY_GAP_RE`-clean). (2) `ProactiveLoop.extract_and_execute_notebooks(agent, text)` — a lean writer mirroring `extract_and_execute_dms` (parse → AD-550 dedup → `write_notebook` → strip markers), deliberately omitting the proactive path's AD-552/553/554/555 anti-flooding telemetry (that targets autonomous repetitive writes, not explicit Captain requests). (3) `DmReplyPipeline.step_4i_notebook_parse`, registered after `step_4b_dm_outbound_parse`: Yeo → persist; any other agent → unwrap stray markers without persisting. No new store, no consensus gate, proactive path untouched (zero regression there). +14 pytest (`tests/test_ad911_yeoman_notebook.py`, real `RecordsStore`/`ProactiveCognitiveLoop`/`DmReplyContext` per BF-287); `tests/test_ad726_dm_reply_pipeline.py`, `test_ad845`, `test_ad869`, `test_ad870`, `test_ad550_notebook_dedup.py` all green. When the Captain asks Yeo to save a note in 1:1 chat, it is now durably written to Ship's Records.

**AD-910 shipped (2026-06-06).** Yeoman personal standing orders + Bridge department assignment. Yeo (AD-766) had **no** personal standing orders — `config/standing_orders/` had no `yeoman.md`, so `compose_instructions` loaded only the federation + ship tiers for him. He was also unmapped in the legacy department fallback (`get_department("yeoman")` → `None`), so he missed the Bridge department tier (`bridge.md`) the Counselor inherits. Two additive config/data changes: (1) `config/standing_orders/yeoman.md` — Yeo's Personal Standing Orders modeled on `counselor.md` and aligned with the AD-766 `_ROLE_RULES` (act in the Captain's name for admin; triage scans to one digest; delegate via @-mention; maintain standing orders + surface conflicts; never tactical/financial/destructive without approval), with an explicit **Honesty About Your Capabilities** section ("Never claim to have done something you did not do") as the standing-order seam against confabulation (AD-911 is the code seam). (2) `_AGENT_DEPARTMENTS["yeoman"] = "bridge"` so Yeo inherits the Bridge protocols. Content/config only; the `YeomanAgent.department = "Bridge"` class attribute and the existing `counselor` mapping are unchanged. +6 pytest (`tests/test_ad910_yeoman_standing_orders.py`: file + header, compose includes personal orders + role phrase + honesty clause, `get_department("yeoman") == "bridge"`, Counselor mapping intact, unmapped agent → `None`).

**BF-611 fixed (2026-06-06).** Web search returned no results — `WebSearchAgent` (AD-248, `src/probos/agents/utility/web_agents.py`) handed its LLM the **raw, unparsed** DuckDuckGo HTML truncated at 8000 chars (`obs["fetched_content"] = body[:8000]`). The DDG page emits a large `<head>`/inline-CSS block, header banner, and search `<form>` *before* the first `<div class="result …">`, so the budget was spent on page chrome and the result `<div>`s were cut off — the LLM saw zero results. Yeo reported exactly this on a live SpaceX-IPO search ("HTML was cut off before the actual result entries rendered… I won't invent search results"). The sibling `PageReaderAgent` already strips tags *before* truncating; `WebSearchAgent` lacked the equivalent parse-before-truncate step (`http_fetch`'s 1 MiB cap was not involved). Fix: three module-level helpers — `_strip_tags` (tag strip + `html.unescape`), `_decode_ddg_url` (extracts the real target from DDG's `//duckduckgo.com/l/?uddg=…&rut=…` redirect), `_parse_ddg_results(body, max_results=10)` (regex over `result__a` titles + `result__snippet` blocks, paired by index) — plus `_format_ddg_results` rendering compact `Result N: / Title / URL / Snippet` blocks. `perceive` now sets `fetched_content` to the parsed result text (capped at 8000 chars of *results*, not chrome), honest-degrading to the tag-stripped body when nothing parses. +7 pytest (`TestBF611DdgParsing` in `tests/test_utility_agents.py`, real DDG fixture with 9000 chars of `<head>` ahead of the result divs); `tests/test_utility_agents.py` (70) and `tests/test_bf599_yeo_web_research_delegation.py` green.

**AD-902 shipped (2026-06-06).** Per-agent **developmental** (T3, proficiency-tracked) skill management surface — the gap BF-610 surfaced (cognitive T2 skills are rank/department-gated and not per-agent editable; developmental skills are acquired through `AgentSkillService` and had no management UI). Two thin additive layers over the AD-428 service, no new store and no consensus gate. **Backend (`src/probos/skill_framework.py`):** two `AgentSkillService` verbs — `suspend_skill(agent_id, skill_id, suspended=True)` soft-suspends/reinstates a held record (returns `None` when the agent does not hold the skill; preserves proficiency AND `assessment_history` across the DB round-trip, so a reinstate restores the full record rather than re-acquiring at FOLLOW), and `update_proficiency(agent_id, skill_id, new_level, source="assessment", notes="")` sets a new `ProficiencyLevel`, refreshes `last_exercised`, appends an `{timestamp, level, source, notes}` entry to `assessment_history`, and emits `SKILL_REGRESSION` on a downward move else `SKILL_EXERCISED`. **HTTP (`src/probos/routers/crew.py`, prefix `/api/crew`, router already registered):** four governed verbs over an agent's developmental record — `GET /{agent_id}/skills` (lists ALL records incl. suspended, each serialized with its definition `name`/`category`; honest-degrades to `{agent_id, skills: [], count: 0}` when the skill service is absent), `POST /{agent_id}/skills` (body `{skill_id, proficiency?, source?}` → `acquire_skill`; 503 no service / 400 missing `skill_id` / 404 unknown skill / 400 invalid proficiency / 400 with the `ValueError` prereq message `from None`; default proficiency FOLLOW, `source` defaults `"captain"`), `PATCH /{agent_id}/skills/{skill_id}` (body may carry `proficiency` → `update_proficiency(source="captain")` and/or `suspended` → `suspend_skill`; 503 / 400 neither field / 404 not held), and `DELETE /{agent_id}/skills/{skill_id}` (soft-suspend — `suspend_skill`, returns `{suspended: true, agent_id, skill_id}`; 503 / 404). **UI (`ui/src/components/personnel/SkillManagement.tsx`, mounted in the AD-897 Service Record `sr-section-skills`):** a `DEVELOPMENTAL SKILLS` panel keyed on `agentId` that lists the agent's records (`GET /api/crew/{id}/skills`), offers an acquire control bound to the skill catalog (`GET /api/skills/registry`, `POST` with proficiency FOLLOW, surfacing the server `detail` inline), a single-click level-**up** and a two-step confirm level-**down** (both `PATCH {proficiency}`, clamped 1..7), and a two-step confirm **suspend** (`DELETE`) with a one-click **reinstate** (`PATCH {suspended: false}`). Soft-suspend over hard delete (Reversibility Preference); crew-prefixed REST reusing `update_proficiency` (DRY); no consensus gate — a Captain-authority, fully reversible proficiency edit (Minimal Authority). Stroke-only SVG, amber accents, no emoji (HXI #3). **+8 pytest** (`tests/test_ad902_skill_management.py`, BF-287 real `AgentSkillService` + `SkillRegistry` on `tmp_path` driven through an in-process FastAPI `AsyncClient`) **+7 Vitest** (`ui/src/components/personnel/SkillManagement.test.tsx`: render, acquire POST, rejection-detail surface, level-up single-click PATCH, level-down two-step PATCH, suspend DELETE + reinstate PATCH, no-emoji guard). Full backend gate green (15669 passed, 22 skipped); the AD-897 (5) and full personnel Vitest suite (36) still green; `tsc --noEmit` clean. One commit.

**BF-610 fixed (2026-06-06).** Service Record "Skills 0" contradiction — the AD-897 **Skills & Proficiency** section showed a single unlabeled `Skills` count from `rec.skill_count` directly above the cognitive-skill list, so Yeoman's record read `Skills 0` over four populated cognitive skills. Root cause: the count is the **developmental** (T3, proficiency-tracked, acquired via commissioning/training — Yeo has none) skill total, while the list is **cognitive** (T2, `SKILL.md` instruction-knowledge, rank/department-gated) skills with their own `cognitive_skill_count`; the UI conflated the two. Fix relabels the count `Developmental skills` and adds a `COGNITIVE SKILLS (N)` subheader (`sr-cognitive-skills-header`, `N = rec.cognitive_skill_count ?? cognitiveSkills.length`) above the list, so each count matches what it labels. Presentation-only — no backend touched (the ACM already supplied both fields). AD-897 Vitest seeds `cognitive_skill_count` and asserts both labels (5 green). Surfaced the related gap — there is no per-agent skill *management* surface yet — scoped as the AD-902 proposal.

**AD-899 shipped (2026-06-06; Wave 258; #863).** Eleventh and final AD of the Crew Personnel Management epic (#855-865) — the tool certification management view, the asset/qualification surface over the AD-894 governed endpoints. A new `ui/src/components/personnel/ToolCertifications.tsx` is framed as PQS-style qualification (the asset-management counterpart to the personnel record): it browses the ship-wide tool catalog (`GET /api/tools`, honest-degrades to empty), carries a self-contained crew selector bound to `GET /api/crew/roster`, and on selection fetches that agent's active certifications (`GET /api/crew/{id}/tools`, honest-degrades to empty). The Captain gets two governed verbs — **grant** (a `tool-grant-form`: a catalog tool select, an `observe`/`read`/`write`/`full` permission select, an optional reason; client-validates that a crew member and a tool are chosen before any request; `POST /api/crew/{id}/tools`, surfacing the server `detail` inline as `tool-grant-error`) and **revoke** (a two-step `Revoke` → `Confirm revoke` red affordance, `DELETE /api/crew/{id}/tools/{grant_id}`, surfacing the server `detail` as `tool-row-error`). Each certification renders its `tool_id`, a permission chip, and — when `is_restriction` — a red `RESTRICTION` flag (the AD-894 store carries both elevations and reductions). The view mounts in the console behind a **third** header view-switcher tab (`personnel-tab-tools` 'Tool Certs'; the `view` type becomes `roster`|`skills`|`tools`) in its own `personnel-tools-pane`, defaulting to `roster` so every AD-896/AD-897 master-detail testid and behavior renders unchanged. Thin UI over the governed AD-894 endpoints — no new consensus gate; grant/revoke are audited Captain-authorized `ToolAccessGrant` edits and the authority + audit model stays in the backend. Stroke-bordered chip buttons, amber accents, no emoji (HXI #3). **+6 Vitest** (`ui/src/components/personnel/ToolCertifications.test.tsx`: catalog renders, grant POSTs with body, grant validates a missing tool before POSTing, revoke requires a two-step confirm before DELETE, a rejected revoke surfaces the server error inline, no-emoji guard); the AD-896 (8) suite still green; `tsc --noEmit` clean. **Epic complete: eleven ADs (891-901), all committed in build order, none pushed.**

**AD-898 shipped (2026-06-06; Wave 257; #862).** Tenth AD of the Crew Personnel Management epic (#855-865) — the Skill Library management view, the admin console over the AD-895 governed skill-definition endpoints. A new `ui/src/components/personnel/SkillLibrary.tsx` browses the definition list (`GET /api/skills/definitions`, honest-degrades to an empty list, optional `?category=` filter from a `pcc`/`role`/`acquired` select), and gives the Captain four governed verbs: **create** (a `skill-new` form — `skill_id`, name, category select, description, domain, comma-separated prerequisites, decay-rate — `POST /api/skills/definitions`), **edit** (the same form pre-filled, `skill_id` locked, `PUT /api/skills/definitions/{id}`), and **retire** (a two-step `Retire` → `Confirm retire` red affordance, `DELETE /api/skills/definitions/{id}`). Client-side validation blocks a submit with a missing name (or missing `skill_id` on create) before any request fires; the AD-895 server-side guards — duplicate `skill_id`, dangling prerequisite, built-in PCC protected, in-active-use protected — are surfaced **inline** (the create/edit `skill-form-error`, the retire `skill-row-error`) rather than swallowed. Built-in definitions carry a `BUILT-IN` marker. The view is mounted **in the console** behind a new header view-switcher (`personnel-view-tabs`: Roster ↔ Skill Library) that defaults to `roster`, so every AD-896/AD-897 master-detail testid and behavior renders unchanged on the default view; the Skill Library lives in its own `personnel-skills-pane`. Thin UI over the governed endpoints — no new consensus gate; the validation and Minimal-Authority protection model stays behind the AD-895 HTTP surface. Stroke-bordered chip buttons, amber accents, no emoji (HXI #3). **+5 Vitest** (`ui/src/components/personnel/SkillLibrary.test.tsx`: list renders, create form validates a missing name before POSTing, retire requires a two-step confirm before DELETE, an in-use retire surfaces the server protection error inline, no-emoji guard); the AD-897 (5) and AD-896 (8) suites still green; `tsc --noEmit` clean.

**AD-901 shipped (2026-06-06; Wave 256; #865).** Ninth AD of the Crew Personnel Management epic (#855-865) — the governed edit surface for an agent's standing orders, rendered inside the Service Record (AD-897) Standing Orders section. A new `ui/src/components/personnel/StandingOrders.tsx` extends that section into a **two-part view**: (1) the read-only four-tier composed orders (AD-893, unchanged context — the same `sr-order-tier-{tier}` rows, moved out of `ServiceRecord` and passed down as a `tiers` prop), and (2) a **Directives panel** bound to AD-900. The panel fetches `GET /api/crew/{id}/directives` (honest-degrades to an empty list on a non-ok or thrown response), lists the active + pending-approval directives, lets the Captain **issue** a new order (`content` textarea + `priority`, `POST /api/crew/{id}/directives`, surfacing the authorization `detail` as an inline error on a 400), **approve** a pending directive (`POST /api/crew/directives/{id}/approve` — the governed approval gate made visible), and **revoke** with a two-step confirm (first click reveals "Confirm revoke", second `DELETE /api/crew/directives/{id}`). Pending-approval directives are visually distinct (amber border/tint + an "AWAITING APPROVAL" badge and an Approve affordance); active directives carry a teal accent. Read/write over the **existing** governed path — no new consensus gate; authorization and the approval state machine remain in the AD-900 endpoints. Stroke-bordered chip buttons, amber accents, no emoji (HXI #3). `ServiceRecord` now renders `<StandingOrders agentId tiers={tiers} />` inside its `sr-section-orders` (the AD-897 tier testids are preserved). **+5 Vitest** (`ui/src/components/personnel/StandingOrders.test.tsx`: tiers render, issue POSTs with body, pending directive exposes an approve affordance that POSTs approve, revoke is two-step before DELETE, no-emoji guard); the AD-897 (5) and AD-896 (8) suites still green; `tsc --noEmit` clean.

**AD-897 shipped (2026-06-06; Wave 255; #861).** Eighth AD of the Crew Personnel Management epic (#855-865) — the Service Record detail view (the Workday-style ESR), filling the placeholder pane the AD-896 console shell left for it. A new read-only `ui/src/components/personnel/ServiceRecord.tsx`, mounted into the Crew Personnel Console detail pane and keyed on the selected `agent_id`, fetches three backend reads in parallel: `GET /api/crew/{id}/record` (the ACM consolidated profile + the `billet` block + `active_assignments`), `GET /api/crew/{id}/standing-orders` (the four order tiers), and `GET /api/crew/{id}/tools` (certified tool grants). It is sectioned for progressive disclosure: **Identity & Role** (callsign, post/billet title, department, rank, lifecycle, Big-Five personality bars), **Skills & Proficiency** (skill count, avg-proficiency bar, cognitive-skill list), **Qualifications** — showing **both** qualification homes, the certified tools (AD-894 grants, restrictions flagged red) AND the billet qualification standing (qualified / missing tests, AD-595d), **Duties & Active Assignments** (the configured watch-bill duties + in-flight work items), **Standing Orders** (the four tiers, present/absent rendered distinctly), and **Experience** (trust, earned agency, episode count). Every fetch honest-degrades independently (a `safeJson` helper returns `null` on a non-ok or thrown response; the record is accepted only when it carries an `agent_id` facet, so a roster-shaped or null body falls back to the roster `summary` props), and every empty facet renders a calm empty state ("No standing duties.", "No active assignments.", "No tool certifications.", "No standing orders."). Read-only by design — the grant/revoke and skill-edit actions live in AD-898/899; the AD-896 placeholder-text assertion was updated to assert the `service-record` view mounts. **+5 Vitest** (`ui/src/components/personnel/ServiceRecord.test.tsx`: full-record section render, empty-facet degradation, all-fetch-failure summary fallback, fully-qualified billet, no-emoji guard); UI `tsc --noEmit` clean.

**AD-896 shipped (2026-06-06; Wave 254; #860).** Seventh AD of the Crew Personnel Management epic (#855-865) and the **first UI AD** — the Crew Personnel Console shell (the "Ship's Office"). A *separate experience*, not another profile tab: a resizable, draggable, dockable window (`ui/src/components/personnel/CrewPersonnelConsole.tsx`) that mirrors the AD-837 Ward Room three-mode display system (`docked` ↔ `floating` ↔ `maximized`) with its own independent `localStorage`-persisted geometry under `probos.personnel.*` keys. **Store slice (`ui/src/store/useStore.ts`):** four new fields — `personnelConsoleOpen`, `personnelDisplayMode` (`WardRoomDisplayMode`), `personnelWindowRect` (`WardRoomWindowRect`) — seeded from a new exported `loadPersonnelLayout()` (defaults `mode='floating'`, rect `{x:120,y:80,w:880,h:660}`; log-and-degrade `try/catch` rehydration that falls back on malformed JSON), with `persistPersonnelMode`/`persistPersonnelRect` writers and four actions (`openPersonnelConsole`, `closePersonnelConsole`, `setPersonnelDisplayMode`, `setPersonnelWindowRect`) shaped exactly like the WardRoom precedent. **Component:** master-detail layout — the left master pane is the crew roster bound to `GET /api/crew/roster`, fetched in an `open`-gated `useEffect` into component-local state (roster list + `selectedAgentId` stay component-local; AD-897 receives the selection), department-grouped with rank badges reusing the CrewRosterPanel `DEPT_COLORS`/`RANK_LABELS` conventions; selecting an agent populates the right detail pane, which is a deliberate **placeholder** (`data-testid="personnel-record-pane"`) that AD-897 (Service Record detail) fills in. Header chrome carries Dock/Undock, Maximize/Restore (hidden when docked), and Close stroke-SVG glyphs (`Glyphs.tsx`, amber active `#f0b060`); floating mode gets a drag handle (header `mousedown`) and a bottom-right resize handle, both with the WardRoom add-on-mousedown/remove-on-mouseup listener discipline and min-size clamps (480×360). Fetch failure honest-degrades to an empty roster ("No crew aboard.") rather than crashing the shell. A `PERSONNEL` `NavButton` (testId `personnel-toggle`) is added to the App TopNav People group and `<CrewPersonnelConsole />` is mounted in the App return alongside `<WardRoomPanel />`/`<CrewRosterPanel />`. Read-only against the backend; no consensus gate (Minimal Authority — a window shell that reads the roster). +8 Vitest (`ui/src/components/personnel/CrewPersonnelConsole.test.tsx`: roster render from the API, selection loads the detail pane, default floating geometry, Dock toggle + maximize-hidden-when-docked, mode persistence to `probos.personnel.mode`, `loadPersonnelLayout` rehydrate/malformed-fallback, stroke-SVG no-emoji guard, fetch-failure empty-roster degrade — `global.fetch` stub + real store state per BF-287). UI typecheck (`tsc --noEmit`) clean.

**AD-900 shipped (2026-06-06; Wave 253; #864).** Sixth AD of the Crew Personnel Management epic (#855-865) — the governed directive authoring surface. The `DirectiveStore` (AD-386, `runtime.directive_store`) is the evolvable runtime overlay that `compose_instructions` merges as tier 6 on top of the immutable four-tier `.md` standing-order files; it was already writable but only through the `/order` and `/directives` shell CLI, so a Captain could not issue, approve, revoke, or amend a directive from the personnel console. AD-900 adds a thin HTTP surface over the **existing** governed write path — the chain-of-command authorization model (`authorize_directive`) and the approval state machine (`create_directive` → ACTIVE for Captain `CAPTAIN_ORDER`s, PENDING_APPROVAL for lower-authority directives) are NOT bypassed or rebuilt. Six endpoints on `routers/crew.py` (prefix `/api/crew`): (1) `GET /{agent_id}/directives` resolves the agent's `agent_type` via the registry (404 unknown), returns every non-inactive directive targeting that type or the `"*"` broadcast (`store.all_directives(include_inactive=False)` filtered), including PENDING_APPROVAL items so the approval queue is visible; honest-degrades to `{directives: [], count: 0}` when the store is absent. (2) `POST /{agent_id}/directives` (body `{content, priority?}`) issues a `CAPTAIN_ORDER` at `Rank.SENIOR`, resolving the target department via `runtime.ontology.get_agent_department` falling back to `standing_orders.get_department`; 503 no store, 404 unknown agent, 400 missing content, 400 with the authorization `reason` (e.g. a duplicate order) when `create_directive` returns `None`. (3) `POST /directives/{id}/approve` promotes a PENDING_APPROVAL directive to ACTIVE (`store.approve`, 404 unknown/not-pending). (4) `DELETE /directives/{id}` soft-revokes (`store.revoke(revoked_by="captain")`, 404 unknown). (5) `PATCH /directives/{id}` (body `{content}`) FRAGO-amends content in place (`store.amend`, 400 missing content, 404 unknown/not-amendable). Mirroring the `/order` CLI, every mutation calls `standing_orders.clear_cache()` to invalidate the composed-instruction cache. A `_serialize_directive` helper projects `RuntimeDirective` to the personnel-record shape `{id, directive_type, content, status, priority, issued_by, target_department}`. **Governance:** no new consensus gate — Minimal Authority is enforced by the pre-existing `authorize_directive` (the gate, unchanged); Captain `CAPTAIN_ORDER`→ACTIVE is existing policy; every mutation is reversible (revoke/amend) and audited (the store records `issued_by`/`revoked_by`). +18 pytest (`tests/test_ad900_directive_api.py`: captain-order lands ACTIVE and lists, unknown-agent 404, no-store honest-degrade, `"*"` broadcast visible, issue invokes `clear_cache`, missing-content 400, unknown-agent issue 404, no-store 503, duplicate returns the `"Duplicate"` reason, approve pending→ACTIVE + `clear_cache`, approve unknown 404, approve no-store 503, revoke disappears + `clear_cache`, revoke unknown 404, amend updates content + `clear_cache`, amend missing-content 400, amend unknown 404 — driven against a **real** tmp-file `DirectiveStore` via `httpx.ASGITransport`/`AsyncClient` so the sync-`sqlite3` store and the async endpoints share one thread per BF-287; no MagicMock at the substrate boundary). `directive_store.py`, `standing_orders.py`, the order `.md` files, and `authorize_directive` are untouched.

**AD-895 shipped (2026-06-06; Wave 252; #859).** Fifth AD of the Crew Personnel Management epic (#855-865) — the writable skill-definition library, given a delete verb and an HTTP surface. The skill-definition library is `runtime.skill_registry` (`SkillRegistry`, SQLite-backed, cloud-ready via `connection_factory`) — already writable through `register_skill`, but it had **no delete verb** and **no HTTP read/write surface**, so a Captain could not retire a skill or author a new one from the personnel console. AD-895 adds both without building a parallel store (the ontology role-template path `config/ontology/skills.yaml` via `ontology/loader.py` is a different subsystem and is left untouched). **Registry verbs:** (1) `update_skill_definition(defn, *, create=False)` — a validated wrapper over `register_skill`: `create=True` rejects a duplicate `skill_id`, every prerequisite must reference an already-registered skill (a dangling prerequisite, or a self-referential one, is rejected so the prereq DAG never points at a missing node), then it persists and returns the definition. (2) `delete_skill_definition(skill_id, *, skill_service=None)` — two Minimal-Authority guards: a **built-in PCC** (seeded from `BUILTIN_PCCS`) is never deletable (every crew member is commissioned with it), and a skill **in active use** by any agent (`skill_service.count_agents_with_skill(skill_id) > 0`) is refused so a delete cannot orphan acquired records; on success it pops the cache and `DELETE`s from the store. A new `AgentSkillService.count_agents_with_skill(skill_id)` (`SELECT COUNT(*) FROM agent_skills WHERE skill_id = ?`, 0 when no backing store) supplies the in-use signal. **HTTP surface (`routers/skills.py`, prefix `/api/skills`):** `GET /api/skills/definitions` (optional `category`/`domain` filter; honest-degrades to `{"definitions": [], "count": 0}` when the registry is absent; 400 on an invalid category), `POST /api/skills/definitions` (builds a `SkillDefinition` from the body, 503 no registry, 400 missing `skill_id`/`name`/`category` or invalid category, 400 on duplicate/dangling `from None`, `create=True`), `PUT /api/skills/definitions/{skill_id}` (404 when `get_skill` is None, `create=False`), `DELETE /api/skills/definitions/{skill_id}` (404 when unknown, 400 `from None` on the built-in/in-use guards). The per-agent skill-record endpoints (assess/exercise/commission), the AD-887 unified-profile path, the `SkillDefinition` field shape, and `register_skill`/`start`/`register_builtins` are all untouched. No consensus gate — skill-library authoring is a reversible Captain-authority edit and the validation guards are the safety mechanism (Minimal-Authority axiom; recorded in DECISIONS.md). +18 pytest (`tests/test_ad895_skill_library_crud.py`: create/list round-trip, duplicate rejected, update existing, dangling-prereq rejected, delete round-trip, built-in PCC protected, delete-in-use rejected, persistence survives reload, no-store count → 0, plus nine HTTP boundary cases — real `SkillRegistry`/`AgentSkillService` on `tmp_path`, TestClient driven through a FastAPI lifespan so the aiosqlite connections share the request event loop per the AD-894 cross-loop lesson and BF-287).

**AD-894 shipped (2026-06-06; Wave 251; #858).** Fourth AD of the Crew Personnel Management epic (#855-865) — the tool asset catalog plus the per-agent certification (privilege-grant) surface. The tool registry (`ToolRegistry`, AD-422) and the additive CRUD+O permission store (`ToolPermissionStore`, AD-423b) already existed and were wired onto the runtime (`runtime.tool_registry`, `runtime.tool_permission_store`), but neither had an HTTP read/write surface for the personnel console, so a Captain could not see the ship's tool inventory or certify a crew member on a tool from the manning view. AD-894 adds both. **Router split (design decision):** the ship-wide catalog `GET /api/tools` cannot live under the `/api/crew` prefix (it is ship-scoped, not crew-scoped, and a FastAPI prefix applies to every route in a router), so a new `src/probos/routers/tools.py` (prefix `/api/tools`, registered in `api.py` alongside `crew_router`) hosts `list_tool_catalog` — honest-degrades to `{"tools": [], "count": 0}` when the registry is absent, else returns `{tools: [reg.to_dict() …], count}` over `registry.list_tools()`. The three per-agent certification endpoints are personnel-record facets and live in `crew.py`: (1) `GET /api/crew/{agent_id}/tools` lists the agent's active grants (`tool_permission_store.get_active_grants_sync(agent_id)`), each joined with its catalog metadata (`registry.get(grant.tool_id).to_dict()`, or `tool: null` when the tool is no longer registered), shaped `{grant_id, tool_id, permission, is_restriction, reason, issued_by, issued_at, tool}`; honest-degrades to an empty list when the store is absent. (2) `POST /api/crew/{agent_id}/tools` certifies the agent: 503 when the store is absent, 400 on missing `tool_id`/`permission`, 404 when the tool is not registered, 400 on an invalid `ToolPermission` value (`raise … from None`), else `await tool_permission_store.issue_grant(agent_id, tool_id, perm, reason=…, issued_by="captain")` and returns the grant summary. (3) `DELETE /api/crew/{agent_id}/tools/{grant_id}` revokes: 503 when absent, `await revoke_grant(grant_id)`, 404 when the grant is unknown (real-DB rowcount path), else `{revoked: true, grant_id, agent_id}`. The grant/revoke pair is a Captain-authority privilege edit gated by the Minimal-Authority axiom (no consensus quorum) and fully auditable via the `ToolAccessGrant` record (reversible). Neither `ToolRegistry` nor `ToolPermissionStore` is modified — only read/wrapped. +11 pytest (`tests/test_ad894_tool_cert_api.py`, BF-287 real fixtures: real `ToolRegistry` with `_StubTool` Tool-protocol stubs and a real tmp-file `ToolPermissionStore` whose lifecycle runs in the `TestClient` loop via a lifespan handler — catalog list + empty-when-no-registry, certifications empty/no-store/joined-with-metadata, grant happy-path/unknown-tool-404/missing-fields-400/invalid-permission-400/no-store-503, revoke happy-path-then-disappears/unknown-404).

**AD-893 shipped (2026-06-06; Wave 250; #857).** Third AD of the Crew Personnel Management epic (#855-865) — the read-only standing-orders surface for the service record. `compose_instructions` (`src/probos/cognitive/standing_orders.py`) only returns ONE merged system-prompt string (`## `-prefixed sections + identity/personality), but the Service Record (AD-897) renders the four tiers as **distinct** sections, so a separate tier reader was needed. AD-893 adds a public `get_order_tiers(agent_type, *, orders_dir=None) -> list[dict]` that reads the four tier files directly via the existing private `_load_file` + `get_department`, returning each as `{tier, source_file, present, text}` for tiers `federation` / `ship` / `department` / `agent`. It runs **no** LLM composition and injects **no** personality block — the orders, not the assembled prompt. A tier whose file is absent (including the department tier when the agent has no department, where `source_file` is None) is reported with `present: False` and empty `text` rather than omitted, so the manning view always shows the full four-tier shape. The private `_load_file`/`_DEFAULT_ORDERS_DIR` helpers are NOT widened — one public function uses them. A new `GET /api/crew/{agent_id}/standing-orders` (`routers/crew.py`) resolves the agent_type via `runtime.registry.get(agent_id)` (404 when unknown), calls `get_order_tiers`, and returns `{agent_id, agent_type, tiers}`. Read-only, no consensus gate. `compose_instructions` and the order `.md` files are untouched. +6 pytest (`tests/test_ad893_standing_orders_api.py`: four named tiers in order, missing agent file → present false, unmapped department → source_file None / present false, all-absent four-tier shape preserved, endpoint four tiers for known agent, endpoint 404 for unknown — tier reader against `tmp_path`, endpoint against real `AgentRegistry` per BF-287).

**AD-892 shipped (2026-06-06; Wave 249; #856).** Second AD of the Crew Personnel Management epic (#855-865) — the HTTP read surface for the crew roster and the per-agent service record. A new router `src/probos/routers/crew.py` (prefix `/api/crew`, distinct from the existing `crew_tasks` router at `/api/crew-tasks`) exposes two read-only endpoints, wired into `api.py` (added `crew as crew_router` to the import tuple + the include loop). (1) `GET /api/crew/roster` (async): walks `runtime.registry.all()`, keeps crew agents via `crew_utils.is_crew_agent(agent, ontology)`, and joins each against the ontology org-chart facets from `ontology.get_crew_manifest(trust_network=..., callsign_registry=...)` keyed by `agent_type`. A manifest-present agent carries `callsign/post/department/rank` + `assigned: true`, `billet_state: "billeted"`; a crew-recognized agent the manifest does **not** bill surfaces as `post/department/rank: null`, `assigned: false`, `billet_state: "unbilleted"` — a visible manning gap (callsign falls back to `agent.callsign`). Each entry is augmented with `lifecycle_state` (`(await acm.get_lifecycle_state(id)).value`), `skill_count` (`len((await skill_service.get_profile(id)).all_skills)`), and `tool_count` (non-restriction active grants via `tool_permission_store.get_active_grants_sync`) — each in its own Tier-2 debug-degrade guard. Entries sort by `(department, agent_type)`. None registry → `{"crew": [], "count": 0}`.
