(2) `GET /api/crew/{agent_id}/record` (async): 503 when `acm` is absent, 404 when `registry.get(agent_id)` is None; otherwise returns `await acm.get_consolidated_profile(agent_id, runtime)` augmented with `active_assignments` (from `await work_item_store.list_work_items(assigned_to=id, limit=100)` filtered to `open`/`in_progress`, shaped `{id,title,work_type,status,priority}`) and a `billet` block (resolve the agent_type's `assignment.post_id`, `billet_registry.resolve(post_id)` for title/department, `await billet_registry.check_qualifications(post_id, agent_type, id)` for `qualified` + `missing_qualifications`) — omitted via honest-degrade when the agent has no assignment. Both endpoints document in the module docstring that the roster is registry-authoritative for who-is-aboard + ontology-authoritative for the org chart, the record is ACM-authoritative for the HR view, and the two are **not** reconciled. No consensus gate (read-only). +9 pytest (`tests/test_ad892_crew_record_api.py`, real `AgentRegistry` substrate + explicit hand-written ontology/ACM fakes per BF-287; covers billeted-facet wrap, lifecycle/skill/tool counts, the unbilleted manning-gap branch, non-crew exclusion, empty-registry, record happy-path with assignment+billet, no-assignment billet omission, 404, 503).

**AD-891 shipped (2026-06-06; Wave 248; #855).** First AD of the Crew Personnel Management epic (#855-865) — the duty-schedule lens. The ACM consolidated profile (AD-885) already surfaced role, skills, cognitive skills, and tool grants, but a crew agent's **configured duty schedule** was invisible to the personnel record — AD-885/AD-889 both noted "NO duties step" because no *public, non-mutating* per-agent duty accessor existed (the proactive loop's `_duty_tracker` was private and only exposed the volatile "due now" view via `get_due_duties`, which mutates execution status). AD-891 closes that seam with three additive pieces. (1) `DutyScheduleTracker.list_duties_for_agent(agent_type) -> list[DutyDefinition]` (`src/probos/duty_schedule.py`): returns the **configured** duties for an agent type sorted by `priority` descending — a stable personnel-record view that **never reads or writes execution status** (contrast `get_due_duties`, which records executions). (2) A public `ProactiveCognitionLoop.duty_tracker` property (`src/probos/proactive.py`) returning `self._duty_tracker` (None when proactive cognition is disabled) so callers never reach the private attr (Law of Demeter). (3) Runtime wiring in `startup/finalize.py` — after the duty-schedule setup block, `runtime.duty_schedule_tracker = proactive_loop.duty_tracker` runs unconditionally (None when disabled). The ACM lens gains **block 9**: guarded by `getattr(runtime, 'duty_schedule_tracker', None)`, it resolves the agent's `agent_type` via `runtime.registry.get(agent_id)` and, when present, sets `profile["duties"]` (list of `{duty_id, description, cron, interval_seconds, priority}`) + `profile["duty_count"]`. Honest-degrade throughout (Tier-2 `logger.debug(..., exc_info=True)` matching blocks 5-8): no tracker → block omitted; unknown agent (no `agent_type`) → block omitted; unconfigured agent type → empty list, count 0. No consensus gate (read-only lens). +9 pytest (`tests/test_ad891_duty_lens.py`, BF-287 real fixtures — real `AgentCapitalService` + real `DutyScheduleTracker` from real `DutyDefinition` config objects, no MagicMock at the substrate boundary): tracker sort-by-priority; unconfigured type → empty; non-mutating (listing never flips due-state or touches `_status`); lens present/absent/empty/unknown-agent. Gate green (`tests/test_ad891_duty_lens.py` + AD-885 blast-radius `tests/test_ad885_acm_unified_lens.py`, 15 passed serially). One commit.

**AD-890 shipped (2026-06-06; Wave 247; #854).** Final AD of the Skills & Tools Unification epic (#849-854) — retires the stale capability docs so no future reader is misled. **Docs-only, no code.** `docs/development/unified-tool-layer.md` titled itself "AD-543: Unified Tool Layer," but AD-543 is a **number collision**: AD-543 shipped as the Native SWE Harness Tool Execution Abstraction (`src/probos/tools/executor.py`), not that doc's skill→tool design — which actually landed as AD-888 (`resolve_tools_for_skill`) and was wired end-to-end by the AD-889 commissioning capstone. AD-890 (1) prepends a **SUPERSEDED** banner to `unified-tool-layer.md` stating the AD-543 collision, redirecting readers to the canonical `docs/development/skills-and-tools-architecture.md`, and noting the design landed as AD-888/AD-889; (2) adds a one-line "Canonical: see skills-and-tools-architecture.md" pointer atop `docs/research/crew-capability-architecture.md`; (3) confirms the AD-543 collision fact is already recorded in the canonical doc's seam table (#6 row) and §8 convergence sequence (item 6) — verify-only, no edit. No source, test, or other doc touched. Full serial gate green. One commit. **Epic closed (#849-854): AD-885 (ACM single lens) → AD-886 (designed skill→Tool) → AD-887 (skill-library merge) → AD-888 (skill→tool resolver) → AD-889 (Role→Skills→Tools commissioning capstone) → AD-890 (docs).**

**AD-889 shipped (2026-06-06; Wave 246; #853).** Capstone of the Skills & Tools Unification epic (#849-854) — wires the full **Role → Skills → Tools** commissioning spine and makes the ACM its single front door. The pieces existed in isolation: role templates (`OntologyService.get_role_template_for_agent`), developmental skill acquisition (AD-428 `AgentSkillService.acquire_skill`), the AD-888 skill→tool resolver, and AD-885's tool-grant lens — but nothing walked them end-to-end when a crew agent was born. AD-889 adds `AgentCapitalService.commission(agent_id, agent_type, runtime) -> dict` (`src/probos/acm.py`): a self-stateless front door (reads only `runtime` + logger, no `self._db` — so it needs no `acm.start()`) that (1) calls the legacy `skill_service.commission_agent` for back-compat, (2) reads the agent's role template off `runtime.ontology` and, for each `SkillRequirement`, maps `min_proficiency` → `ProficiencyLevel` (invalid → `FOLLOW`) and `await skill_service.acquire_skill(..., source="commission", proficiency=level)` (a `ValueError` for an unmet prereq is debug-logged, not raised), (3) fetches the resulting `SkillProfile` **once**, then (4) for each acquired skill resolves tools via the AD-888 `resolve_tools_for_skill` and **idempotently** issues a `ToolPermission.READ` grant per tool — skipping any tool the agent already holds a non-restriction active grant on (`tool_permission_store.get_active_grants_sync`), so re-commissioning never double-grants. It returns a summary `{agent_id, agent_type, skills_acquired: [...], tools_granted: [...]}`. **Wiring (Option A — birth path):** the commission hook lives in `runtime.create_pool` immediately after `onboarding.wire_agent`, guarded by `if self.acm and is_crew_agent(agent, self.ontology)` and wrapped Tier-2 log-and-degrade (a commission failure never aborts pool creation). Option A was chosen over wiring inside `agent_onboarding.wire_agent` because the AD-515 onboarding service deliberately lacks `runtime`/`skill_registry`/`tool_permission_store` handles; the ACM front door pulls every dependency off the live `runtime`, keeping onboarding dependency-light. The `/skills/agents/{agent_id}/commission` endpoint (`src/probos/routers/skills.py`) is re-pointed to `runtime.acm.commission` — a **deliberate contract change**: it now returns the commission summary dict instead of the old `profile.to_dict()`, the visible consequence of unifying the two commission paths behind one lens. **NO duties step** (no public per-agent duty accessor exists — same verify-first NO-SEAM as AD-885). Honest-degrade throughout: missing `skill_service` → empty summary; missing ontology/template → legacy-only; missing `tool_permission_store`/`tool_registry`/`skill_registry` → skills acquired but no tool grants. +6 pytest (`tests/test_ad889_commission_chain.py`, BF-287 real fixtures — real `AgentCapitalService` + real `SkillRegistry`/`AgentSkillService` on a tmp DB + real `ToolRegistry` + real `ToolPermissionStore`, no MagicMock at the substrate boundary): template skills acquired + tools granted; legacy-only fallback when no template; idempotent re-commission; a skill with no preferred tool grants nothing cleanly; degrade without a permission store; empty summary without a skill_service. Full gate green (15589 passed, 22 skipped; the only 2 reds are xdist-parallel timing flakes — `test_auto_commit_after_debounce`, `test_bf304_single_flight_drops_concurrent_describe` — both pass serially). One commit.

**AD-888 shipped (2026-06-06; Wave 245; #852).** Fourth AD of the Skills & Tools Unification epic (#849-854) — finishes the orphaned Skill→Tool binding. `SkillDefinition.preferred_tools: list[ToolPreference]` (AD-423, with a SQLite migration) has existed for waves but **nothing resolved it**: there was no path from "this agent exercises skill X" to "invoke the tool(s) that fulfil X." AD-888 adds a pure, side-effect-free resolver — a new standalone module `src/probos/tools/skill_tool_resolver.py` (chosen over a `ToolRegistry` method for Dependency Inversion) — `resolve_tools_for_skill(skill: SkillDefinition, *, agent_id, tool_registry, hebbian=None) -> list[ToolRegistration]`. Resolution order: (1) honour `skill.preferred_tools` in ascending `ToolPreference.priority` (lower = higher), keeping only tools that exist, are enabled, and that the agent holds at least `READ` on via `tool_registry.check_permission`; (2) when **no** preferred tool resolves, fall back to capability-tag discovery — `tool_registry.list_tools(tag=skill.domain)` (skipped for the universal `"*"` domain), again permission-filtered; (3) the optional `hebbian` parameter is a **documented no-op** — the real ranking primitive `HebbianRouter.get_preferred_targets` ranks agent→agent compatibility (`REL_AGENT`) and there are no agent→tool edges in the Hebbian graph yet, so there is nothing to rank tools by; the param is kept with a `# TODO` so this becomes the activation point once agent→tool edges exist, and the resolver works with `hebbian=None`. The resolver only **resolves** — it never invokes a tool, grants a permission, or touches consensus (that auto-invocation/granting is AD-889 + the AD-543-549 agentic executor, unchanged). An empty result is a clean `[]` — never a raise — for a skill with no fulfilling tool. The `ToolPreference`/`preferred_tools` schema is untouched. Layer note: the substrate module imports `SkillDefinition` (foundation) and the tool types (same layer) directly, and keeps the `hebbian` type annotation behind a `TYPE_CHECKING` guard so the substrate never imports the mesh layer at runtime. +6 pytest (`tests/test_ad888_skill_tool_resolver.py`, BF-287 real fixtures — real `ToolRegistry` + real `SkillDefinition`/`ToolPreference` + a minimal `Tool`-protocol stub): preferred tool returned first by priority; permission-denied preferred tool skipped; tag-fallback when no preferred tool; tag-fallback respects permission; a provided `hebbian` does not change ordering; empty result is a clean `[]`. AD-423 tool-foundation/permission/context + skill-framework blast-radius suites green (98) serially. One commit. AD-889 (the capstone) hard-depends on this resolver.

**AD-887 shipped (2026-06-06; Wave 244; #851).** Third AD of the Skills & Tools Unification epic (#849-854) — one query surface for both skill kinds. ProbOS has two skill stores: the AD-428 `AgentSkillService` (developmental/T3 proficiency-tracked `AgentSkillRecord`s) and the AD-596 `CognitiveSkillCatalog` (cognitive/T2 instruction-defined `SKILL.md` entries). AD-596c bridges catalog→registry but they were still queried separately, so "what skills does this agent have?" had two answers. AD-887 adds a `cognitive_skills: list[dict[str, Any]]` field (default empty) to the `SkillProfile` dataclass (`src/probos/skill_framework.py`) — typed as plain kind-tagged dicts so this **foundation-layer** dataclass stays decoupled from the cognitive-layer `CognitiveSkillEntry` type — and a new merge method on the bridge: `SkillBridge.get_unified_profile(self, agent_id, *, department=None, agent_rank=None) -> SkillProfile` (`src/probos/cognitive/skill_bridge.py`). It calls `self._service.get_profile(agent_id)` (the T3 developmental profile, **unchanged and T3-pure** — the service never consults the catalog) and then queries `self._catalog.list_entries(department=, min_rank=)` for the cognitive entries, appending each as a `{name, description, skill_id, department, min_proficiency, min_rank, origin, kind: "cognitive"}` dict to `profile.cognitive_skills`. The developmental records stay in their existing `pccs`/`role_skills`/`acquired_skills` buckets (implicitly developmental — no T3 record schema change). The merge site is the **bridge** — not the service, not the catalog — and there is **no `skills.db` schema change** (the cognitive kind is derived, not stored). A catalog query failure **log-and-degrades** (Tier-2) to a developmental-only profile with `exc_info=True`. The optional `department`/`agent_rank` keyword filters mirror the AD-885 dept/rank pattern and pass straight through to `list_entries`; defaulting both to `None` returns all visible cognitive entries. The `SKILL.md` on-disk format, progressive-disclosure loading (`get_instructions`/`find_by_intent`), and developmental proficiency math are untouched. +7 pytest (`tests/test_ad887_skill_library_unification.py`, BF-287 real fixtures: real `SkillRegistry`+`AgentSkillService` on a tmp DB + real `CognitiveSkillCatalog` + real `SkillBridge`): an agent with both kinds reports both (developmental in `all_skills`, cognitive tagged `kind="cognitive"`); cognitive entries carry the required keys; cognitive-only and developmental-only agents both resolve; a department filter excludes department-specific entries while keeping wildcards; `AgentSkillService.get_profile` stays T3-only (`cognitive_skills == []`); and a forced catalog failure degrades to developmental-only. AD-428 + AD-596 blast-radius suites green (94) serially. One commit.

**AD-886 shipped (2026-06-06; Wave 243; #850).** Second AD of the Skills & Tools Unification epic (#849-854) — reclassifies the legacy self-mod `Skill` as a first-class Tool. When the self-modification pipeline designs a new skill, it has always been attached to the `SkillBasedAgent` dispatch path (instance + class `_handled_intents`/`intent_descriptors`), but it was invisible to the AD-423 `ToolRegistry` — so the unified capability lens (AD-885), tool-grant permission checks, and any tool-aware routing couldn't see designed skills. AD-886 closes the seam with a **callback injection** (Dependency Inversion — the cognitive-layer pipeline doesn't import the substrate `ToolRegistry`): `SelfModificationPipeline.__init__` (`src/probos/cognitive/self_mod.py`) gains an optional `register_tool_fn: Callable | None = None` (stored as `self._register_tool_fn`); `handle_add_skill` adds a "step 5b" immediately after the existing `_add_skill_fn` call — guarded `if self._register_tool_fn is not None:` and wrapped in try/except → `logger.warning(..., exc_info=True)` (Tier-2 log-and-degrade: a ToolRegistry failure must not abort skill design). The callback is wired through `startup/cognitive_services.py` (`init_cognitive_services` gains keyword-only `register_tool_fn` passed into the pipeline ctor) from `runtime.py` Phase 4, where the concrete impl is the new `ProbOSRuntime._register_designed_tool(self, skill)`: it reads `self.tool_registry` (honest-degrade `logger.debug` + return if `None` — populated by Phase 7 `init_communication`, but the closure is only *called* post-startup at skill-design time, so the registry is always present by then), builds an `InfraServiceAdapter(tool_id=skill.name, name=skill.name, description=<descriptor.description or name>, intent_name=skill.name, intent_bus=self.intent_bus)` (the skill's handler is async and bus-dispatched, so InfraServiceAdapter — not DeterministicFunctionAdapter — is the correct adapter), and registers it `provider="designed"`. The designed skill now appears in `tool_registry.list_tools()` with `tool_type == ToolType.INFRA_SERVICE`, is invocable (awaitable) through the adapter (which re-broadcasts the skill's intent on the bus), AND still dispatches through its `SkillBasedAgent` exactly as before — the two paths coexist. +8 pytest (`tests/test_ad886_selfmod_tool_registration.py`, BF-287 real `ToolRegistry()` + `SimpleNamespace` runtime stub holding the real registry and a fake bus — calls the REAL `ProbOSRuntime._register_designed_tool`, no MagicMock at the substrate boundary): designed skill registered with `provider="designed"` + `tool_type == INFRA_SERVICE`; invocable through the adapter (`result.success`, `result.output == "hello"`, bus saw the `greet_user` intent); `None` registry is a clean no-op; pipeline `register_tool_fn` defaults `None` and stores/invokes a provided callback; and `SkillBasedAgent` dispatch survives tool registration (class-state snapshotted/restored to avoid test pollution). Blast radius `tests/test_self_mod.py` (66) green serially. One commit.

**AD-885 shipped (2026-06-06; Wave 242; #849).** First AD of the Skills & Tools Unification epic (#849-854) — makes the ACM the true single capability lens. `AgentCapitalService.get_consolidated_profile` (`src/probos/acm.py`) aggregated lifecycle, crew profile (AD-376), trust, earned agency (AD-357), AD-428 skills, and episode count, but was blind to cognitive skills (AD-596) and tool grants (AD-423). AD-885 appends two additive, read-only blocks, each guarded `hasattr`/`try` exactly like blocks 5-6: **(7) cognitive skills** — when `runtime.cognitive_skill_catalog` is present, `profile["cognitive_skills"]` is a list of `{name, description, skill_id}` dicts from `catalog.get_descriptions(department=profile.get("department"), agent_rank=profile.get("rank"))` (filtered by the agent's crew identity so the lens shows what THIS agent can see), plus `profile["cognitive_skill_count"]`; **(8) tool grants** — when `runtime.tool_registry` is present, `profile["tools"]` is the tool IDs the agent may invoke, computed by filtering `tool_registry.list_tools()` through `tool_registry.check_permission(agent_id, reg.tool_id, ToolPermission.READ, agent_department=<dept>, agent_rank=<rank or "ensign">)` using the agent's **real** rank/department from block 2 (not the ensign default — a senior agent's grants would otherwise be understated), plus `profile["tool_count"]`. **Duties are deliberately OMITTED** (verify-first NO-SEAM): there is no public per-agent duty accessor (`runtime.duty_schedule` is a scan-policy gate, `DutyScheduleTracker.get_due_duties` is private on `runtime.proactive_loop._duty_tracker` — a Law-of-Demeter violation to reach), so duties join the lens in a future AD that first ships a public `runtime.duty_schedule_tracker`. The existing 6 blocks, the method signature, and the `acm.db` schema are untouched — purely additive aggregation. +8 pytest (`tests/test_ad885_acm_unified_lens.py`, BF-287 real fixtures: real `AgentCapitalService`, real `ToolRegistry`, real started `CognitiveSkillCatalog(skills_dir=tmp_path)`, plain `_LensRuntime` stub holding the real subsystems so block-3/4/5/6 stay dormant via `hasattr`): cognitive skills present/absent/empty, tool grants ship-wide/rank-gated (commander gets a tool an ensign cannot — proving real rank is passed)/absent/empty, and existing blocks remain present. Blast radius `tests/test_acm.py` (37) green. One commit.

**BF-609 shipped (2026-06-05; Wave 241; #848).** Fixed Yeo's inline web lookups timing out ("Good catch on the name — let me look that up now. (The web_search lookup came back empty (Agent did not respond in time.))"). Root cause: the AD-869 Tier-2 mesh-read path (`DmReplyPipeline.step_4h_mesh_read_parse`, `src/probos/cognitive/dm/reply_pipeline.py`) applied a single flat `_MESH_READ_TTL_SECONDS = 5.0` ceiling to **every** allowlisted read intent. But `web_search` / `read_page` are network + LLM-bound — `WebSearchAgent`/`PageReaderAgent` (`src/probos/agents/utility/web_agents.py`) mesh-fetch DuckDuckGo through the rate-limited `http_fetch` path (AD-270: ≥2s per-domain wait + an internet round-trip) and, because both descriptors carry `requires_reflect=True`, then run a synthesis LLM call — realistically 8-20s end to end. The 5s ceiling tripped `asyncio.wait_for` in `IntentBus.send` (`src/probos/mesh/intent.py`) every time, returning the literal `"Agent did not respond in time."`. The other allowlisted reads (`list_directory`/`read_file`/`stat_file`/`search_files`) are local-IO in milliseconds, so 5s is correct for them. Fix: a per-intent TTL override map `_MESH_READ_TTL_BY_INTENT = {"web_search": _MESH_READ_NETWORK_TTL_SECONDS, "read_page": _MESH_READ_NETWORK_TTL_SECONDS}` with `_MESH_READ_NETWORK_TTL_SECONDS = 25.0`; the dispatch now passes `ttl_seconds=_MESH_READ_TTL_BY_INTENT.get(intent_name, _MESH_READ_TTL_SECONDS)`. This does **not** weaken the Tier-2 safety boundary — the allowlist + "consensus can't resolve in one turn" is what enforces read-only safety, not the latency ceiling (writes/`http_fetch` remain excluded). +4 pytest (`tests/test_ad869_mesh_read_inline.py`, real `IntentBus`+`SignalManager`+`AgentRegistry`+subscribed handler per BF-287): `web_search`/`read_page` dispatch with the 25s network ceiling, local-IO reads keep the 5s default, and the override map targets exactly the two network intents. Full AD-869 suite (20) green serially. One commit.

**AD-882 shipped (2026-06-05; Wave 240).** Final AD of the Quartermaster-hardening epic (#847) — a federation node-scope guard, shipped as a **default-safe no-op with a forward seam**. Liveness is checked against the **local** `AgentRegistry`, so in a multi-node mesh an item assigned to an agent on **another node** looks "not live" locally and would be wrongly reclaimed by this node's sweep. Verify-first finding (DECISIONS.md AD-882): **no per-agent or per-work-item node marker exists today** — node ownership lives only at config/peer granularity (`FederationConfig.node_id` / `enabled`, `src/probos/config.py`) and `WorkItem.assigned_to` is a bare agent id with no node qualifier. AD-882 therefore cannot *determine* remote ownership from existing data; it installs the seam: an optional `metadata['owner_node']` convention that future federation code can populate. In the shared `_process_item` (`src/probos/agents/quartermaster.py`), **before** acting on a `clear_and_reroute` decision and **before** the AD-877 attempt counter is incremented, if `self._federation_enabled` **and** `wi["metadata"].get("owner_node")` is set **and** it differs from `self._local_node_id`, the sweep skips the item with `reason="remote_owner"`, increments `counts["remote_owner_skipped"]`, and does **not** unassign / re-dispatch / accrue a local attempt. In all other cases (federation disabled — the default — marker absent, or marker == local node) the sweep proceeds as today, so on a single-node deployment the branch is never taken: a pure no-op. Local node id + federation flag are injected onto the live agent in `startup/finalize.py` `_wire_board_reconciler` (`agent._local_node_id = config.federation.node_id`, `agent._federation_enabled = config.federation.enabled`) and mirrored by constructor kwargs `local_node_id="node-1"` / `federation_enabled=False` for direct test construction. The always-present counts dict gains `remote_owner_skipped` (the AD-875 empty-board exact-dict updated accordingly). +5 pytest (`tests/test_ad882_federation_node_guard.py`, real `WorkItemStore` + real `AgentRegistry` per BF-287): federation-off ignores the marker (item reclaimed), enabled+remote-owner is skipped and left untouched (not unassigned, not dispatched), enabled+local-owner reclaimed normally, enabled+no-marker treated as local, and a remote_owner skip does not bump `reconcile_attempts`. No federation routing, peer lookup, or `WorkItem`/registry node field was added (out of scope). One commit. **Epic #847 complete (AD-877→884).**

**AD-881 shipped (2026-06-05; Wave 239).** Seventh AD of the Quartermaster-hardening epic (#847) — detect live-but-stalled assignees. The reconciler reclaimed only items whose assignee was **absent** from the registry (`WorkItemReconciler.resolve_live_agent` returns `None`); an assignee that is **live but silently stuck** (claimed an item, made no board progress) was never reclaimed. AD-881 adds a **config-gated, default-off (0)** coarse staleness signal. Verify-first finding (documented in DECISIONS.md): `updated_at` is **last board-mutation, not a heartbeat** — `WorkItemStore.update_work_item` (`src/probos/workforce.py`) sets `updated_at = time.time()` on every update, so it advances on claim / status / assignment changes routed through the board, but **not** from agent-internal "still working" progress that never touches the board. So `now - updated_at` is a coarse "claimed long ago, never progressed" signal, not a true liveness probe — hence default-off and no heartbeat plumbing added (out of scope). Implementation keeps `WorkItemReconciler` pure: the sweep owns the clock + threshold and passes a precomputed `is_stalled` boolean into `classify(wi, *, is_dispatchable, is_stalled=False)` (`src/probos/cognitive/work_reconciler.py`); when `is_stalled` and the assignee is live and `status == "in_progress"`, classify returns `clear_and_reroute` with `reason="stalled"` instead of the usual skip. In the shared `_process_item` (`src/probos/agents/quartermaster.py`, so the rule applies to both periodic `reconcile()` and reactive `reconcile_for_agent()`), the sweep computes `is_stalled = _stall_timeout_seconds > 0 and status == "in_progress" and float(updated_at) < time.time() - _stall_timeout_seconds` (**strict `<`** older-than) and increments `counts["stalled"]` when a `clear_and_reroute` carries `reason="stalled"`. A stall-reroute flows through the **AD-877 attempt guard** exactly like any other clear_and_reroute — it increments `reconcile_attempts`, honours backoff, and quarantines at the threshold — so a chronically stalled item can't thrash. New bounded config on `WorkBoardReconcilerConfig` (`src/probos/config.py`): `stall_timeout_seconds: int = Field(default=0, ge=0, le=86400)` (`0` = disabled, transitional default-off — conv #14), injected onto the live agent as `agent._stall_timeout_seconds` in `startup/finalize.py` `_wire_board_reconciler`, with a matching `stall_timeout_seconds=0` constructor kwarg. +6 pytest (`tests/test_ad881_stall_detection.py`, real `WorkItemStore` + real `AgentRegistry` per BF-287 so `resolve_live_agent` resolves a registered live agent; `updated_at` seeded via `create_work_item`): disabled (0) never reclaims a live-old item, enabled reroutes an old live in_progress item with `counts['stalled']++`, a fresh item is not reclaimed, an absent assignee takes the normal absent path (not double-counted as stalled), a stall-reroute quarantines at the AD-877 threshold, and the strict `<` boundary is not stalled. The AD-875 empty-board exact-dict test gained the always-present `stalled` key (obsolete-contract guard). One commit.

**AD-880 shipped (2026-06-05; Wave 238).** Sixth AD of the Quartermaster-hardening epic (#847) — reactive reclaim on agent removal. Reclaim was poll-only: a crashed/removed agent's in-flight items waited up to `interval_seconds` (default 300s) for the next periodic sweep. AD-880 adds an **additive, default-off** reactive path. `AgentRegistry.unregister` (`src/probos/substrate/registry.py`) — the single agent-removal chokepoint (`pool.remove_agent` → `registry.unregister`) — now emits a new `EventType.AGENT_REMOVED` (`src/probos/events.py`, agent-lifecycle cluster) **outside** the registry lock (BF-598 re-entrancy safety), carrying `{agent_id, agent_type}`, and only when a present agent was actually popped (absent-id pop emits nothing; an unbound `_emit_event_fn` is a no-op). The Quartermaster gains `reconcile_for_agent(agent_id)` (`src/probos/agents/quartermaster.py`): an honest-degrade scoped sweep that lists `open` + `in_progress` items, filters to `assigned_to == agent_id`, and reuses the new shared `_process_item` per-item routine — so the AD-877 thrash/quarantine/backoff guards, AD-878 boot-grace skip, and AD-879 oldest-first ordering all apply identically to reactive and periodic reclaim. The per-item body was extracted from `reconcile()` into `_process_item` (DRY, standing order) and the 11-key counts shape into `_new_counts` (single source of truth for later-AD counters); `reconcile()` behavior is byte-for-byte preserved (AD-875/876/877/878/879 suites green unchanged). The reactive sweep emits `WORK_ITEM_RECONCILED` with `{**counts, "trigger": "reactive", "agent_id": ...}`, stores an episode, and records `self._last_sweep` (AD-883 surface) with `trigger="reactive"`. New default-off config `reactive_reclaim: bool = False` on `WorkBoardReconcilerConfig` (`src/probos/config.py`, transitional flag — conv #14); when enabled and the reconciler is otherwise wired, `startup/finalize.py` `_wire_board_reconciler` subscribes a Tier-2-guarded handler to `AGENT_REMOVED` via `runtime.add_event_listener` and holds the reference (`runtime.board_reactive_reclaim_handler`); when disabled (default) no subscription is created. +8 pytest (`tests/test_ad880_reactive_reclaim.py`, real `AgentRegistry` + real `WorkItemStore` per BF-287, `_Fake` only at the router/runtime-bus boundary): unregister of a present agent emits `AGENT_REMOVED` once, absent-id pop emits nothing, an unbound emit-fn doesn't crash, the payload carries `agent_id`+`agent_type`, `reconcile_for_agent` reclaims only the named agent's items, it honours the AD-877 attempt guard (quarantine not loop), it records a `trigger="reactive"` last-sweep, `reactive_reclaim=False` creates no subscription, and `reactive_reclaim=True` wires a handler that reclaims a dead agent's orphan item. One commit.

**AD-883 shipped (2026-06-05; Wave 237).** Fifth AD of the Quartermaster-hardening epic (#847) — reconciliation observability. The sweep already emits `WORK_ITEM_RECONCILED` counts to the event log, but the Captain couldn't see reconcile activity at a glance. AD-883 surfaces the last-sweep summary through the **existing introspection path** (HXI #6/#10) with **no new slash command, HTTP endpoint, or `panels.py` change**: `QuartermasterAgent.reconcile()` now records `self._last_sweep = {"counts": dict(counts), "at": time.time(), "trigger": "periodic"}` at the end of each pass (initialized `None` for never-run), and a new `QuartermasterAgent.info()` override (`src/probos/agents/quartermaster.py`) calls `super().info()` and adds a `reconciliation` block — `{"last_sweep": None}` when never run, else `{"last_sweep": {...counts...}, "age_seconds": round(now - at, 1), "trigger": ...}`. The block copies the whole counts dict (no hard-indexed keys), so later-AD counters (`quarantined`, `too_fresh`, `truncated`, `stalled`, `remote_owner_skipped`) never KeyError an older renderer. It surfaces automatically via the `agent_info quartermaster` intent (AD-320 delegation → `IntrospectionAgent._agent_info` → `agent.info()`). +5 pytest (`tests/test_ad883_reconcile_observability.py`, real `WorkItemStore` + real `QuartermasterAgent` per BF-287): `reconcile()` records the summary, `info()` includes the `reconciliation.last_sweep` block, never-run reports `last_sweep: None` without crashing, `age_seconds` reflects elapsed time, and `info()` renders cleanly when counts carry later-AD keys. One commit.

**AD-884 shipped (2026-06-05; Wave 236).** Fourth AD of the Quartermaster-hardening epic (#847) — a governance + regression-guard record, not a behavior change. Makes the `QuartermasterAgent`'s authority scope **explicit**: its mutations (`unassign_work_item`, re-`dispatch_work_item`, the AD-877 metadata `quarantined` flag) are all reversible board housekeeping, so per the Reversibility Preference and Minimal Authority axioms it requires **no** consensus gate — and that decision is now recorded (DECISIONS.md AD-884) and locked against regression. Added one class constant `RECONCILE_ONLY_INTENTS: frozenset[str] = frozenset({"reconcile_board"})` on `QuartermasterAgent` (`src/probos/agents/quartermaster.py`) as the allow-list the guard asserts against; no consensus gate, quorum vote, red-team hook, or destructive intent was added (the explicit decision is that none is needed). +3 pytest (`tests/test_ad884_authority_scope.py`, real agent per BF-287, no LLM): declared `intent_descriptors` names are reconcile-only (`⊆ RECONCILE_ONLY_INTENTS`, `== {"reconcile_board"}`), no declared descriptor sets `requires_consensus=True`, and the agent stays `tier="utility"` / `agent_type="quartermaster"`. One commit.

**AD-878 shipped (2026-06-05; Wave 235).** Third AD of the Quartermaster-hardening epic (#847). Closed a boot-race / first-dispatch reclaim window in `QuartermasterAgent.reconcile()` (`src/probos/agents/quartermaster.py`): a work item created moments before a warm-boot sweep (mid-first-dispatch, not yet `in_progress`, not yet assigned) could be reclaimed by the reconciler and accrue a wasted `reconcile_attempts` bump before the legitimate dispatch landed. Fix: a per-item boot-race grace period applied **before** any classify/attempt logic — inside the per-item `try`, after `md_current = wi.get("metadata") or {}` and before the AD-877 quarantine guard, if `self._min_item_age_seconds > 0` and `wi["created_at"] > time.time() - self._min_item_age_seconds` the item is counted `too_fresh` and skipped (`continue`), so a fresh item is never reclaimed and never accrues an attempt. The summary dict always carries the new `too_fresh` counter; setting the grace to `0` disables the skip entirely (every item processed). New bounded config on `WorkBoardReconcilerConfig` (`src/probos/config.py`): `min_item_age_seconds` (default 30, `[0,600]`) — an always-on safety bound, not a transitional flag — injected onto the live agent in `startup/finalize.py` `_wire_board_reconciler`. +6 pytest (`tests/test_ad878_boot_grace_period.py`, real `WorkItemStore` per BF-287, `_FakeRouter` only for the dispatch boundary; every item seeds an explicit `created_at`): fresh item skipped + never dispatched, old item processed normally, boundary at exactly `now - age` is processed (strict `>`), `min_item_age_seconds=0` disables the grace, `too_fresh` key present when zero, and a `clear_and_reroute` of an old item does not let a fresh sibling accrue an attempt. The pre-AD-878 quartermaster tests (AD-875/876/877) that drive the sweep with fresh items opt out of the grace (`min_item_age_seconds=0` in their helpers / config) and the AD-875 empty-board exact-dict gained the always-present `too_fresh` key (obsolete-contract guard). One commit.

**AD-879 shipped (2026-06-05; Wave 234).** Second AD of the Quartermaster-hardening epic (#847). Fixed a starvation bug in `QuartermasterAgent.reconcile()` (`src/probos/agents/quartermaster.py`): `WorkItemStore.list_work_items` returns `ORDER BY priority ASC, created_at DESC` (newest-first within a priority band), so under the `scan_limit` cap the oldest stranded items could sort last and never enter the scanned window. The epic's "confirm ordering is already oldest-first" branch was verified FALSE; AD-879 adds an explicit re-sort of the merged `open` + `in_progress` set: `ordered = sorted(merged.values(), key=lambda i: (i.priority, i.created_at))` — priority ascending (critical first) then `created_at` ascending (oldest first) — and the sweep iterates `ordered`. Added a starvation-visibility flag: if `len(merged) >= self._scan_limit` the summary sets `counts["truncated"] = True` and logs one Tier-2 warning (oldest items prioritized but backlog growing); otherwise `truncated` defaults `False` and is always present in the counts dict. Full backlog drainage beyond `scan_limit` is explicitly out of scope — the `truncated` flag is the signal. No change to `list_work_items` SQL (other callers depend on newest-first) and `scan_limit` default stays 200. +5 pytest (`tests/test_ad879_scan_ordering.py`, real `WorkItemStore`; `_FakeRouter` records dispatch order): `(priority asc, created_at asc)` processing order, same-priority-older-first, lower-priority-number-first-regardless-of-age, `truncated` True at the cap, `truncated` False for a small backlog. The AD-875 empty-board exact-dict test gained the always-present `truncated` key (obsolete-contract guard). One commit.

**AD-877 shipped (2026-06-05; Wave 233).** First AD of the Quartermaster-hardening epic (#847). Added a reconcile-attempt thrash guard to `QuartermasterAgent.reconcile()` (`src/probos/agents/quartermaster.py`) so a permanently-stranded work item can no longer be unassigned+re-dispatched on every sweep forever. Each `clear_and_reroute` now reads the item's `metadata` (read-modify-write — `update_work_item` REPLACES the metadata column), bumps `reconcile_attempts`, and stamps `last_reconcile_at`. When `reconcile_attempts + 1 >= max_reconcile_attempts` (config default 3) the item is **dead-lettered**: metadata gets `quarantined=True`, `quarantine_reason="max_reconcile_attempts"`, `quarantined_at`, the attempt counter is persisted, a single `EventType.WORK_ITEM_QUARANTINED` event is emitted (`{work_item_id, reason, attempts}`), and the item is left assigned (NOT unassigned) so it stops thrashing. Two early-skip guards run before classification: already-`quarantined` items are skipped (`quarantined_skipped`), and items reconciled within `reconcile_backoff_seconds` (config default 600s; `0` disables) are skipped (`backoff_skipped`) — a per-item cooldown so a transient gap doesn't get hammered every tick. The summary dict always carries the three new counters (`quarantined`, `quarantined_skipped`, `backoff_skipped`); `degraded` keeps its AD-875 bool semantics. New bounded config on `WorkBoardReconcilerConfig` (`src/probos/config.py`): `max_reconcile_attempts` (default 3, `[1,20]`) and `reconcile_backoff_seconds` (default 600, `[0,86400]`), injected onto the live agent in `startup/finalize.py` `_wire_board_reconciler`. No status-enum change (quarantine is a metadata flag, not a `WorkItemStatus`). +10 pytest (`tests/test_ad877_reconcile_thrash_guard.py`, real `WorkItemStore`/`AgentRegistry`/`WorkItemReconciler` per BF-287, `_FakeRouter` only for the dispatch boundary). One commit.

**AD-876 shipped (2026-06-05; Wave 232).** Closed the Quartermaster / work-board reconciliation epic (#846) by wiring the AD-875 `QuartermasterAgent` into the runtime with a cadence ticker. New transitional config `WorkBoardReconcilerConfig` (`src/probos/config.py`, `enabled=False` per conv #14, `interval_seconds=300` bounded `[30,3600]`, `warm_boot=True`, `scan_limit=200` bounded `[1,2000]`; requires hybrid_dispatch) added to `SystemConfig.work_board_reconciler`. The `quartermaster` template is registered in `runtime.py` (mirrors `introspect`) and a single-agent `quartermaster` pool is created in `startup/agent_fleet.py` **only when** `work_board_reconciler.enabled`. New `src/probos/mesh/board_reconciler_ticker.py` — `BoardReconcilerTicker` owns the cadence (the agent has no loop): an idempotent `start()` holds the `asyncio.create_task` reference, `_loop()` runs an optional warm-boot reconcile after a startup delay then `await self._agent.reconcile()` every `interval_seconds`, each sweep wrapped Tier-2 log-and-degrade (`CancelledError` re-raised), and an idempotent `async stop()` cancels + awaits cleanly. `startup/finalize.py` gains `_wire_board_reconciler` (between `_wire_hybrid_dispatch` and `_wire_capability_gap_driver`): requires `runtime.work_item_router` (only present with hybrid_dispatch), `work_item_store`, and `registry` — any missing → INFO no-op + `False`; otherwise it builds a `WorkItemReconciler`, resolves the live quartermaster via `registry.get_by_pool`, injects collaborators by their exact private attrs, constructs + `start()`s the ticker, and parks it on `runtime.board_reconciler_ticker`. `startup/shutdown.py` `await`s `board_reconciler_ticker.stop()` (after the AD-733-1 attachment_reaper stop, honest-degrade). +12 pytest (`tests/test_ad876_reconciler_wiring.py`, real `WorkItemStore`/`AgentRegistry`/`SystemConfig` per BF-287, `_Fake*` only for the router/dispatch boundary; config-bounds rejection, disabled/missing-dep no-ops, injection assertions, ticker task-reference source scan, warm-boot reconcile-once, idempotent stop, warm-boot redispatch integration). One commit.

**AD-875 shipped (2026-06-05; Wave 231).** Second AD of the Quartermaster / work-board reconciliation epic (#846). Added the `QuartermasterAgent` (`src/probos/agents/quartermaster.py`) — a plain deterministic `BaseAgent` (`tier="utility"`, no LLM, mirrors `IntrospectionAgent`) that consumes the AD-874 `WorkItemReconciler` decisions and performs the actual board mutations. `reconcile()` lists only the live `open` + `in_progress` work items from the `WorkItemStore` (terminal statuses are never scanned), classifies each via `WorkItemRouter.is_dispatchable` + `WorkItemReconciler.classify`, and executes: `live_redispatch → router.dispatch_work_item`, `clear_and_reroute → store.unassign_work_item` then re-dispatch the freshly-cleared item, `skip → count only`. Returns a `{scanned, redispatched, cleared, skipped, degraded}` counts dict; per-item failures are tier-2 log-and-degrade (`degraded=True`, sweep continues), and a missing collaborator (store/router/reconciler `None`) returns a degraded zero-summary without raising. On completion it emits a single `EventType.WORK_ITEM_RECONCILED` (new enum value) with the counts and stores an optional episodic `Episode` summary. The agent follows the `perceive → decide → act → report` lifecycle; `handle_intent` only services `reconcile_board` (returns `None` for anything else). No ticker/startup wiring yet (AD-876). +12 pytest (`tests/test_ad875_quartermaster.py`, real `WorkItemStore` + real `AgentRegistry` + real `WorkItemReconciler`, `_FakeRouter` for the dispatch boundary). One commit.

**AD-874 shipped (2026-06-05; Wave 230).** First AD of the Quartermaster / work-board reconciliation epic (#846). Refactored the AD-581a `WorkItemRouter` (`src/probos/mesh/work_item_router.py`) so the dispatch path is reusable (Open/Closed): extracted `async dispatch_work_item(wi: dict)` — every line from the `is_dispatchable` early-return through `DepartmentDispatcher.route` (direct/broadcast decision), `TaskEvent` construction, `HYBRID_DISPATCH_DIRECT`/`BROADCAST` emit, and `dispatcher.dispatch` — out of `on_work_item_created`, which now just unwraps the event envelope and delegates (behavior-preserving for the create path). Added the deterministic stranded-item classifier `WorkItemReconciler` (`src/probos/cognitive/work_reconciler.py`): `resolve_live_agent(assigned_to)` checks the live `AgentRegistry` first, then walks the AD-441 sovereign-DID seam (assignee slot → birth-cert `agent_uuid` → re-spawned live slot via `AgentIdentityRegistry.get_by_slot`); `classify(wi, *, is_dispatchable)` maps a work-item dict to a frozen `ReconcileDecision` (`skip` / `live_redispatch` / `clear_and_reroute`) with reasons (`terminal`, `not_dispatchable`, `unassigned_dispatchable`, `in_progress_live_owner`, `assignee_live`, `assignee_not_live`). No sweep loop yet (AD-875) and no wiring (AD-876). All collaborator access is tier-2 log-and-degrade. +13 pytest (`tests/test_ad874_work_reconciler.py`, real `AgentRegistry` + real `AgentIdentityRegistry`) and +4 (`tests/test_ad839_work_item_dispatch.py`: direct/broadcast routing identical through `dispatch_work_item`, non-dispatchable early-return, `on_work_item_created` still routes). One commit.

**BF-608 shipped (2026-06-05; Wave 229).** Completed the work item `1e0ffcdb7b57` (`in_progress` / `Unassigned`) saga that BF-606/BF-607 only partially closed. Live `/api/work-items` inspection proved the Captain-reported item is a **stale orphan**: created 11:03, reached `in_progress` 11:08 — *before* the BF-607 restart at 15:01 — and the AD-581a `WorkItemRouter` (dispatches only on `WORK_ITEM_CREATED`) never re-dispatches it; BF-607's claim only runs on the *direct*-dispatch path (`target_agent_id == self.id`), but this item reached `in_progress` via broadcast dispatch / the AD-855 capability-gap `blocked → in_progress` resume. True root cause: AD-498 defined `WorkTypeTransition("open", "in_progress", requires_assignment=True)` but `validate_transition` only checked the edge *exists*, never the flag — so **any** path could drive an unassigned item into `in_progress`. Fix (single store-boundary chokepoint): new `WorkTypeRegistry.transition_requires_assignment(...)` exposes the per-edge flag, and `WorkItemStore.transition_work_item` (`src/probos/workforce.py`) now refuses a transition into a `requires_assignment` status while `assigned_to is None` (logs a `BF-608` warning, returns `None`, leaves the item in its prior dispatchable status) — making the contradictory board state unreachable from every path (direct, broadcast, AD-855 resume, REST endpoint) without the store importing the agent registry (it rejects rather than auto-assigning). The one legitimate "no single worker" case — the AD-867 crew **parent** (a coordination container; its children carry the real assignments) — is claimed for `"crew_orchestrator"` in `crew_orchestrator._promote_parent` before promotion (more honest on the board than `Unassigned`). The stale orphan predates the fix and is cleaned up out-of-band via the live API. +5 pytest (`tests/test_workforce.py`: refusal with no DB write / no `STATUS_CHANGED`, the `BF-608` warning, assigned item transitions, AD-855 resume keeps owner, `transition_requires_assignment` helper) + 9 existing transition tests updated to assign-before-promote + `test_ad867_crew_orchestrator.py` parent-promotion asserts the claim; cluster of 214 + 155 transition/crew/yeo tests green serially (the full-suite xdist run hits a pre-existing `ServicePackMajorVersion` worker-down crash, unrelated to this change). **This commit also lands the BF-606 same-status no-op guard + its tests** — documented in `a33f67bd` but whose *code* was never committed (only the banners landed), so HEAD's `workforce.py` finally matches its own BF-606 docs; the two changes are entangled in the same hunks (the BF-606 tests' setup transition now needs BF-608 assignment) and ship together. One commit.

**BF-607 shipped (2026-06-05; Wave 228).** Fixed a Captain-reported board defect: a `yeo-delegated` task showed `in_progress` / `dispatched` but `Assigned to: Unassigned`. Root cause: when the AD-581a `WorkItemRouter` direct-dispatches an item it emits the `work_item_dispatched` task event but **never persists `assigned_to`** — and the agent's `_handle_work_item_dispatch` (`src/probos/cognitive/cognitive_agent.py`) only transitioned the item to `in_progress`, so the board correctly showed work running with nobody assigned. Fix (single-point, co-located with the existing `in_progress` transition): the handling agent now *claims* the item — `if not current.assigned_to: await store.update_work_item(work_item_id, assigned_to=self.id)` — guarded so an item the Captain or crew orchestrator already assigned is left untouched, and tier-2 log-and-degrade so a failed claim never raises into the cognitive queue (the item still moves to `in_progress`). `assigned_to` is mutable (`update_work_item` honors it; not in `_IMMUTABLE_FIELDS`). The LLM `tier=standard` empty-content warnings reported alongside this were diagnosed as a **transient** (config + proxy both confirmed Sonnet 4.6; AD-632c detected the empty completion and AD-643a recovered) — no code change. +3 pytest (`tests/test_ad839_work_item_dispatch.py`: claims an unassigned item, does not reassign an already-assigned item, log-and-degrades a failed claim while still transitioning); the existing 5 AD-839 dispatch tests stay green (8 total). One commit.

**BF-606 shipped (2026-06-05; Wave 227).** Stopped a work-item death-spiral surfaced by the 11:28:08 crash forensic: work item `1e0ffcdb7b57` logged `Invalid transition … 'in_progress' → 'in_progress'` dozens of times. Root cause: `work_item_dispatched` is delivered **at-least-once** (broadcast fan-out to every crew agent — each recipient's `_handle_work_item_dispatch` ends in `transition_work_item(id, "in_progress")`; the AD-855 capability-gap `_resume` path sets `in_progress` then re-dispatches; bus redelivery), but the AD-498 state machine has no `in_progress → in_progress` edge, so every redundant delivery logged a warning and returned `None` (read by callers as failure). Fix (single-point, store boundary): `WorkItemStore.transition_work_item` (`src/probos/workforce.py`) now treats a same-status transition as an idempotent no-op success — new `if new_status == item.status: return item` guard placed **before** the AD-498 `validate_transition` check, so it returns the item unchanged with no DB write, no `WORK_ITEM_STATUS_CHANGED` event, and no warning. This is the idempotent-consumer pattern for at-least-once delivery (transitioning to the current state is success-by-definition). The guard sits before terminal-status logic, so a terminal item asked to move to a *different* status still hits the existing rejection. A pre-existing test (`test_transition_from_terminal_status_rejected`) was passing by accident — it never actually reached `done` (`open → done` is illegal for `task`) and only passed because `open → open` used to return `None`; its setup was corrected to `open → in_progress → done`. +2 pytest (`tests/test_workforce.py`: same-status returns the item with zero emits; same-status emits no `Invalid transition` warning). The dispatch handler's re-run behavior is untouched, so the AD-855 resume re-dispatch keeps working. One commit.

**BF-605 shipped (2026-06-05; Wave 226).** Stale-constant fix: `ui/src/components/icons/Glyphs.test.tsx` asserted `count === 25` but 29 glyph components are now exported from `Glyphs.tsx` (+4 added since BF-041 without updating the test's count tripwire) — bumped to `29`. Pre-existing red-on-main, unrelated to BF-604, caught during its UI gate. One commit.

**BF-604 shipped (2026-06-05; Wave 225).** Fixed three Captain-reported HXI board complaints in one UI change (no backend touched): (1) the WORK board (`ui/src/components/work/WorkBoard.tsx`) and KANBAN board (`ui/src/components/bridge/FullKanban.tsx`) headers overlapped the fixed `ViewSwitcher` tab bar — both roots now reserve `paddingTop` (40px) clearance with `boxSizing: border-box`; (2) WORK columns are now resizable — new `colWidths` state + a `role=separator` `col-resize` drag handle on each non-terminal column's right edge (explicit px width overrides the default `flex: 1; minWidth: 140`); (3) the "why two boards?" confusion — KANBAN (Mission-Control build/design pipeline, AD-322/325) is distinct from WORK (crew tasks, AD-496/497) but was an always-empty board next to it, so `ViewSwitcher` now hides the KANBAN tab when `missionControlTasks` is empty (progressive disclosure, HXI Principle #5) with a `useEffect` redirect `kanban`→`work` if the tab is hidden out from under the viewer. +7 vitest (`WorkBoard.bf604.test.tsx` ×3, `ViewSwitcher.bf604.test.tsx` ×4); the existing `ComponentRendering.test.tsx` ViewSwitcher case was updated to seed a build. One commit.

**BF-603 shipped (2026-06-05; Wave 224).** Closed an intent self-tag leak: the AD-722a `<intent emotion=NAME>` divergence self-tag reached the Captain verbatim in a Yeo 1:1 DM (`<intent emotion="warm">` printed at the head of the reply). Root cause: the parse/strip regexes in `src/probos/avatars/divergence_detector.py` only accepted an **unquoted** value (`[a-zA-Z_]+`) anchored to **end-of-line**, but the LLM emitted a **quoted** value (`emotion="warm"`) at the **start** of the reply — so `parse_intent_self_tag` silently dropped the divergence signal AND `strip_intent_self_tag` failed to remove the tag, leaking it. The strip runs unconditionally inside `apply_divergence_check` (single call site, `routers/agents.py:agent_chat`), so the regex was the only fault. Fix (regex-only, no wiring change): (1) `_TAG_RE` and `_TAG_STRIP_RE` now tolerate an optional `"`/`'` around the value; (2) `_TAG_STRIP_RE` dropped the `$`/`MULTILINE` end-of-line anchor so the tag is stripped from any position (leading / inline / trailing); (3) `strip_intent_self_tag` collapses the match to a single space and `.strip()`s (was `.sub("")` + `.rstrip()`) so inline removal never merges adjacent words. +7 pytest (`tests/test_ad722a_divergence_detector.py`: double/single-quoted parse, quoted self-closing, the exact leading-quoted leak shape, leading-unquoted strip, inline no-word-merge, quoted idempotency); the 91-case AD-722a/AD-737 divergence suite stays green. One commit.

**BF-602 shipped (2026-06-05; Wave 223).** Fixed a shutdown-time crash where a Ward Room coalesce timer fired after the ward_room DB connection was torn down, surfacing as `asyncio: Task exception was never retrieved … ValueError('no active connection')` from `WardRoomRouter._fire → route_event → get_thread`. Root cause: `route_event_coalesced` (`src/probos/ward_room_router.py`) scheduled `_fire()` via `loop.call_later` + a **bare** `asyncio.create_task` (fire-and-forget, no reference held). During shutdown the explicit dream_cycle makes agents post to the Ward Room (observed: `yeoman` proactive_think during consolidation); each post schedules a ~200ms coalesce timer (AD-616), and when the timer fired after the DB closed, `route_event` crashed inside aiosqlite as an unretrieved task exception. Fix: (1) a `_stopping` flag + new sync, idempotent `WardRoomRouter.stop()` that cancels all pending `_coalesce_timers` handles and in-flight `_fire` tasks; (2) early-return guards keyed on `_stopping` at the top of `route_event_coalesced` **and** `route_event` (so no routing touches a possibly-closed DB once shutdown begins); (3) `_fire` now holds a task reference in a new `_coalesce_fire_tasks` set (drained via `add_done_callback`) and wraps `route_event` in a `CancelledError`-reraising, `Exception`-degrading try/except so a late fire log-and-degrades instead of becoming an unretrieved exception; (4) `startup/shutdown.py` calls `runtime.ward_room_router.stop()` alongside the BF-296 Phase A intent-dispatch close (before the explicit consolidation dream_cycle), so routing is quiesced before any shutdown post is created — honest-degrade if the router/attr is absent on transitional procs. +11 pytest (`tests/test_bf602_ward_room_shutdown_quiesce.py`: stop() teardown/idempotency/timer+task cancellation, routing-suppressed-when-stopping, the closed-DB `_fire` log-and-degrade regression, in-flight task-reference tracking, and a shutdown-wiring source assertion); the AD-616 partial-router unit test got `_stopping = False` added to its `__new__` fixture (same precedent as BF-598). Full gate green. One commit.

**AD-873 shipped (2026-06-04; Wave 222).** Closed the Memory Epistemics wave (AD-871 provenance → AD-872 forge observability → AD-873) with an Ebbinghaus forgetting curve over episodic memory plus a config-gated composite recall reranker ([#845](https://github.com/seangalliher/ProbOS/issues/845)). Every `Episode` (`src/probos/types.py`) now carries `strength: float = 1.0` and `stability: float = EBBINGHAUS_DEFAULT_STABILITY_SECONDS` (new module constant `1_728_000.0` = 20 days). Two pure decay helpers in `src/probos/cognitive/episodic.py`: `decayed_strength(strength, stability, delta_seconds)` (`strength * e^(-Δt/stability)`, guarding non-positive stability/delta) and `reinforced_stability(stability, activation)` (`stability * (1 + 0.25 * activation)`). New `EpisodicMemory.sweep_episode_decay(activation_tracker=None, *, limit=500) -> {"swept": N, "reinforced": M}` reads one page of episode metadata via `asyncio.to_thread`, batch-reads activations (honest-degrade → `{}`), and per episode recomputes `stability` **idempotently from the default baseline** (so a sweep that runs twice never double-reinforces), decays `strength`, and persists via the AD-608 `update_episode_metadata` primitive. A `DreamingEngine` Step 7f2 (`dreaming.py`, between 7f and 7g) drives the sweep during the offline dream cycle — decay never touches the recall hot path. Config-gated composite reranking: `recall` over-fetches (`k*10` vs `k*3`) and reorders via a static geometric `_composite_recall_score` (similarity baseline × `clamp01(field)**weight` for strength / recency=`exp(-age_hours/168)` / importance / confidence, each skipped at weight 0; all-zero weights collapse to raw similarity) **only when** `recall_rerank_enabled`, preserving the relevance threshold + AD-607a anomaly gate. Gated by two new `__init__` kwargs (`recall_rerank_enabled: bool = False`, `recall_rerank_weights`) backed by two new `MemoryConfig` fields and wired at the primary `__main__.py` boot site (migration CLI site left at defaults → rerank disabled). **Disabled by default** so zero-config boot recall is byte-identical to pre-AD-873 semantic-only. New fields round-trip through ChromaDB (`float()` coercion on write, default reads on read → pre-AD-873 rows recall cleanly, zero migration). +25 pytest (`tests/test_ad873_episode_decay.py`, real `EpisodicMemory`/`tmp_path` + real `DreamingEngine`/`MockEpisodicMemory` fixtures per BF-287; both integration tests seed an episode before constructing the engine because `dream_cycle` early-returns on empty memory); `tests/test_episodic.py` + `tests/test_dreaming.py` + `tests/test_ad871_provenance_envelope.py` (70) green. Closes [#845](https://github.com/seangalliher/ProbOS/issues/845). One commit, no push.

**AD-872 shipped (2026-06-04; Wave 221).** Forge observability for the self-modification pipeline — turned the designer black box (a bare `status` string + scattered logs) into measurable throughput, continuing the Memory Epistemics wave ([#844](https://github.com/seangalliher/ProbOS/issues/844)). New pure, fully-typed, side-effect-free module `src/probos/cognitive/forge_observability.py` with three units: (1) `validate_forge_shape(intent_name, intent_description, parameters) -> list[str]` — a cheap pre-design gate that returns error strings (empty = proceed, same contract shape as `CodeValidator.validate`) for an empty/whitespace/degenerate/non-alphanumeric intent name, empty description, `None` parameters, or an empty parameter key (an empty `{}` param map is a **valid** no-parameter intent); (2) `classify_forge_rejection(record, validator_errors=None) -> str` — deterministic bucketing (`syntax_error | forbidden_import | schema_nonconformance | judge_correctness | dependency_declined | dependency_failed | user_rejected | max_limit | design_failed | failed_sandbox | failed_registration | shape_rejected | other`) that maps **every** `DesignedAgentRecord.status` emitted at HEAD plus representative `CodeValidator` error substrings, duck-typed on `.status`/`.error` with an explicit `else → "other"` so a future/unseen status (e.g. `failed_pool`) never crashes the aggregator; (3) `ForgeStatsAggregator(records)` — pure aggregation reporting both `attempt_approval_rate` **and** `unique_intent_approval_rate` (the key insight: a 3-retry recovery on one intent ≠ 3 distinct successes), plus `rejection_histogram`, `total_unique_intents`, `total_attempts`, and a flat `summary()` dict (`removed` counts as approval; the records list is materialized into a local copy so post-construction source mutation can't drift the stats). **Read-only wiring** into `SelfModificationPipeline` (`src/probos/cognitive/self_mod.py`): `forge_stats() -> ForgeStatsAggregator` over `self._records`; and a Tier-2 honest-degrade shape gate at the very top of `handle_unhandled_intent` — on a non-empty error list it records a `DesignedAgentRecord(status="shape_rejected", error=<joined>)` and returns `None`, skipping the expensive design call; a shape-gate exception logs a warning and falls through to normal design (never raises, never blocks a legitimate request). The optional `EventType.FORGE_SHAPE_REJECTED` was **not** added — self_mod's `EventLog` uses string event names, not the `EventType` enum, so it did not slot cleanly (prompt-permitted skip). No change to design/validate/sandbox logic, no new LLM calls, no persistence (in-memory over `_records` only). +28 pytest (`tests/test_ad872_forge_observability.py`, real `DesignedAgentRecord` fixtures + a real pipeline for the short-circuit/honest-degrade/forge_stats tests per BF-287 — no MagicMock at the substrate boundary); `tests/test_self_mod.py` + `tests/test_self_mod_deps.py` stay green; full gate 15411 passed. Closes [#844](https://github.com/seangalliher/ProbOS/issues/844). Second unit of the Memory Epistemics wave; AD-873 (decay + rerank) deferred to a later commit. One commit, no push.

**AD-871 shipped (2026-06-04; Wave 220).** Provenance-aware memory envelope — the first unit of the Memory Epistemics wave ([#843](https://github.com/seangalliher/ProbOS/issues/843); AD-872/873 deferred). Every `Episode` (`src/probos/types.py`) now carries four new last-position fields: `source_type: str = ""` (how the memory was acquired), `confidence: float = 1.0` (graded belief), `verification_count: int = 0`, and `contradicted_by: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)` (ids of episodes that contradict this one) — so a memory knows *how it was learned* and *how trustworthy it is*, not just *what happened*. New `types.py` provenance kernel: `SOURCE_TYPE_CONFIDENCE` (per-source default belief: `user_statement`/`tool_result`=1.0, `observation`/`external`=0.8, `agent_inference`/`reflection`=0.5), `SOURCE_TO_SOURCE_TYPE` (back-fills `source_type` from the legacy `MemorySource` string — `direct`→`observation`, `secondhand`→`agent_inference`, `ship_records`/`briefing`→`external`, `reflection`→`reflection`), `resolve_provenance(source, source_type, confidence) -> tuple[str, float]` (back-fills an empty `source_type` from `source`, derives confidence from the map **only** when `source_type` is set AND confidence is still the 1.0 default — a caller's explicit graded belief is always preserved), and `mark_contradicted(episode, contradicting_id) -> Episode` (idempotent, empty-id no-op, frozen-safe `replace`). **Three episodic seams** (`src/probos/cognitive/episodic.py`): `store()`'s AD-598 importance-rescore now reconstructs the frozen Episode via `dataclasses.replace(episode, importance=...)` instead of a field-by-field copy, so the new provenance fields survive re-scoring (caught: a failure outcome at default importance=5 rescores to 7 and the old copy silently dropped `confidence`); `_episode_to_metadata` stamps the envelope onto the ChromaDB row (`resolve_provenance` at the write boundary, `contradicted_by` JSON-encoded to `contradicted_by_json` per the str/int/float/bool-only metadata rule); `_metadata_to_episode` decodes it back with full Tier-2 honest-degrade (malformed `confidence`→1.0, malformed `verification_count`→0, malformed/ non-list `contradicted_by_json`→`[]`) so **pre-AD-871 rows recall cleanly with neutral defaults**. `compute_episode_hash` allowlist and `_hash_v` untouched (provenance is not identity). +14 pytest (`tests/test_ad871_provenance_envelope.py`, real `EpisodicMemory`/`tmp_path` fixtures per BF-287 — no MagicMock at the substrate boundary; includes the AD-598 reconstruction regression); `tests/test_episodic.py` (16) green; full gate 15382 passed (the lone `test_trust_dampening` failure is pre-existing xdist state-bleed, green serially). Closes [#843](https://github.com/seangalliher/ProbOS/issues/843). One commit, no push.

**AD-847 shipped (2026-06-04; Wave 219).** Closed the lightweight-delegation wave (AD-869→870→846→847) with the desktop surface: a native OS notification when a Yeo-delegated task finishes, so a Captain with the window minimized still gets pulled back to the result. New `window.probos.notifyTaskDone({title, body, route})` context-bridge method (`desktop/src/preload/index.ts`) → `ipcMain.handle("probos:notifyTaskDone", ...)` (`desktop/src/main/index.ts`) → existing AD-759 `notify(args, {showAndRoute})` primitive. The untrusted renderer payload is validated by a new pure helper `coerceTaskDonePayload(payload): NotifyArgs | null` (`desktop/src/main/notifications.ts`) that requires a non-empty `title` + string `body` and drops a blank `route` to `undefined`; a present `route` (e.g. Yeo's 1:1 chat deep-link) makes the click activate the window and route there. Reuses the `probos:*` channel naming and the AD-759 wrapper — no new WebSocket/window plumbing. +9 vitest (`desktop/src/main/notifications.test.ts`, electron mocked via `vi.mock`); full desktop gate 65 green; `electron-vite build` clean. Closes [#842](https://github.com/seangalliher/ProbOS/issues/842). One commit.

**AD-846 shipped (2026-06-04; Wave 218).** Closed the async half of Yeo's Tier-3 delegation loop: when a task Yeo opened on the Captain's behalf finishes, Yeo proactively DMs the Captain with the outcome instead of leaving the result to be discovered on the kanban board. New `src/probos/task_completion_notifier.py` (`notify_captain_of_task_completion(runtime, event)`) listens for `WORK_ITEM_STATUS_CHANGED` and fires only when the new status is terminal (`done`/`failed`) AND the item is a Yeo-originated dispatchable task (`metadata.dispatchable` truthy AND `tags` contains `"yeo-delegated"`) — the tag gate is load-bearing so system/crew work items never spam the Captain's DM. It resolves the live Yeo from the `yeoman` registry pool and posts via the AD-485 Captain-DM primitive (find-or-create `dm-captain-{yeo_id[:8]}` channel → `create_thread`), with a one-line "Task complete: <title>." (done) or "Task did not finish: <title>… let me know if you'd like me to open it again." (failed). Wired in `startup/finalize.py` (`_wire_task_completion_notifier`, mirrors the AD-857 capability-request notifier) via `runtime.add_event_listener(..., event_types=["work_item_status_changed"])`. Every failure mode is Tier-2 honest-degrade (no ward room, no live Yeo, registry raises, non-matching event → log if relevant, never raise); the kanban board remains the result surface regardless. Desktop notification (AD-847) is the gated follow-up. +9 pytest (`tests/test_ad846_completion_dm.py`, real fake ward room + real registry pool fixtures per BF-287); AD-857 notifier suite (4) stays green. Closes [#841](https://github.com/seangalliher/ProbOS/issues/841). One commit.

**AD-870 shipped (2026-06-04; Wave 217).** Taught Yeo *when* to reach for each delegation tier, completing the lightweight-delegation pair with AD-869. Extended `YeomanAgent._conversational_task_protocol` from the AD-845 `[CREATE_TASK]`-only guidance to the full four-tier threshold: Tier 1 Answer (reply, no tag) / Tier 2 Do-and-report (quick read-only lookup → AD-869 `[MESH <intent> key=value ...]`) / Tier 3 Write-it-down (substantial/stateful work → `[CREATE_TASK ...]`) / Tier 4 Get-help (`specialist=@Callsign`). New helper `_available_mesh_read_intents()` reads the live registry and teaches `[MESH]` forms only for read pools that currently have an agent (`list_directory path=<dir>`, `read_file path=<file>`, `search_files path=<dir> pattern=<glob>`, `web_search query=<terms>`, `read_page url=<url>`). The closing rule of thumb encodes the two-axis threshold ("if you can get the answer in the time it takes to reply and it changes nothing, just do it; otherwise write it down"). Grounded in graduated-autonomy doctrine (command-by-negation / Mission Command) + a decision-cost model (Vroom–Yetton). Instructions-first (Principle #6) — taught in the conversational prompt, not `decide()`/`act()`. Honest-degrade tightened: `[MESH]` gated per-pool on a live agent, `[CREATE_TASK]` on a wired store; a registry that raises degrades to the `[CREATE_TASK]`-only block, never crashes. Gap-regex-clean (asserted). +8 pytest (`tests/test_ad870_yeo_delegation_threshold.py`, real `AgentRegistry` per BF-287); `tests/test_ad845_yeo_chat_task_creation.py` + `tests/test_yeoman_agent.py` (34) green. Closes [#840](https://github.com/seangalliher/ProbOS/issues/840). One commit.

**AD-869 shipped (2026-06-04; Wave 216).** Gave Yeo (YeomanAgent, AD-766, the always-on conversational front-door) its lightweight delegation *default* — the missing Tier-2 "do-and-report" of the four-tier delegation model (Tier 1 Answer / **Tier 2 Do-and-report** / Tier 3 Write-it-down = AD-845 `[CREATE_TASK]` / Tier 4 Get-help = AD-845 `specialist=@`). When the Captain asks for a quick read-only lookup Yeo can answer *this turn*, Yeo now emits a `[MESH <intent> key=value key=value]` reply tag. Two new `DmSanityGate` methods parse it (`extract_mesh_read` / `strip_mesh_read` with `_MESH_READ_RE` / `_MESH_READ_STRIP_RE` / `_MESH_PARAM_KEY_RE` — the key regex anchors on a token boundary so a `=` inside a URL query string is preserved and multi-word values like `query=Nvidia SPARK RTX` parse intact). New `DmReplyPipeline.step_4h_mesh_read_parse` (registered before `step_4f_extract_artifacts`) maps the intent to its registry pool via the module-level allowlist `_MESH_READ_INTENT_POOLS` (`list_directory`→`directory`, `read_file`/`stat_file`→`filesystem`, `search_files`→`search`, `web_search`→`web_search`, `read_page`→`page_reader`), resolves the first live agent via `runtime.registry.get_by_pool` (BF-599 pattern), runs ONE targeted `IntentBus.send(IntentMessage(..., ttl_seconds=5))` (single dispatch, not a broadcast), renders the result inline (truncated to 1500 chars), and strips the tag. A non-allowlisted intent (`http_fetch`, writes) is stripped + shipped, never executed. **The allowlist is a structural safety boundary, not policy:** a single-turn ~5s synchronous read is *incapable* of mutation because destructive ops require consensus and consensus cannot resolve in one turn — the latency ceiling enforces the safety ceiling. Web/file access stays mesh-delegated (Design Principle #10; no `httpx`/`requests` added, asserted by source scan). Every failure mode (missing gate/bus/agent, send failure, timeout, unsuccessful result) is Tier-2 honest-degrade with `_CAPABILITY_GAP_RE`-clean note wording, never raises. +16 pytest (`tests/test_ad869_mesh_read_inline.py`, real `IntentBus`+`SignalManager`+`AgentRegistry`+subscribed handler+real `DmSanityGate` per BF-287); DM pipeline + sanity-gate regression suites (53) green. Closes [#839](https://github.com/seangalliher/ProbOS/issues/839). Design grounded in graduated-autonomy doctrine (command-by-negation / Mission Command; workflows-vs-agents; Vroom–Yetton decision model). One commit. AD-870 (Yeo's *when-to* threshold instructions) ships next; AD-846/847 (completion DM + desktop notification) are gated follow-ups.

**AD-868 shipped (2026-06-04; Wave 215).** Gave Lieutenant+ crew agents the ability to *self-originate* a structured crew task, closing the chain-of-command collaboration epic (AD-863 chain; closes [#838](https://github.com/seangalliher/ProbOS/issues/838)). Two surgical additions: (1) `CrewOrchestrator.originate_crew_task(*, origin_agent_id, goal, work_type="task") -> str | None` in `src/probos/cognitive/crew_orchestrator.py` — decomposes the goal **before** creating any WorkItem (so a decompose failure / empty specs / disabled flag leaves no dangling parent), then creates a parent with `created_by=origin_agent_id` + `metadata={origin: "self_originated", originator: <id>}`, persists the AD-863 `capability`/`department` hints onto each child's metadata, and drives the AD-867 `run_crew_task` pipeline; honest-degrades to `None` (never raises) when `orchestrator_enabled` is off, the goal is blank, no decomposer is available, or decomposition yields zero specs; AD-861's synthesizer owns trust attribution, so `originate_crew_task` makes **no second trust write**. (2) A new `[CREW] … [/CREW]` proactive tag in `src/probos/proactive.py` parsed right after `[ASSIGN]`, gated Lieutenant+ by **reusing the exact AD-654d `_RANK_ORDER_ASSIGN` comparison** (ensign silently ignored, tag stripped regardless); when wired it calls `crew_orchestrator.originate_crew_task` and records a `{type: "crew", parent_id, goal}` action; missing/raising orchestrator → Tier-2 log-and-continue. +16 pytest (`tests/test_ad868_self_originated_crew.py`) against a **real** `WorkItemStore` fixture (BF-287 — only the LLM decomposer / collaborators are `_Fake*` stubs) covering originate happy-path + provenance + pipeline drive, all honest-degrade branches, `_get_decomposer` lazy-build, and the proactive `[CREW]` rank gate (Lt/Cmdr/Senior allowed, ensign blocked, missing-orchestrator skip). 116 proactive regression tests stay green. One commit, no push.

**AD-867 shipped (2026-06-03; Wave 214).** Threaded the dormant crew pipeline into one end-to-end runtime entry point, completing the chain-of-command collaboration epic (AD-863 chain; closes [#837](https://github.com/seangalliher/ProbOS/issues/837)). New Cognitive-layer module `src/probos/cognitive/crew_orchestrator.py`: `CrewOrchestrator` composes the AD-864 assignment resolver, AD-865 delegator, AD-859 `CrewTaskExecutor`, AD-860 `SubtaskVerifier`, and AD-861 `CrewSynthesizer` into one flow — **resolve → delegate → fan-out → verify → synthesize** — behind `runtime.crew_orchestrator.run_crew_task(parent_id)`. `__init__(*, assignment_resolver, delegator, crew_executor, verifier, synthesizer, work_item_store, runtime, emit_fn=None, config=None)` (keyword-only DI). **Flow:** Stage 0 `_promote_parent` transitions the parent `open → in_progress` (honest-degrade, only when `status == "open"`) — required because `CrewSynthesizer._complete_parent` does `in_progress → done` and the AD-498 state machine forbids `open → done` directly (the AD-867 "parent state glue" deviation); Stage 1 per-child `_assign_child` builds a `WorkItemSpec` view, calls `assignment_resolver.resolve` then `delegator.delegate`, and `update_work_item(child.id, assigned_to=<worker>, metadata={chief_agent_id, order_id, delegated, delegation_reason, assigned_capability, assigned_department})` (unresolved children left unassigned, never abort siblings); Stage 2 `results = await crew_executor.run(parent_id)`; Stage 3 `_verify` skips non-`done` results, calls `await verifier.verify(result)` per child and wraps each in a `ConvergenceOutcome(status="converged" if verdict.accepted else "unverified")`; Stage 4 `return await synthesizer.synthesize(parent_id, outcomes)`. **Every stage honest-degrades and `run_crew_task` never raises** — executor failure → `[]`, a single verifier failure degrades that one child, synthesizer failure → `SynthesisResult(parent_id, final_output="", completed=False, total_count=len(outcomes))`. Emits `CREW_ORCHESTRATION_STARTED` (new `EventType`) with `{parent_id, child_count}`. **Trigger:** `maybe_dispatch_crew(parent_id)` returns `None` when the flag is off or the parent has `<= 1` child, else `asyncio.create_task(run_crew_task(...))` held in `self._tasks: set` + `add_done_callback(self._tasks.discard)` (no fire-and-forget). **Gate:** new `orchestrator_enabled: bool = False` on `AgenticDispatchConfig` (NOT a new `CrewConfig`) — ships OFF. **Wiring:** `_wire_crew_orchestrator(*, runtime, config)` in `startup/finalize.py` (invoked right after `_wire_consultation_dispatch`) returns `False` when the flag is off or any of `work_item_store/registry/capability_registry/ontology/trust_network/llm_client` is missing (Tier-2 INFO `"AD-867: crew_orchestrator skipped; missing dependencies: %s"`), else constructs the six collaborators (passing `ontology` into the AD-866 verifier ctor) and attaches `runtime.crew_orchestrator`; `attachment_store` resolved via `_get_attachment_store` (honest-degrade to `None`). +18 pytest (`tests/test_ad867_crew_orchestrator.py`) against a **real** `WorkItemStore` fixture (BF-287 — no MagicMock at the storage boundary; only resolver/delegator/executor/verifier/synthesizer are `_Fake*` stubs at the collaborator boundary) covering end-to-end completion, unresolved-child/verifier-refute/failed-subtask degrade paths, provenance writes, parent promotion, synthesizer/executor/missing-parent honest-degrade, flag-off no-op, single-child skip, held-task-reference, event emission, and the three wirer paths (attach/flag-off/missing-dep). No changes to `CrewTaskExecutor`/`SubtaskVerifier`/`CrewSynthesizer` internals — orchestrated as-is. One commit, no push.

**AD-866 shipped (2026-06-03; Wave 213).** Made the org chart load-bearing for AD-860 adversarial verifier *selection*, continuing the chain-of-command collaboration epic (closes [#836](https://github.com/seangalliher/ProbOS/issues/836)). Surgical change to `src/probos/cognitive/crew_verifier.py` only: `SubtaskVerifier.__init__` gains an optional keyword-only `ontology=None` (Dependency Inversion), and `_pick_independent_verifier(producer_id)` gains an org-chart-aware selection order **when an ontology is wired**: (1) **department peer** — first alive agent `!= producer` whose `ontology.get_agent_department(agent_type)` equals the producer's department (the most qualified independent judge); (2) **authority chain** — the producer's chief, found by walking `ontology.get_chain_of_command(producer_post.id)` and resolving the first superior post's live wired agent via `ontology.get_agents_for_post`; (3) **any independent** — the AD-860 first-registered-`!= producer` loop **verbatim**; (4) **None** → honest-degrade to `unverified`. Every candidate is gated on `agent_registry.get(id) is not None` so dead/unregistered billets are excluded. **`ontology=None` (the default for every existing call site) skips steps 1-2 entirely and runs the AD-860 path byte-for-byte** — the 11 `tests/test_ad860_crew_verifier.py` stay green, unchanged. Steps 1-2 are wrapped Tier-2 log-and-degrade (`exc_info=True`): any ontology lookup error falls through to step 3, never propagates into `verify`. New helpers `_agent_type_for`, `_pick_department_peer`, `_pick_authority_chief` (fully typed). +7 pytest (`tests/test_ad866_dept_verifier.py`) against **real** `AgentRegistry`/`VesselOntologyService`/`TrustNetwork` fixtures (concrete `BaseAgent` subclass; `config/ontology` copied per-test) — no MagicMock at the substrate boundary per BF-287; only the LLM judge / store / AD-859a executor are `_Fake*` stubs. `verify`/`converge`/trust-recording/verdict logic untouched. One commit, no push.

**AD-865 shipped (2026-06-03; Wave 212).** Made the org chart load-bearing for crew sub-task assignment, continuing the chain-of-command collaboration epic (AD-863 chain; closes [#835](https://github.com/seangalliher/ProbOS/issues/835)). New Cognitive-layer module `src/probos/cognitive/crew_delegation.py` turns an AD-864 `AssignmentDecision` into a **governed delegation** — a **pure decision**, no WorkItem mutation, no dispatch (AD-867 reads it).
